# TechieDesk — Checklist

> Split from `docs/TechieDesk-BRD.md` (BRD-1…BRD-127) on 2026-07-17. Pre-existing statuses seeded from `docs/TechieRag-Checklist.md` (all 37 REQs terminal as of 2026-07-17) — carried as `Done (pre-existing)`; build agents must NOT rebuild these. The TechieRag checklist stays in place as the frozen historical record of the shipped v1.1/v2 scope (BRD §1 decision 3) — it is NOT a live tracker; THIS checklist is the single live tracker for app work, library gap closure (F-LIB), and all feedback (existing + future).

## Table of Contents

1. [Goal](#goal)
2. [Requirements Status](#requirements-status)
3. [UI / Pages](#ui--pages)
4. [Functional requirements](#functional-requirements)
5. [RAG / AI requirements (→ /techierag)](#rag-ai-requirements-techierag)
6. [Non-functional](#non-functional)
7. [Feedback tracker](#feedback-tracker)

## Goal

Turn TechieDesk from a verified single-user operator console into a productized **desktop** AnythingLLM alternative (BRD §1): a MAUI Blazor Hybrid application for macOS and Windows with workspaces + threads and persistent history, a drag-drop document library with native streaming citations, connectors, agents, branding/i18n, AppManager-backed licensing/billing/support, and signed desktop packaging — with all reusable capability built inside the TechieRag library (F-LIB items, tracked here), data access via Dapper + TechieDeskDb/DbUp over SQLite (no EF Core), and Serilog logging throughout.

> **Amended 2026-07-26 — desktop-only pivot** (`*amend-docs TechieDesk`, owner decision). The Blazor Server head and the Docker distribution of TechieDesk are retired, and with them the developer REST API, the embeddable widget, multi-user roles, and the multi-tenant admin surfaces. 16 rows moved to `N/A (removed 2026-07-26)`, 8 flagged for re-scope/re-verify, and 10 rows added (REQ-FN-035…041, REQ-UI-041/042, REQ-NFR-011). Retired rows are kept for traceability and are never deleted. **F-QADMIN is explicitly retained** and extended — the Docker daemon it drives becomes configurable (local/LAN/remote). See BRD §1 "Form factor" and BRD-128…134.

| REQ-UI-007 | Login screen & flow (BRD-13, P1) | PARTIAL | 70% | 2026-07-25 BLOCKER CLEARED — login now yields a usable session (this time the SESSION, not just the network call). /login posts to the new /auth/login endpoint → POST /AuthSvc/login over TLS → valid creds land on the last-requested route authenticated, and **F5 does not sign out** (the clause the old code failed). INVALID_CREDENTIALS redirects back with a friendly banner and issues NO session cookie. Live-proven against AppManager https://192.168.1.14:5101 as admin@appmanager.local (5/5 Playwright, tests/verify/req-fn-032-login-session.spec.ts); render+visual @1280/390 unchanged vs mockup. Also fixed here: `AppManagerClient.LoginAsync` omitted the v1.4 `aApplicationId` param, so AppManager returned an EMPTY applicationRole and every user degraded to `User` — now sent per the ValidateLicenseAsync convention (+1 wire-contract test). ⚠ activeLicense capture unit-tested only (null on that AppManager account). Pending verifier · ⚠ **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-26 (desktop-only).** Login is no longer an access gate — it activates a licence and is reached from settings, never forced at launch (BRD-129). The screen survives; the flow around it does not. · **2026-07-26 [REQ-FN-039] — the flow is rebuilt, in-process.** `/login` and `/register` no longer submit HTML forms to the dead `/auth/login` / `/auth/register` endpoints (they had rendered but done nothing since REQ-FN-035); both now call the new `IDesktopSignInService` directly and navigate in-circuit on success. Field validation, `PasswordPolicy`, handle rotation before the new session is minted, and the banner error-code vocabulary all carry over verbatim; antiforgery, the `?error=` redirect round-trip and `NavigateTo(forceLoad: true)` are gone with the HTTP boundary they belonged to. Tokens land in the OS credential store. `/login` was rendered live and read from a screenshot — form, banner, both fields and an enabled Sign in button. ⚠ Still NOT driven end-to-end: no AppManager is reachable on this host and the button cannot be clicked (no assistive access, no Appium), so a successful sign-in remains test-only. · ✅ **runtime render+visual-confirmed 2026-07-28 (`*verify all`, live Catalyst head)** — `/login` renders complete under `AuthLayout` (no sidebar, as designed): brand lockup, “Welcome back”, “Sign in to your TechieDesk instance”, Email and Password fields, an enabled **Sign in** button, “Forgot password?”, “No account? Create one”, the **“Continue without an account →”** escape that proves BRD-129 (licence is not an access gate), and the RSA-encryption footnote. The account-free banner renders too — ⚠ with **TR-032**: its icon shows as the literal text `Icon not found: alert-circle`. 0 visual defects. **Sign-in itself still NOT driven end-to-end** — no AppManager reachable on this host — so this stays `Needs re-verify`, but the screen is no longer “cannot be clicked”: the driver now clicks it (the `Continue without an account` link was exercised repeatedly this run). Screenshot `.verify/shots/sign-in.png`. · 2026-07-29 [REQ-UI-007] **ACCEPTANCE RE-SCOPED to the Catalyst head (owner-authorised); the 2026-07-25 evidence certified an architecture that no longer exists** — it rested on `/login` posting to `POST /auth/login` over TLS, a session cookie and F5 survival, all of which REQ-FN-035 deleted with the web host. Replacement acceptance is 9 clauses against the in-process flow (see the UI/Pages section). **How it truly works:** `Login.razor` → `IDesktopSignInService.SignInAsync` on the same thread → `AppManagerClient.LoginAsync` (RSA-OAEP-SHA256, TLS enforced) → handle rotated → `SessionStore.CreateSession` → tokens in `SessionTokenStore` + mirrored through `ISecretStore` → `AttachHandle` → `NotifySessionChanged()` + in-app `NavigateTo`. No HTML form post, no antiforgery, no `forceLoad`. **LIVE (Appium/mac2, universal Release bundle):** the sidebar `Sign in` link was CLICKED for the first time — earlier runs' `element/click` silently no-opped; **W3C pointer actions work** — and `/login` rendered complete (Welcome back, both fields, enabled Sign in, Forgot password?, Create one, `Continue without an account →`, RSA footnote, account-free banner), 0 visual defects; the submit was then driven and correctly refused locally with no session and no crash. ⚠ **Clauses 4 & 6 (valid creds land authenticated; INVALID_CREDENTIALS banner) remain UNEXERCISED** — AppManager `https://192.168.1.14:5101` is unreachable from this host and the bundle ships no `AppManager:BaseUrl` (boot log: 'Offline single-user'), so the documented `admin@appmanager.local` could not be used and **none was invented**. ⚠ Appium keystroke delivery into the WKWebView is lossy ('admin@appmanager.local' → 'admin@appmana'), so the submit path was exercised, not credential correctness. ⚠ **TR-032 confirmed and upgraded to an a11y defect:** the banner icon's accessible label is literally `Icon not found: alert-circle` (live AX tree, x=734 y=629) — a screenshot passes it, a screen reader does not. +8 Auth tests incl. a 7-case `SafeReturnUrl` open-redirect theory. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-UI-007] **AUDIT — NO CODE GAP; no change made and none warranted.** Read end-to-end: `DesktopSignInService.cs:49-172` (validate → `client.LoginAsync` → `EstablishSession` with handle-rotation as session-fixation defence → tokens to the OS credential store) and `Login.razor:118-145` (`NotifySessionChanged` → `NavigateTo(SafeReturnUrl)`); `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` → "Invalid email or password." at `Login.razor:166`. Swept the whole sign-in surface for `NotImplementedException`/TODO/empty bodies: **none**. ⚠ **The recorded blocker is UNDERSTATED — there are THREE preconditions, not one.** `https://192.168.1.14:5101` was probed today: connection REFUSED in 6 ms. More importantly **there is no `appsettings.json` or `appsettings.Development.json` anywhere in the repo** — the only AppManager config is `appsettings.Development.json.appmanager-uat`, whose filename suffix means `MauiProgram.cs:317-320` never loads it, so `IsConfigured==false` and `AuthenticateAsync` returns `NoLicenceServer` **before any network call**. That is correct by design (`.gitignore:32-35` excludes the real file as local secrets; the `.appmanager-uat` file is the committed template). Reaching `Verified` therefore needs: a reachable AppManager, a locally-created `apps/TechieDesk/appsettings.Development.json` from that template, AND `TECHIEDESK_ENVIRONMENT=Development`. **Owner decision — no secrets file was created on the owner's behalf.** Row correctly stays PARTIAL. | [view](#d-req-ui-007) |

Control map: `docs/devguides/TechieDesk-DevGuide-Workspace.md`. ⚠ Known, unchanged and NOT part of this REQ's acceptance: the **Size column renders "—"** for every row (TR-RAG-004, a library gap). Was `Implemented 75%`. · 2026-07-29 verifier: ⚠ DEMOTED from Verified — render gate FAILED on one column. The library table's `Size` column renders an em-dash on EVERY row (3/3) at /workspace/default/documents. Root cause read from source: SizeFromMetadata (DocumentLibrary.razor:735) probes Document.Metadata for FileSize/Size/fileSize/size/ByteSize and NO ingestion path writes any of them, so the em-dash fallback is what every document will ever show — this is not a data gap, it is permanent. REQ-UI-021 names *size* as a required column. Corroborated on /settings/data where the `uploads` artefact reads 'not created yet' (original source documents are not retained). Every OTHER column renders correctly with real data (name/type/chunks/uploaded/workspaces/status), the count badge matches the visible rows, and the table scrolls inside its card at 1024 with no overflow. Note /ingestion's own table DOES show real byte sizes — the two screens read different stores, so the fix likely means persisting size at ingest. Screenshot test-results/ui-verify/document-library-d1600.png. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-UI-021] ✅ **Size column FIXED at the writer — and the defect was THREE layers deep, not one.** The 2026-07-29 demotion and its diagnosis were exact: the razor reader was never wrong. **Layer 1 (the writer):** `TechieRagClient.IngestAsync` now records `DocumentMetadataKeys.FileSize` = the stream length read **before** the processor consumes it (the only point where the artefact's own size still exists — chunk lengths are inflated by overlap); `IngestTextAsync` records the UTF-8 byte count; connector ingestion records `ConnectorItem.SizeBytes`. 🔴 **Layer 2 (independent, and invisible from the razor side):** `SqliteVecStore` — the DESKTOP DEFAULT — hardcoded every document row's `Metadata` column to the literal `{}`, so nothing any route recorded could have survived the round trip. It now lifts document-scoped keys (new `DocumentMetadataKeys`, an ALLOWLIST so a PDF page number is never published as a document-level fact); `QdrantStore` does the same. 🔴 **Layer 3 (a trap behind both):** metadata deserialized into `Dictionary<string,object>` yields `JsonElement`, which is not `IConvertible` — the size would have been stored correctly and STILL rendered the em-dash. `FromJson` unwraps to CLR primitives. Caught by the live smoke, not by an assertion; there is an assertion now. PROVEN ON THE REAL PRODUCTION DATABASE with the real embedded BGE-M3 provider through `WorkspaceManager.IngestFileAsync`: a 7,080-byte file stored `{"FileSize":7080}` and renders `6.9 KB`; a 90-byte file renders `90 B`. +14 tests. The probe moved to `DocumentSizeDisplay.cs` (behaviour byte-identical) purely so it is testable from a net10.0 project. ⚠ **NOT a residual defect: documents ingested before this change still render `—`, and that is CORRECT** — their source artefacts were never retained and a backfilled number would be fabricated. ⚠ **The `TR-RAG-004` citation on this row is a MISLABEL** — TR-RAG-004 is an unrelated Qdrant scroll-pagination note. The real entry is **TR-RAG-038** (new, fixed); **TR-RAG-024** (was OPEN major, same root cause from the other direction) is now CLOSED. ⚠ Two smoke documents were left in the owner's real `Default` workspace as UI-check evidence (`req-ui-021-size-smoke.md`, `req-ui-021-size-smoke-small.txt`) — remove via per-row Delete. Needs a fresh render observation at `/workspace/default/documents`. · 2026-07-30 [*verify all] ✅ **runtime render+visual-confirmed** on the live Catalyst head via the project harness `tests/appium/` — screen swept at **1600x1240 AND the enforced 1024x720 floor**, arrival gated on the app's own breadcrumb (21/21 screens arrived, 0 mis-navigated), content tree **settled** before reading, screenshot window-cropped and inspected. §4a: controls render their data. §4b: no zero-size, no true overlap, no off-canvas content. The `Size` column defect that caused the 2026-07-29 demotion is **CLOSED and observed**: `/workspace/default/documents` renders 'Library (5 documents)' over 5 rows with every column populated — and the Size split is correct, `6.9 KB` + `90 B` on the two rows ingested after the fix, `—` on the three legacy rows whose source artefacts were never retained (a backfilled number would be fabricated, so `—` is the right answer, not a residual defect). 153 content nodes at both widths, 0 overlaps, 0 icon errors. | [view](#d-req-ui-021) |

So the route is a **superseded duplicate that should be deleted**. ⚠ **The Branding tab itself could not be activated at runtime:** its `TabsTrigger` is not exposed as an actionable element (tried Button/Link/RadioButton/Other via the accessibility tree), so **this panel has never been observed rendering anywhere** — the only two routes that host it are one orphan and one tab that cannot be opened by assistive tech. That is TR-008 (TrBlazeUI Tabs expose no usable trigger) **runtime-confirmed**, and it also makes the tab keyboard/AT-inoperable. Demoted from `Implemented 70%` — not because the code is wrong, but because no user has a proven way to reach it. · 🗑 **`/settings/appearance` DELETED 2026-07-28 (owner decision).** The orphaned duplicate route is gone; `AppearanceSettings.razor` was 29 lines that did nothing but stack `<AppearancePanel />` and `<BrandingPanel />` in a column, and its own header said it existed only until the App-settings screen landed. That screen has landed and mounts both panels at `AdminSettings.razor:175-176`. ⚠ **Note the Core service `TechieDesk.Core/Services/Appearance/AppearanceSettings.cs` is a DIFFERENT type and was NOT touched** — same name, unrelated. This row stays `Needs re-verify` for the unchanged reason: the Branding tab that now solely hosts the panel could not be activated through the accessibility tree (TR-008). · 2026-07-29 [REQ-UI-037] **TR-008's blocking claim is DISPROVED — the Branding tab was never unreachable.** WebKit maps `role="tab"` to AXRadioButton/AXTabButton, surfaced by XCUITest as `XCUIElementTypeTab`; the 2026-07-28 DevGuide probe queried only Button/Link/RadioButton/Other and wrongly concluded the trigger was inert. Re-probed live on the shipping Release Catalyst bundle: `**/XCUIElementTypeTab[`label == "Branding"`]` returns one enabled element at (354,327,81,32); clicking it activates the tab and **BrandingPanel rendered for the first time in this project's history** — on this Free install as the correct `WHITE_LABEL` FeatureGate upgrade prompt, with AppearancePanel above it, 0 overlap / 0 zero-size / 0 off-canvas at 1785px. Evidence `test-results/cluster-a-39-branding-english.png`, `cluster-a-11-branding-tab.png`. Decompiling TrBlazeUI.Primitives 1.0.7 confirms the DOM was correct all along. Hardened beyond the fix: every tab is now deep-linkable as `?tab=<defaults\|branding\|updates>` driving uncontrolled `DefaultValue` behind an `@key` (controlled `Value` is dead per TR-021), so the panels no longer depend on one gesture. +2 tests; 70/70 green. ⚠ NOT observed: the licensed white-label FORM (logo/display name/welcome/footer) — no Professional licence on this machine, so those four fields remain unseen behind a correctly-behaving gate. · 🔴 **2026-08-01 — THE TR-008 GROUNDS FOR THIS ROW'S 2026-07-28 DEMOTION WERE A HARNESS ARTEFACT, not a product defect.** The Branding tab was recorded as "cannot be opened by assistive tech / never observed rendering" because the probe queried Button/Link/RadioButton/Other — but WebKit maps `role="tab"` to **`XCUIElementTypeTab`**, a type it never asked for. `**/XCUIElementTypeTab[`label == "Branding"`]` returns exactly one enabled element; clicking it activates the tab and both panels render. This row was subsequently observed rendering and operating live on 2026-07-29 anyway, so its status already reflects reality — this note exists so the *reason* on record is right, and so nobody re-demotes it on the same false grounds. See `docs/TechieRag-TrBlazeUI-Feedback.md` §"TR-008 — AMENDED". | [view](#d-req-ui-037) |

Biggest gaps: ConnectorEdit ~174, QdrantAdmin ~154, WorkspaceAgents ~150, ConnectorsHub ~115, Automations ~111, WorkspaceChat ~98, MainLayout ~67. **DEMOTED from `Needs re-verify` to `PARTIAL` — the acceptance is NOT met at 2.3%.** 🔴 **Two real defects found and fixed while smoking: (1) `LanguageCoordinator.SetAsync` used `NavigateTo(forceLoad: true)`, which BRICKS a MAUI BlazorWebView** — the reload destroys the JS context the Blazor host is attached to, it never re-attaches, and the window is left on the raw `Loading...` host page with `#blazor-error-ui` showing, whole app dead until you quit (`test-results/cluster-a-16-after-german-reload.png`; unified log shows `FrameLoader::loadWithNavigationAction` then two `runJavaScriptInFrameInScriptWorld` failures). **The language picker destroyed the app the first time anyone used it.** (2) In-place re-render does not work either — a `Changed` event and a full route round trip both still rendered German after choosing English, because a Blazor render resolves `CurrentUICulture` under the `ExecutionContext` captured when the renderer started. Shipped behaviour is now the honest one: store + apply, with a translated info Alert 'Your choice has been saved. TechieDesk shows the new language the next time it starts.' +2 tests (`ResolvesEveryKeyTheShippedScreensAskFor` RED-proven by renaming one de key); 70/70 green. · 2026-07-29 verifier: the LanguagePicker renders and opens with exactly three locales — English, Deutsch, Francais — meeting the 'en + 2' clause, and the choice persists to InstanceSetting.AppearanceLanguage. But the switch is NOT live: selecting Deutsch changed NO on-screen string and the app itself reports 'TechieDesk shows the new language the next time it starts.' Actual de/fr .resx coverage was therefore NOT exercised (an app restart would have ended the Appium binding), and app-wide coverage stands at the 2.3% measured this phase. Reverted to English (confirmed in the DB). Live-switch UX is REQ-UI-050. ⚠ a11y: the picker's Select trigger surfaces as AXStaticText and its current value ('English') is absent from the accessibility tree entirely. · 2026-07-29 [amend-docs] **BRD-91 AMENDED (owner decision): the shipped locales are now NAMED `en` + `hi` (Hindi); `de`/`fr` are WITHDRAWN.** The requirement previously said 'at least 2 locales' and named none, so an earlier build shipped German and French on no recorded basis — nobody chose them against the audience. Target audience is India. **This row is re-scoped to: mechanism + Hindi.** What survives unchanged: the `.resx` mechanism, `LanguagePicker`, `AppStrings.cs`, the store/persistence path, `LocalizationTests`, and the 2026-07-29 live proof that a locale switch really does re-render the app in another language (proven with German, which is now being removed — the MECHANISM proof stands, the content does not). What must change in the next build: add `AppStrings.hi.resx`, delete `AppStrings.de.resx` + `AppStrings.fr.resx`, and drop the `de`/`fr` entries from `SupportedLanguages.cs:32-33`. ⚠ Hindi is **Devanagari, not RTL** — needs font-fallback verification inside the BlazorWebView (a missing glyph renders as tofu boxes and a screenshot review WILL catch it), but no RTL layout work; the BRD's RTL deferral stands. % cut 45→25 because the second locale is now unimplemented rather than implemented-but-wrong. · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] **Locale swap COMPLETE**: `AppStrings.hi.resx` added (106 keys, complete), `AppStrings.de.resx` and `.fr.resx` deleted, `SupportedLanguages.cs` lists `en` + `hi` only, Hindi named in itself as `new AppLanguage("hi", "Hindi", "हिन्दी")` per the file's own convention. 🔴 **Deleting the .resx files was NOT sufficient and German would still have shipped**: the compiled `de/` and `fr/` **satellite assemblies survived in 20+ bin/obj directories including `TechieDesk.app/Contents/MonoBundle/` and TechieDeskScheduler's Release output**, because nothing in the build removes a satellite whose source is gone. Purged and verified — 16 `hi` satellites, 0 `de`, 0 `fr`. 🔴 **`OffersEnglishPlusTwo` replaced by `OffersEnglishAndHindi`, now asserting the exact sequence `[en, hi]` rather than a COUNT** — a count-based assertion is precisely what stayed green while the product shipped two languages nobody had chosen. New `WithdrawnLocalesAreGone` (deleting a .resx otherwise leaves NO failing test — lookups quietly return English), `EveryHindiStringIsWrittenInDevanagari` (**plumbing only, explicitly not a glyph check**), `FormattedStringsKeepTheirPlaceholders` (a dropped `{0}` is invisible to key-set checks and silently deletes the workspace name from the breadcrumb). 36/36 localization tests green. On the shipping Release bundle the app logs `UI language set to hi (Hindi)` at startup, so the mechanism resolves end to end. ⚠ **Translations are agent-produced, NOT a native speaker's — review recommended before shipping.** Register is deliberately conservative (loanwords kept where Hindi-speaking software users actually use them; product/protocol nouns left in Latin script). Least confident: `टेक्स्ट इनजेशन` (ingestion has no settled Hindi form), `मूल्य निर्धारण` for Pricing (correct but formal), `जामुनी` for Indigo. 🔴 **No screen was ever seen in Hindi — the tofu-box check is outstanding** and both rows are capped until it runs. · 2026-07-29 [build-phase smoke] 🔴 **DEVANAGARI CONFIRMED — zero tofu.** Driven on the universal maccatalyst bundle via Appium/mac2 after selecting हिन्दी and restarting (log: `UI language set to hi (Hindi)`). | REQ-UI-040 | No-code agent flow builder UI (BRD-92, P5) | PARTIAL | 70% | 2026-08-01 [*build-phase Cluster D] **BUILT AND LIVE-SMOKED — the builder half.** Ships at `/workspace/{slug}/flows` as a **structured list/outline editor, not a canvas** — the prebuilt `trblazeui.css` has no JIT (TR-007) and a bespoke drag-drop canvas in a BlazorWebView is disproportionate risk for a P5 feature; the builder also found an empirical reason nobody had raised: **mac2 cannot drive WKWebView drag gestures, so a canvas would have been unsmokeable too.** Create/list/edit/enable-disable/delete/run, workspace-scoped, persisted by migration `0008-Flow.sql` (`DefinitionJson` holds `FlowSerializer.ToJson` VERBATIM as the single source of truth; Name/Description/SchemaVersion mirrored only so the list can sort and grey a row without parsing every blob; `TryFromJson` per row so **one corrupt document cannot break the page** — it renders "Unreadable" with the parse error and offers only Delete). **Node editors render GENERICALLY from `FlowNodeCatalog.Describe(kind).Fields`**, switching only on `FlowFieldDescriptor.Editor` — so a future node kind needs no change to this file, verified live (the Agent step and the End step rendered different forms from the catalogue alone). Live `FlowValidator.Validate` on every edit, localized **by stable code** (all 26 `FlowValidationCodes`) and highlighted against the offending step/link; a branch preview runs `FlowCondition.IsSatisfiedBy` against a sample value without executing anything. **All five app-side items REQ-RAG-042 left are done**: persistence; 🔴 the **7 new `AgentStepKind` trace arms** in `AgentTrace.cs` — without them every flow step fell through the `_ =>` arm and rendered as **"Final answer"**, the same lying-surface class as REQ-FN-010 / REQ-NFR-013 / REQ-FN-052 — now covered by 11 tests and RED-proven by deleting the arms (10/11 red); the REQ-NFR-013 **egress gate installed as a `DelegateFlowGuardrail` in `FlowRuntime.HostGuardrails` on every run**, under an id no flow can name, add or remove (7 tests, RED-proven twice — including one that empties every node's own `GuardrailIds` and confirms the gate still holds, i.e. **a flow is not a route around it**); `IFlowAgentResolver` over `AgentRegistry` **by handle** (what the user typed and what `@mentions` already use, not a surrogate row id) plus a 3-entry app guardrail catalogue, both reusing the SAME per-turn skill+MCP tool composition chat uses, so a flow cannot become a wider tool path than chat; Serialization is sound (ThreadExporterTests 5/5 green), but the DELIVERY path is a browser blob download: apps/TechieDesk/wwwroot/js/download.js does `anchor.download = fileName; anchor.click()` on a Blob URL, invoked from WorkspaceChat.razor:800-801. Inside a Mac Catalyst BlazorWebView this runs in WKWebView, which routes an anchor download through a WKDownloadDelegate that MAUI's BlazorWebView does NOT install — so the click has no handler and no file is written, while WorkspaceChat.razor:802 unconditionally raises ToastService.Success('Exported ...'). The row's contrary evidence ('live smoke downloaded 566B MD + 845B JSON') was captured against the DELETED browser head. This is the only download.js call site, so the defect is contained; the fix is a native save path (NSSavePanel / FileSaver), not a JS one. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-010] ✅ **SILENT FALSE SUCCESS FIXED — a file now actually lands on disk.** Delivery rewritten from the WKWebView Blob-anchor download to a native save path: new `IFileSaveService` / `FileSaveResult` / `FileSaveStatus` in `TechieDesk.Core/Services/Files`, `MacCatalystFileSaveService` using `UIDocumentPickerViewController(initForExportingURLs:asCopy:)` — **not `NSSavePanel`, which AppKit cannot reach from Catalyst without a plug-in bundle** — following the existing `DesktopFolderPicker` pattern, and `ThreadExportService` which **stats the returned path** so a saver claiming success while writing nothing is downgraded to `Failed`. `FileSaveStatus` is deliberately three states (Saved/Cancelled/Failed), not a bool, which is what makes "cancel raises no toast" expressible. `WorkspaceChat.razor` toasts Success only on `IsSuccess`, Error on `IsError`, and **nothing on cancel**. `wwwroot/js/download.js` DELETED (it was the only call site — leaving it would be a trap). `ThreadExporter` untouched, its 5 tests still 5/5. PROOF OF WRITE (real store `techiedesk-rag-store.db`, real thread `d4a9cf2a-2144-4fdb-b755-bb107184a329`, 10 messages, no invented user): Markdown **982 bytes** and JSON **1,943 bytes** both confirmed on disk with correct role/content/sources/timestamps; cancel path produced `Cancelled` + no file + no toast; unsupported head returns `Failed` with an honest message, never a silent no-op. +12 tests including the defect-pinning `ExportReportsFailureWhenSaverClaimsSuccessButWritesNoFile`. ⚠ **NOT yet driven through the real macOS save panel** — the panel presentation, its pre-filled filename and its cancel behaviour are unobserved (harness down). The cancel observation is the one that must be seen: a success toast there is a regression to the original defect. ⚠ Windows has no `IFileSaveService` implementation, so a Windows build resolves `UnsupportedFileSaveService` and export fails LOUDLY rather than lying (see REQ-FN-035). | [view](#d-req-fn-010) |

| REQ-FN-020 | Connector background jobs + progress (BRD-65, P2) | Implemented | 90% | **[REQ-FN-020] 2026-07-28 — built on the EXISTING scheduler, not a second one.** `ConnectorJobHandler : IScheduledJobHandler` plugs into `AddTechieDeskScheduling` (ADR-009, "one scheduler, two possible hosts"); `AddTechieDeskConnectorJobs()` is a plain `AddSingleton<IScheduledJobHandler, …>` — no timer, no queue, no second run table, no second in-flight registry. Live smoke confirms one runner with two handlers (`[di] handlers registered : Maintenance, Connector`). New in `apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/Connectors/`: `IConnectorJobService`/`ConnectorJobService` (a filtered *view* over `IBackgroundJobService` + `IScheduleRunRepository`), `ConnectorJobPayload`, `ConnectorRunReport`, `ObservedConnector` (decorates `IDataConnector` — the `ProgressReportingWebContentFetcher` pattern), `RagConnectorDocumentSink`, `IConnectorResolver`. 25 tests, deterministic (no sleeps — mid-run reached via a `BeforeFetch` hook, background waits on a `TaskCompletionSource`). **Behaviour-removal proven RED:** dropping the per-item `reason` argument → 5 tests red; deleting the `Unchanged` reconciliation → 1 test red. **BRD-65 honesty rule enforced end to end** — live in-process smoke over a real migrated SQLite DB + real scheduler + real `ITechieRag` showed 18 distinct progress snapshots *during* the run, a `Partial` outcome, and every non-ingested item naming its reason (`403` on a path, "held no readable text", "9,000,000 bytes exceeds the 2,097,152-byte limit"); cancellation kept the already-ingested documents **and** their sync state; re-sync of unchanged items ingested nothing. `PercentComplete` is deliberately **null until items are listed** and may go *down* as new pages are listed — honest over a guessed total. ⚠ **Open gaps:** (1) a **changed item DUPLICATES rather than replaces** its document (9 → 12 catalogue rows after a version bump) — `IngestTextAsync` has no upsert and nothing maps item→document; (2) **two hand-started runs of the same connector can overlap** — `BackgroundJobService.StartAsync` has no in-flight guard and `IsRunning` keys on `ScheduleId`, which is null for hand-started runs, so a double-click on "Sync now" double-ingests; (3) unchanged/oversize items are recorded only at end-of-run and are **lost if the run is cancelled** (TR-RAG-022 — `ConnectorRunner` returns everything in one value at the end, so a run stopped at item 480/500 ingests zero); (4) non-failure item rows capped at 200/run; (5) `GetReportAsync` scans a 200-run window — an older run is invisible to the screen. ~~(1) and (2) are assigned to the wiring cluster.~~ **UPDATE 2026-07-28 — (1) and (2) are FIXED and the job layer is now wired and UI-visible.** `AddTechieDeskConnectorJobs()` is registered in `MauiProgram.cs:289` and `TechieDeskScheduler/Program.cs:211`. **(1) re-sync duplication fixed** via a `ConnectorItemDocument` (ItemId→DocumentId) map consulted before each ingest; the new document is written **first** and the superseded one deleted after, so a failed ingest cannot destroy the searchable copy — proven with 3 files version-bumped, catalogue stays 3 and the old text is gone from the index (mutation: bypassing the map gives "Expected 3, Actual 6"). **(2) double-start fixed** with an in-flight claim keyed `Connector:<connectorId>` taken before anything starts; the second caller **joins** the winner's run instead of opening a second one — live: "click 1 → run 2; click 2 → run 2; SAME run". Gaps (3)–(5) remain open (TR-RAG-022). **UI now consumes this** — `ConnectorsHub.razor` renders live progress, run history and per-item results with reasons; live-smoked on Mac Catalyst, 0 `[ERR]`/`[FTL]`, visual PASS at 1800px and 789px, screenshots read. A real run was driven from the UI: **Completed · 2/2 · 1m44s**. ⚠ **Still unexercised on the running app:** the `CredentialResolves == false` warning banner (no product path can currently produce that state) and per-keystroke input behaviour (AX text entry kills the Catalyst WKWebView, so form values came from temporarily seeded initializers, reverted byte-identical — sha256 verified). ⚠ **The scheduler helper cannot complete an ingest on macOS** — it boots, resolves the connector, reaches GitHub, lists and fetches, then every ingest dies on TR-RAG-025 (ONNX native load); recorded honestly as `Partial` with named per-item failures, nothing silent. "Sync at 07:00" is the helper's whole purpose, so this matters. Logged TR-RAG-022 (major, open), TR-RAG-023 (minor), TR-RAG-024/025/026 (major, open). Pending verifier · 2026-07-29 verifier: background-job acceptance fully asserted — 31 tests green across ConnectorJobHandlerTests 19/19, ConnectorJobServiceTests 6/6 and ConnectorEndToEndTests 6/6, covering live progress (ProgressCarriesAStatusLineAndOnlyEverARealPercentage), per-item results, named failure reasons, partial-run honesty (APartialRunIsNeverReportedAsASuccess) and cancel-preserves-ingested. The screen render remains a UI gate. | [view](#d-req-fn-020) |

| REQ-FN-032 | Session continuity across circuits (login loop) | Blocked | 70% | 2026-07-25 FIXED — owner chose the signed-cookie/opaque-handle option this session. Per-circuit SessionTokenStore replaced by a handle-keyed singleton `ISessionStore`; the browser holds only a signed HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Lax `td.sid` cookie whose single claim is a 256-bit base64url opaque handle — **no JWT, no email, no role, so 'tokens never leave the server' HOLDS** (REQ-FN-002/NFR-004 preserved). Circuits adopt the handle via `IHostEnvironmentAuthenticationStateProvider` at circuit start (log: 'Circuit adopted a session handle … present: True'); login moved to a real `POST /auth/login` endpoint, the only place the cookie is written. Handle rotates on login (session fixation); antiforgery validated before any AppManager call; returnUrl filtered against open redirect; sliding 60min + hard 12h expiry. LIVE-PROVEN end-to-end incl. F5 survival. Offline mode unchanged (20/20 route sweep). ⚠ single-instance in-process store (documented); silent-refresh + all-devices-logout + expiry unit-tested only, not driven live. ⚠ /auth/logout is an unprotected GET (nuisance-class CSRF) — accepted, flagged · ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-26 by BRD-132 → REQ-FN-039.** MAUI Blazor Hybrid has no circuits, so there is no session to lose across a navigation and nothing for the `td.sid` cookie to solve. `SessionCookie`, `SessionHandle`, `SessionStore`, `SessionContext`, `SessionEndpoints` and the `POST /auth/login` endpoint become removable once tokens move to the OS credential store. The 5-day login-loop blocker this closed is genuinely gone — dissolved by the form factor, not regressed. · 2026-07-29 [REQ-FN-032] **ACCEPTANCE RE-SCOPED from 'across circuits' to the desktop model (owner-authorised).** The 2026-07-25 evidence certified a signed `td.sid` cookie, per-circuit handle adoption via `IHostEnvironmentAuthenticationStateProvider`, `POST /auth/login` and antiforgery — **none of which exists on this head**; the login loop is dissolved by the form factor, not regressed. Replacement acceptance is 10 clauses across the two boundaries that remain: a new component scope, and a process relaunch. **The real mechanism:** `ISessionStore`/`ISessionContext` are SINGLETONS (`DesktopAuthExtensions.cs:83,87`), so there is nothing to adopt; the one live session is mirrored through `ISecretStore` (Keychain, key `techiedesk.session.v1`) and restored lazily on first read, minting a fresh handle while carrying the ORIGINAL 12h hard deadline (**restore, never renew**); the 60min idle window bites in a running app but deliberately not across a closed one; `SessionTokenStore.Changed` keeps the stored copy in step with REQ-FN-002 silent refresh. **SECURITY INVARIANT VERIFIED, NOT REGRESSED — arguably strengthened:** on a BlazorWebView the browser is in-process, so the boundary is the rendered principal, which carries NameIdentifier/Name/Email/Role and **no access token, no refresh token, not even the opaque handle**; no token reaches `localStorage` (only `td-ui-scale`/`td-theme-cache`), `document.cookie`, any JS-interop argument/return, or either `[JSInvokable]`; devtools are `#if DEBUG` only; and `ISecretStore` implementations are forbidden a file fallback (they degrade to memory). At-rest protection moved from a file-backed DataProtection key ring to the machine+user-bound Keychain. **LIVE:** TWO real process relaunches, each confirmed a fresh process by new startup lines in the app's own log, both landing in the workspace shell with **never a forced login** — clause 3 proven twice. 🔴 **Clause 4 (a signed-in session surviving relaunch) CANNOT hold on any build produced today — see REQ-FN-043:** every launch logs `The OS credential store rejected this process (Exception): Error adding record: MissingEntitlement`, so `OsCredentialStore` falls back to `EphemeralSecretStore` (`IsDurable=false`). Not a confidentiality failure (nothing is written to disk) but a functional one. **Dead-code finding for REQ-FN-041:** `SessionCookie.cs` IS now unreachable — its only consumer is `TechieDeskAuthenticationStateProvider.AttachHandleFrom`, reached only from `SetAuthenticationState`, which nothing on a WebView head ever calls; REQ-FN-041's stated reason for keeping it is obsolete, and that method is a latent hazard (it would `AttachHandle(null)` and wipe the live session). 🔴 **Conversely `SessionStore`/`SessionStoreOptions`/`ISessionStore`/`ISessionContext`/`SessionHandle`/`SessionTokenStore` are ALL LIVE and must NOT be deleted — REQ-FN-041's current scope line lists them and would break REQ-FN-039 if executed.** +7 tests incl. the singleton-lifetime guard and the no-token-in-the-principal invariant. · 2026-07-29 verifier: 9 of the 10 re-scoped clauses PASS — DesktopSessionContinuityTests 13/13 including the singleton-lifetime guard (SessionIsAppWideNotPerScope), the no-token-in-the-principal invariant (RenderedPrincipalCarriesNoTokenMaterial), NoHttpOrCookieMachineryIsRegistered and SignOutIsVisibleToEveryLaterScope, plus SecretStoreSessionTests 18/18 and SessionStoreTests 10/10. 🔴 Clause 4 (a signed-in session surviving a real relaunch) FAILS live and is provably unreachable on ANY build produced today: all SEVEN launches logged today hit OsCredentialStore MissingEntitlement and degrade to EphemeralSecretStore. Telling detail — the suite's own ANonDurableSecretStoreLosesTheSessionOnRestartAndSaysSo asserts the DEGRADED behaviour, i.e. it documents the failure rather than the acceptance. Moved FAIL to `Blocked` because the cause is REQ-FN-043 (no Entitlements.plist, no CodesignEntitlements) awaiting the owner's signing-identity decision, not app code. NOT a confidentiality regression — nothing token-shaped reaches disk. | [view](#d-req-fn-032) |

(2) **Persistence**: new migration `0007-McpServer.sql` (one workspace-scoped table, unique on WorkspaceId+ServerName), `SqliteMcpServerRegistry` over Dapper, registered in DI as `IMcpServerRegistry` — the library's process-lifetime `InMemoryMcpServerRegistry` is **never** registered, pinned by a test that goes red if it is. Arguments persist as a **JSON array**, not a command line, so nothing has to re-split them. (3) **Agent-loop wiring** via `McpAgentExtensions`, with local skills winning a name clash so nothing can shadow `rag-search`. **SECURITY**: HTTP-hosted MCP tools inherit `EgressGate` (REQ-NFR-013) keyed off the stored **transport**, not a name list, sharing the same once-per-turn decision and failing closed; a gated tool stays visible to the model and it is the outbound call that stops, so a decline returns `SkillUnavailable` and the turn survives. ⚠ **stdio tools are deliberately NOT gated** — a local child process does not leave the machine, and the gate's own prompt wording ("sends a request off this machine") would be **false**; training a user to approve a dialog whose text does not match reality is the exact failure REQ-NFR-013 was raised about. Its consent is the registration itself. Pinned by `StdioToolsAreNotEgressGated` so the decision cannot flip silently either way, and the limit is stated plainly: if a stdio program makes its own network calls, TechieDesk cannot observe that and does not claim to. Credentials go to the OS credential store, falling back to the REQ-NFR-004b encrypted sidecar and then to memory — **no plaintext path exists**, asserted by a whole-file byte scan of the DB; the row keeps only NAMES, so REQ-FN-043's missing keychain degrades to "Stored value missing for: X" rather than calling out unauthenticated. **Proven against REAL servers**: a loopback `TcpListener` speaking actual HTTP+JSON-RPC and a `/bin/sh` child over stdio, with real tool calls round-tripping through the agent loop on both. REQ-NFR-008 guarded by **byte count at the socket**, and the counter itself mutated to hardcode 0 to prove the assertion is not vacuous. 38 tests, 11 RED-proven by mutation. Library gap **TR-RAG-041** filed (`BuildWorkspaceToolsAsync` returns no tool→server mapping, so the egress guard must prefix-match — ambiguity resolves toward GATING, so the worst case is an extra confirmation, never a silent egress). · 2026-07-31 [*verify all, chained inline] ✅ **PROVEN LIVE ON THE RUNNING APP, not just in tests.** Registered a real stdio MCP server through the UI on the Catalyst head: **Test connection returned "Connected. 1 tool(s) available." listing `whoami`** — the app launched the child process and spoke real JSON-RPC. Saved; the DB row is correct (`Transport=Stdio`, `Command=/bin/sh`, `Arguments=["/tmp/mcpsmoke.sh"]`). 🔴 **COLD RESTART PASSED** — process killed, fresh session, fresh launch, and the registration survived and rendered. That is precisely the failure the in-memory registry would have had, and it is the whole point of the persistence deliverable. All test data cleaned up. ⚠ **One deviation from the builder's own prediction**: the freshly-saved row shows **no advertised tools**, because `RegisterAsync` clears the tool cache on save and discards what Test connection had just discovered — so a user must press Test again to see the list. ⚠ Held at `Implemented 90%`, not `Verified`: an MCP tool CALL through a live agent turn needs an LLM to decide to invoke it, and no provider is reachable on this host — discovery and transport were proven instead. | REQ-RAG-031 | LIB: web ingestion (URL/crawler/YouTube) (BRD-112, P2) | Blocked | 90% | **[REQ-RAG-031] 2026-07-27 — IMPLEMENTED in the library; unblocks REQ-RAG-016/017/018.** New `src/TechieRag/Web/`: `WebPage`, `WebPageReader`, `IWebContentFetcher` + `HttpWebContentFetcher`, `WebFetchException`, `WebCrawlOptions`, `SiteCrawler` (+ `CrawlResult`/`CrawlFailure`), `YouTubeUrl`, `YouTubeTranscriptReader` (+ `YouTubeTranscript`), `WebIngestionExtensions` (+ `WebIngestionResult`). **No new package** — reuses the HtmlAgilityPack the HTML processor already pulls. Ingestion is exposed as **extension methods on `ITechieRag`**, not new interface members: the interface is implemented by consumers of a published package, so adding members breaks every implementer, and web ingestion is strictly fetch + `IngestTextAsync` with no new storage behaviour. **⚠ SECURITY — SSRF is the real risk here and is treated as one.** A crawler follows links it did not choose, so a public page can point it at `http://localhost:8080` or `http://169.254.169.254` and use the app as a proxy into the user's own network. Private/loopback/link-local targets are refused **by default** (`BlockPrivateNetworkTargets`), the check runs on the seed, on every candidate link, **and again on the post-redirect final URL** — without that last one the guard is one HTTP 302 away from useless. Settable, because crawling an intranet is legitimate when the operator chooses it. Documented limit: literal-address and well-known-name checks only, no DNS resolution — stated in the XML docs rather than implied. Other bounds, all conservative by default: `MaxDepth` 1, `MaxPages` 25, `SameHostOnly` true, 250 ms politeness delay, 8 MB body cap enforced **while reading** (not just from `Content-Length`, which is advisory and often absent), non-HTML content types refused. Crawl is **breadth-first on purpose** — depth-first with a page budget spends the whole budget descending one branch. Extraction drops site chrome (`nav`/`header`/`footer`/`aside`) from the TEXT but still follows its LINKS: chrome text repeated on every page would dominate a site's embeddings, while chrome links are where the site's structure lives. Fragments and trailing slashes are normalised so one document is not crawled and ingested twice. YouTube handles `watch?v=`/`youtu.be`/`embed`/`shorts`/`live`/bare-id, prefers a human track over ASR (ASR punctuation is poor and chunking follows punctuation), and **fails loudly when a video has no captions** rather than ingesting an empty document. ⚠ **Honest fragility:** YouTube publishes no transcript API, so the reader parses `captionTracks` out of the watch page's embedded player JSON — an undocumented internal shape that **will** break when YouTube changes it; it is written to fail with an operator-facing reason, not to return blank text. 51 new tests (135/135 lib, from 84), **7 behaviour-removal mutations proven RED** (6 SSRF link cases + the page-budget cap). ~~⚠ **Not driven against a live site** — every test runs a fake fetcher; no real network crawl was performed.~~ **2026-07-27 — LIVE-RUN GAP CLOSED, and it found 4 real defects the 135-test hermetic suite could not.** 56 further tests added (33 hermetic + 23 live, opt-in behind `TechieRagLiveNetworkTests=1` + `[Trait("Category","LiveNetwork")]` so the default suite stays hermetic and green). Proven against the real network: single-page fetch/extraction, a real bounded crawl of `quotes.toscrape.com`, real-redirect SSRF refusal, and end-to-end retrieval through the real BGE-M3 ONNX model + real SQLite-vec store. Defects found and fixed: (1) **SSRF guard walkable via a public DNS name resolving to loopback** — now enforced at connect time (see REQ-RAG-017); (2) **`SqliteVecStore` silently discards document metadata** (TR-RAG-014) — blank source URL on every ingested row; (3) **caption-track regex truncated on nested brackets** — 31 tracks read as none; (4) misleading YouTube diagnostics. ⚠ **YouTube transcript download is dead platform-side (TR-RAG-015)** — blocks REQ-RAG-018, needs an owner product decision. ⚠ Also logged: **TR-RAG-016** — `UseEmbedded()` throws `DllNotFoundException` on macOS/Linux for any plain `net10.0` consumer (ORT's `net8.0` assembly hardcodes `onnxruntime.dll`; package ships `libonnxruntime.dylib`); invisible from the MAUI heads, total for tests/console/server consumers. Pending verifier · App surfaces REQ-RAG-016/017 now built and live-proven; 018 blocked · 2026-07-29 verifier: two of the three deliverables are LIVE-PROVEN today (URL scrape, and a bounded site crawl with depth, maxLinks and politeness delay all honoured against the real internet). The third, YouTube transcripts, is blocked by TR-RAG-015 — re-confirmed failing against a real video today. The remaining 10% is the library block, not app work. | [view](#d-req-rag-031) |

Ingestion exposed as an **extension method** on `ITechieRag` (`IngestConnectorAsync`) — no new interface members, so no break for package implementers. ⚠ **TWO MAJOR SECURITY DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED, neither of which had any test at all.** (1) **TR-RAG-018 — Atlassian token exfiltration:** `ConfluenceConnector` followed `_links.next`/`_links.base` taken from the **response body** while attaching `Authorization: Basic base64(email:token)`, so a hostile response could redirect the walk to any host, steal the credential and set every RAG citation link; fixed by pinning scheme+host+port on cursors, next-links, link bases and absolute page links. **Not an SSRF case** — the attacker names a *public* host, so the private-network guard would never have caught it. (2) **TR-RAG-017 — the SSRF guard was re-derived weakly and defaulted OFF** (`blockPrivateTargets = false`, with a code comment wrongly arguing connectors' threat model differed), and `CreateDefaultClient()` built a bare handler with **no connect-time guard at all**; now reuses `HttpWebContentFetcher.CreateGuardedHandler`. (3) TR-RAG-019 — no aggregate byte budget (worst case `MaxItems × MaxItemBytes` = 1 GB); `MaxTotalBytes` = 64 MB added. **10 behaviour-removal mutations proven RED** (target was 7). Tokens never logged, never in a URL, never in an exception message. 31 hermetic + 5 live tests added (lib suite 476 → 507). **LIVE-PROVEN against `github.com/octocat/Spoon-Knife`:** glob `**/*.md` returned README and genuinely omitted `index.html`/`styles.css`; explicit branch honoured and a bogus branch 404'd naming it; **incremental sync works — a second run with the returned sync state fetched nothing**; `127.0.0.1.nip.io` refused at connect time. ⚠ **BREAKING for self-hosted:** `blockPrivateTargets` now defaults **true** at both `HttpConnectorTransport(..)` and `CreateDefaultClient(..)` — an intranet GitLab/Confluence needs an explicit opt-in at two call sites. ⚠ **Confluence and GitLab were NEVER exercised against a real host** (no Atlassian site, no token, no GitLab instance) — proven only against fake transports built from API docs; treat Confluence as unproven against reality, the same gap class that produced TR-RAG-014/015/016. ⚠ Open: TR-RAG-020 (runner materialises all fetched documents — no streaming API), TR-RAG-021 (`IConnectorTransport` is GET-only, forecloses Confluence CQL POST + GitHub GraphQL), GitLab tree API returns no file size so oversized GitLab files cannot be skipped pre-download, GitHub truncates the recursive tree for large monorepos. ⚠ **`src/TechieRag/Connectors/Email/` (~1,700 lines: hand-rolled `SocketImapConnection` + `MimeParser`) also pre-exists and was NOT audited** — REQ-RAG-049 still says `Not Started`; its transport bypasses the guarded handler. | REQ-RAG-042 | LIB: agent orchestration framework (BRD-123, P5) | N/A | 95% | **Moved 2026-09-03 → `docs/TechieRag-Checklist.md` `REQ-RAG-042` (same ID; status at move `Implemented 95%`; BRD-146 governance reversal — library rows live in the library checklist).** Prior remark retained: 2026-08-01 [*build-phase Cluster C] **BUILT — `src/TechieRag/Orchestration/`, 21 new files, ZERO new packages.** All four capabilities land ON TOP of the existing single-agent loop rather than beside it: an `Agent` node runs the shipped `AgentLoopRunner` unchanged, and `AgentToolHandler` composes through `IToolHandler`/`CompositeToolHandler`, so the loop needed no orchestration branch. **(1) GRAPHS** — 5 node kinds (Agent/Tool/Condition/Handoff/Terminal) with declarative `FlowCondition` routing (11 operators over 4 sources); conditions are data, not delegates, deliberately — a flow is persisted USER DATA, and a delegate cannot be stored, shown in an editor, validated, or prevented from smuggling code past the host. **Termination is TRIPLE-bounded and none is trusted alone**: cycles are a validation ERROR unless `AllowCycles` is set (default refuse), every run is capped by `MaxSteps` regardless of graph shape (this protects flows that never went through validation — an older version, a hand-edited row), and each agent turn by the loop’s own ceiling. **(2) HANDOFFS** — exactly three things cross: the text selected by `HandoffContextMode` (default `LastOutputOnly`; `FullTranscript` is opt-in and named for what it costs), the variables on the `CarryVariables` **ALLOWLIST**, and the reason. The sender’s system prompt, tool definitions, tool results and unlisted variables **never** cross, and the receiver always starts from its own prompt — so an agent behaves identically wherever a flow places it. Allowlist not denylist, deliberately: a denylist would let a variable added later start leaking downstream the moment it was introduced. **(3) GUARDRAILS** — `IFlowGuardrail` at Input/Output/ToolCall, **deny-by-default at every failure mode** (throws, returns null, unresolvable id, no resolver configured) enforced in ONE place so a check cannot fail to run and let the step proceed. A `rewrite-the-payload` verdict was considered and REJECTED: a guardrail that silently alters what an agent sees makes the trace stop being a true account of the run. **`FlowRuntime.HostGuardrails` is the seam REQ-NFR-013’s `EgressGate` plugs into** — runtime-supplied, applied to every node and every tool call, evaluated FIRST, not nameable by the flow and impossible to disable by editing one. That is the trust boundary: a flow definition is untrusted user data, a runtime is host code. Proven by two tests that a flow cannot become a route around the gate (an agent asking for a gated tool, AND a bare deterministic Tool node). A ToolCall block returns an unsuccessful `ToolResult` the model can adapt to, mirroring `EgressGate`’s existing `SkillUnavailable` channel, so the turn survives. **(4) AGENT-AS-TOOL** — one-string schema (a structured contract would force the caller to know the sub-agent’s internals, which is the coupling this exists to avoid); recursion bounded three ways, so two agents wired as each other’s tools terminate rather than having to be prevented. **TRACE IS NOT FORKED**: `FlowStep : AgentStep` on the SAME `IProgress<AgentStep>` channel, with 7 APPENDED `AgentStepKind` members; the original four keep their names AND ordinals, and `AgentLoopRunner` still emits only those four as plain `AgentStep` — all three pinned by tests, so the app’s existing trace renderer (REQ-RAG-021/BRD-85) is unaffected. **ZERO-EGRESS byte-counted at a real `HttpListener` on 127.0.0.1 with the seam ARMED** — a real `OpenAICompatibleLlmProvider` pointed at the collector and registered as resolvable, so an eager warm-up would land bytes — plus a **permanent positive-control test** proving the seam actually sees traffic, so the zero can never go vacuous. 64 new library tests. **18 RED-proof mutations run, and the builder disclosed that TWO initially came back GREEN** — including the egress one, whose injected request went to a closed port and never reached the collector; it recognised the MUTATION was ineffective rather than concluding the test was strong, and redid both (R4b, R14b). ⚠ The step-budget mutation **HUNG for 900s** rather than failing cleanly — reported as a hang, which is itself the proof that `MaxSteps` is what terminates a cyclic flow. **TR-RAG-041 FIXED in the library** (`McpToolHandler.ServerNameFor`, `McpWorkspaceTools.ToolsByServer`, populated from discovery already performed — no extra round trip) rather than filed onward, because the guardrail seam needs per-tool server provenance and **a security boundary should not be deduced from a string prefix**. Build 0 errors, no new warnings, no packages, `--vulnerable` clean; TechieRag.Tests **661 pass / 0 fail** (was 597), TechieDesk.Tests 1,323 pass / 0 fail — `AgentLoopRunner` consumers unaffected. Smoked at its own layer: a real two-agent flow (classify → conditional branch → handoff → close) driven in-process with real tool handlers and a host egress guardrail; the gate held and the handoff carried only declared context. ⚠ Held at `Implemented`, not `Verified`: no verify-phase has run today. ⚠ **Not reachable from any UI yet — REQ-UI-040 (BRD-92) owns that**, and the library cluster left it three things: the app-side persistence table, explicit `AgentTrace` arms for the 7 new kinds (they currently fall through its `_ =>` arm and would render as "Final answer"), and the `EgressGate`→`HostGuardrails` wiring. Was `Not Started 0%`. · 2026-07-29 verifier: confirmed still absent — grep across src/ for AgentGraph, Handoff, Guardrail and AgentAsTool returns ZERO hits. Correctly Not Started (P5). | [view](#d-req-rag-042) |

| REQ-FN-034 | Migrator and app resolve ONE data directory | Implemented | 95% | ✅ 2026-07-26 FIXED (owner approved the one-data-directory approach). New `TechieDeskDb.DataDirectory` is the single authority for every persistent artefact; **no path is resolved against the CWD any more**. Program.cs resolves it once and PINS `AppDb:ConnectionString`, so the migrator and the Dapper repositories cannot diverge by construction; overridable via `AppDb__DataDirectory`. Also folded in 3 further sites found while fixing: the SqliteVec vector DB (was CWD-relative AND outside the volume mount — embeddings lost on `docker compose down`), the saved provider config (was in ContentRoot, same volume gap), and a 4th independent read of that config inside TechieRagManager that could disagree with its own writer. Legacy artefacts are relocated once rather than orphaned. LIVE-PROVEN on a clean slate: boot logs the absolute path, all four artefacts land in `data/` (`techiedesk.db` 61,440B + `techierag.db` + `techiedesk-rag-store.db` + `keys/`), `bin/…/data` left EMPTY, and **`/workspace/default/settings` renders all 4 tabs with real data** — the screen that previously died with `no such table: WorkspaceAssignment`. 7 new xUnit tests pin the invariant (incl. CWD-independence + relocation safety); 332/332 total. Regression specs: tests/verify/build-2026-07-25-{smoke,workspace-probe}.spec.ts 4/4. **Original defect (2026-07-25):** MigrationRunner defaulted to `Data Source=data/techiedesk.db` (CWD-relative) while AppDbConnectionFactory defaulted to `AppContext.BaseDirectory/data/techiedesk.db` (bin-relative) — different files on a plain `dotnet run`, so DbUp migrated a 61,440-byte database while the app opened a **0-byte** one and the boot log reported success either way. Pending verifier · ⚠ **CARRIED FORWARD 2026-07-26 into BRD-130 → REQ-FN-037.** The single-data-directory invariant is retained and strengthened: the directory moves from an app-relative `data/` to the per-user OS location, and DbUp runs in-process at launch so the migrator and the app cannot resolve different files by construction. The regression test moves with it. · 2026-07-29 verifier: the invariant holds on the shipped head — DataDirectoryTests 14/14 green (migrator-app path identity, CWD-independence, relocation safety) and every one of today's 7 launches resolves one absolute directory with all four artefacts co-located. ⚠ TWO caveats worth recording. (1) DataDirectoryTests.EveryArtefactSharesOneDirectory (lines 136-151) is TAUTOLOGICAL — it constructs the paths itself with Path.Combine(dataDirectory, ...) then asserts GetDirectoryName equals dataDirectory; it cannot fail and observes no production call site. The genuine guards are MigratorDefaultMatchesTheResolvedAppDatabase and DefaultResolutionIsAbsoluteAndIndependentOfWorkingDirectory. (2) The claim 'no path is resolved against the CWD any more' is NOT true of the TechieDeskDb.Cli head — Program.cs:30 writes `logs/techiedeskdb-.log`, a bare relative path, and ./logs/techiedeskdb-2026072*.log sit in the REPO ROOT. Low severity (dev-only console head, no secrets) but it falsifies a stated invariant, and the tautological test is exactly why nothing caught it. · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] 🔴 **DEMOTED from `Verified 100%` — the row's headline invariant is FALSE, proven twice today by two independent clusters.** The claim 'no path is resolved against the CWD any more' does not hold. **(1)** `TechieDeskDb.Cli/Program.cs:30` writes `logs/techiedeskdb-.log`, a bare relative path — the files sit in the REPO ROOT (found by the 2026-07-29 verifier). **(2) Worse, and this one is a DATABASE**: the default `VectorStore.ConnectionString` is the relative `Data Source=techierag.db`, resolved against the process CWD; on the Mac Catalyst head that CWD is the **`.app` bundle root**, so a run wrote a live 24 KB `techierag.db` INTO `apps/TechieDesk/bin/Release/net10.0-maccatalyst/TechieDesk.app/`. That **breaks codesign outright** ('unsealed contents present in the bundle root') and puts user data inside a signed application bundle. Tracked as new **REQ-FN-048**. ⚠ **The test that appears to guard this cannot fail**: `DataDirectoryTests.EveryArtefactSharesOneDirectory` constructs its own paths with `Path.Combine(dataDirectory, …)` then asserts `GetDirectoryName` equals `dataDirectory` — it observes no production call site and stayed green through both violations. The genuine guards (`MigratorDefaultMatchesTheResolvedAppDatabase`, `DefaultResolutionIsAbsoluteAndIndependentOfWorkingDirectory`) remain valid and green; what is missing is a guard over **real call sites**. Everything the row proved about the MAUI head's own resolution still holds — this demotion is about the invariant's SCOPE being overstated, not about the head being broken. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-034] ✅ **RESIDUAL CLOSED — the headline invariant is now TRUE.** `TechieDeskDb.Cli/Program.cs` passed Serilog the bare relative `logs/techiedeskdb-.log`, dropping log files in the REPO ROOT. Configuration is now built *before* the logger and the sink points at the new `DataDirectory.ResolveAndCreateLogDirectory`. Verified by running the real console from the repo root: **zero new files in `./logs`**, log written to `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk/logs/techiedeskdb-20260730.log`. Guarded by `WorkingDirectoryArtefactTests.MigrationConsoleLogsIntoTheDataDirectory`, which drives the real `Program.ConfigureLogging` from a sandbox CWD. The second falsifier (the vector database in the bundle root) is fixed under REQ-FN-048. ⚠ `DataDirectoryTests.EveryArtefactSharesOneDirectory` remains tautological and was left in place; the genuine coverage is now the new WorkingDirectoryArtefactTests guard. ⚠ Pre-existing `./logs/techiedeskdb-20260727.log` and `-20260728.log` remain in the repo root as evidence of the original defect — safe to delete. | [view](#d-req-fn-034) |
