| REQ-NFR-004 | Security: TLS, server-side tokens/authz, OWASP, hashed keys (BRD-95) | Implemented | 95% | 2026-07-20 NU1902 CLEARED — AngleSharp 0.17.1 was transitive (TrBlazeUI 1.0.7 → HtmlSanitizer 9.0.892, exact pin [0.17.1]); pinning HtmlSanitizer 9.1.949-beta resolves AngleSharp 1.5.1; build 0×NU1902, `dotnet list --vulnerable` clean. Fixed 2 REAL TLS holes not previously logged: AppManager:BaseUrl accepted ANY scheme (http would have sent X-Api-Key/X-Api-Secret + JWTs in cleartext) — now https-enforced at DI and boot, loopback http Dev-only; both Qdrant clients hardcoded `https:false` forcing cleartext even for https endpoints — now scheme-derived + plaintext-credential warning. Audited CLEAN with evidence: tokens server-side only (0 localStorage/sessionStorage/cookie writes), all SQL parameterized incl. the dynamic EventLog builder, 0 MarkupString/Html.Raw/innerHTML, dev cert flag already Development-gated + AppManager-client-scoped. +20 tests. ⚠ 2 OPEN OWNER DECISIONS: (1) techierag-config.json persists provider apiKeys in cleartext (outbound creds — hashing N/A, needs encryption-at-rest); (2) the pin ships TWO pre-release packages (HtmlSanitizer 9.1.949-beta + AngleSharp.Css 1.0.0-beta.216) — no stable HtmlSanitizer exists on AngleSharp ≥1.5.0; real fix is upstream (TR-009). **2026-07-25 — 004b CLOSED (owner approved encryption-at-rest):** Embedding/VectorStore/Llm/LlmFallback/Rerank ApiKey now stored as `enc:v1:<DataProtection payload>` via the new TechieRagConfigProtector (purpose TechieDesk.TechieRagConfig.ProviderApiKeys.v1); reversible by design because these are outbound creds the app must replay — hashing stays N/A. Legacy cleartext files load and are transparently re-saved encrypted; an undecryptable value is dropped in memory and the file left byte-identical. Key ring persisted to the volume-backed `data/keys` (Program.cs) — the ASP.NET default would be EPHEMERAL in a container and would have made every saved key unreadable after restart. Orchestrator live-verified the full round-trip: saved key absent from disk in cleartext (`enc:v1:CfDJ8Nsh…`), app restarted as a fresh process, value decrypted back byte-identical. 8 ConfigEncryption* tests. **004a REMAINS OPEN** (upstream-only, awaiting a stable HtmlSanitizer). ⚠ NEW residual: VectorStore/Persistence ConnectionStrings are still cleartext and a Postgres one contains `Password=…` — same mechanism would cover them; needs a follow-up REQ. ⚠ Threat-model limit: DataProtection keys sit unencrypted beside the data (no DPAPI/KMS on Linux) — key separation, not a KMS boundary · ⚠ **DEFECT FOUND 2026-07-28 while fixing the settings naming collision — two screens write the same config with no shared state (last-writer-wins).** `RagConfig.razor` (was `Settings.razor`) and `AdminSettings.razor` both inject the **same `TechieRagConfigService`** and both write **`config.Embedding` and `config.VectorStore`**; each loads its own copy on open (`AdminSettings.razor:243`) and saves the whole object back. So: open both screens, change the embedding provider on App Settings, then save RAG Configuration from its now-stale copy, and the first change is **silently reverted** — no conflict, no warning. Not introduced by the rename; the rename is what made the overlap visible, because the two screens finally have distinguishable names. Each still owns something unique — `RagConfig` alone writes `config.Processing` and `config.EnableTelemetry`, `AdminSettings` alone writes `config.Llm` — so neither is redundant and deletion is not the fix. **Suggested fix:** have the overlapping fields live in one place (App Settings' Defaults tab reads as the natural home) and have the other screen show them read-only with a link, or re-read before merge-on-save. Logged against REQ-NFR-004 as a data-integrity defect rather than a UI one. · ✅ **FIXED 2026-07-28 — the lost-update defect logged earlier today is closed, and it was WIDER than first reported.** The first pass found two screens sharing `config.Embedding` + `config.VectorStore`. Grepping every writer found **four**, and a **second** overlap nobody had noticed: `AdminSettings` and `LlmSettings` both write `config.Llm`. Writers and what each owns: **App Settings** (Embedding, VectorStore, Llm) · **RAG Configuration** (Embedding, VectorStore, Processing, Telemetry) · **LLM Settings** (Llm, LlmFallback, Prompt, Resilience, UsageTracking) · **the first-run wizard** (everything). Each loaded the whole document on open and saved the whole document back, so the later save silently reverted the earlier one — success toast, no warning, no log line. **Fix: section-scoped saves at the SERVICE, not per screen.** New `TechieRagConfigSections` flags enum (`TechieDesk.Core/Services/TechieRagConfigSections.cs`) plus `SaveConfigAsync(config, sections)`, which re-reads the current file and copies across only the caller's owned sections. Fixing it screen-by-screen would have left the next screen to reintroduce it — there were already four writers and two independent overlaps before anyone noticed. The re-read is deliberately from **disk**, not `cachedConfig`, because the cache holds what the last writer put there — precisely the stale value being guarded against. The wizard keeps `Sections.All`, which is correct: it establishes a whole configuration on an install that has none. **7 tests** (`tests/TechieDesk.Tests/Settings/ConfigSectionMergeTests.cs`), each using a SEPARATE service instance per screen so the shared in-memory cache cannot mask the staleness. ⚠ **The regression tests were mutation-proven, not assumed:** disabling the merge fails exactly the 3 lost-update tests and leaves the 4 guard tests passing. One of those guards asserts a **stated limit** — two screens editing the SAME section still resolve last-writer-wins, which is honest for a single-user desktop app; this narrows a lost update, it is not a transaction. Another guards against double-encrypting an API key that a save only passes through (disk-read reveals, write re-protects — if that symmetry broke, unowned keys would corrode on every unrelated save). Suite **1452/1452**. Was 70%; still `PARTIAL` for the unchanged open items — two pre-release packages pinned (TR-009, upstream) and the NU1904/NU1903 advisories. · 2026-07-29 [REQ-NFR-004] **NU1904 CRITICAL + ALL SEVEN NU1903 HIGHs CLEARED.** `dotnet list TechieRag.slnx package --vulnerable --include-transitive` returns 'no vulnerable packages' for **all nine projects** (was 25 warning lines across TechieDesk.Core, TechieDesk, TechieDeskScheduler) — independently re-confirmed by the orchestrator after the full rebuild. Diagnosis: `Microsoft.AspNetCore.DataProtection` 10.0.0 was DIRECT (GHSA-9mv3-2cwr-p262 / CVE-2026-40372, EoP) and `System.Security.Cryptography.Xml` 10.0.0 was TRANSITIVE THROUGH IT, so the whole cluster was fixable at one level with no transitive pin and no suppression. Advisory data pulled from the GitHub Advisory API, not guessed: Xml needs 10.0.6 for two advisories but **10.0.10 for five others**, so 10.0.10 is the lowest stable clearing all seven; DataProtection's own advisory is patched at 10.0.7, but 10.0.7's nuspec still pins a vulnerable Xml — hence 10.0.0 → **10.0.10**, not 10.0.7, is the minimal correct bump. **No prerelease, zero `NoWarn` on NU1903/NU1904, no `TreatWarningsAsErrors` escape hatch, no security weakened.** Collateral: `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.Abstractions` 10.0.0 → 10.0.10 (otherwise NU1605 warn-as-error in three projects). ⚠ Stays PARTIAL on two items that are owner/upstream calls, not build work: **(004a)** two pinned pre-release packages (`HtmlSanitizer` 9.1.949-beta + `AngleSharp.Css` 1.0.0-beta.216 — no stable HtmlSanitizer resolves AngleSharp ≥1.5.0; real fix upstream is TR-009); and **(residual)** cleartext VectorStore/Persistence `ConnectionStrings` in `techierag-config.json`, one carrying a pgvector `Password=`, which the app-side Postgres removal does NOT retire because pgvector belongs to the library — needs a follow-up REQ applying `TechieRagConfigProtector`. Threat-model limit unchanged: DataProtection keys sit unencrypted beside the data they protect (no DPAPI/KMS) — key separation, not a KMS boundary. Note 004b (provider apiKey encryption-at-rest) is CLOSED as of 2026-07-25 and should no longer be counted among the 'two open owner decisions'. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-NFR-004] ✅ **004a CLOSED — the last open item on this row.** The 2026-07-20 premise ("no stable HtmlSanitizer resolves AngleSharp ≥1.5.0") has EXPIRED. Queried live from the NuGet flat-container API, not from memory: **HtmlSanitizer 9.1.973** is stable and its nuspec declares **AngleSharp 1.6.0 + AngleSharp.Css 1.0.0** on all four TFM groups, both stable. Pin moved `9.1.949-beta` → `9.1.973` (`TechieDesk.csproj:132`). The product now ships **ZERO pre-release packages** — a sweep of every `project.assets.json` across all nine projects found no pre-release label anywhere. 🔴 **Trap recorded in the csproj comment:** `9.0.967` is ALSO stable but REVERTS to the vulnerable `AngleSharp [0.17.1]` exact pin, so the rule is `>= 9.1.973`, never "latest stable". `dotnet list TechieRag.slnx package --vulnerable --include-transitive` → "no vulnerable packages" for ALL NINE projects; no advisory has moved against us. Build 0 errors, 0×NU19xx, no NoWarn/suppression. **RUNTIME-SMOKED, because a green build proves nothing here:** TrBlazeUI.Components 1.0.7 was COMPILED against HtmlSanitizer 9.0.892/AngleSharp 0.17.1 and is now bound against 1.6.0 — a major jump whose failure mode is `TypeLoadException`/`MissingMethodException` at RUN time. A harness loading the real assemblies together showed TrBlazeUI's **744 types all load** with no sanitizer-related loader error, both `Ganss.Xss` member signatures resolve, and script/`onerror`/`javascript:`-URI stripping, 2 mXSS payloads and the AngleSharp.Css style path all pass. +2 regression tests (`Packaging/PrereleasePackageGuardTests.cs`) guarding both silent reversions. ⚠ Residual on this row is now only the REQ-NFR-012 item (cleartext VectorStore/Persistence connection strings); 004b closed 2026-07-25. Logged upstream as **TR-037** — still open there: TrBlazeUI 1.0.7 declares HtmlSanitizer 9.0.892 → AngleSharp [0.17.1] (exact pin), so every consumer needs a manual override. ⚠ **The "TR-009" this row previously cited is a PHANTOM — no such entry has ever existed in docs/TechieRag-TrBlazeUI-Feedback.md**, so the gap went formally unlogged 2026-07-20 → 2026-07-30. Re-cited as TR-037. | [view](#d-req-nfr-004) |
| REQ-NFR-005 | Accessibility WCAG 2.1 AA basics (BRD-96) | Implemented | 90% | 2026-07-20 axe (@axe-core/playwright, WCAG 2.0/2.1 A+AA) across all 20 shipped routes: **403 violation nodes → 60 (85% reduction)**; /login /register /forgot-password /reset-password fully clean. Fixed app-side: AA contrast 4.34:1→~4.9:1, 40 nested-interactive, 97 decorative icons aria-hidden, 19 missing h1 (which also repaired FocusOnNavigate — a no-op on 19/20 routes), AuthLayout landmarks, named inputs + icon-only buttons, skip link. Highest-value fix was NOT axe-detectable: keyboard focus was COMPLETELY INVISIBLE on the entire sidebar nav, sidebar toggle and user menu (outline:none + transparent ring, WCAG 2.4.7 AA) — found by tabbing the shell; now a 2px outline on every tab stop, test-asserted. ⚠ ~~60 nodes OPEN, ALL inside TrBlazeUI 1.0.7 and NOT app-fixable (TR-008): 6 critical unnameable Select triggers (role=combobox is nameFrom:author and SelectTrigger silently ignores AriaLabel), 8 critical Tabs aria-controls, FileUpload input label, Slider/Progress names. Do NOT mark Verified until a TrBlazeUI release lands.~~ 🔴 **THIS BLOCKER IS LARGELY FALSE — CORRECTED 2026-08-01 (see the amended TR-008 row in the Feedback tracker).** Most of those nodes were **app-fixable all along**, and the belief that they were not is why this row sat blocked on an upstream release that was never actually needed. `SelectTrigger`, `Progress` and `FieldLabel` all declare `AdditionalAttributes`, so a raw lowercase `aria-label` names them correctly — the original diagnosis confused the ATTRIBUTE with a PARAMETER (`AriaLabel="x"` is not a parameter on those components, so Blazor splatted it verbatim as the meaningless `arialabel="x"`, which is why it "compiled, ran and was never emitted"). All 9 app comboboxes have since been named this way. The 8 "critical Tabs aria-controls" nodes were a **harness artefact** — WebKit maps `role="tab"` to `XCUIElementTypeTab`, a type the 2026-07-28 probe never queried. **Genuinely still upstream-blocked and NOT app-fixable:** `Slider` and `FileUpload` (neither has `AriaLabel` nor `AdditionalAttributes`; wrapping either does not satisfy axe — tested), `CardTitle`/`AlertTitle` hardcoded heading levels with no splat (the whole of the 25 remaining `heading-order` nodes), `LucideIcon`'s unnamed `role="img"`, Pagination markup, and the sidebar focus rings. ⚠ So the correct gate is narrower than "wait for a TrBlazeUI release": **re-run axe and count what actually remains** before deciding this row's ceiling. tests/verify/req-nfr-005-007-a11y-browsers.spec.ts 13/13 · ⚠ **DevGuide 2026-07-28 — TR-008 RUNTIME-CONFIRMED, and it is worse than an axe count.** The `/admin/settings` **Branding tab could not be activated at all** through the accessibility tree: its `TabsTrigger` is exposed as neither Button, Link, RadioButton nor a focusable Other, so the tab — and the three REQs behind it (UI-037/038/039) — is unreachable by keyboard or assistive tech, not merely unlabelled. Previously logged as "8 critical Tabs aria-controls" from a static axe scan; this is the observed consequence. · 2026-07-29 [REQ-NFR-005] ⚠ **THE 2026-07-20 HARNESS NO LONGER REACHES THIS APP, and the '60 nodes, ALL library, NOT app-fixable' claim was WRONG** — both re-tested, not assumed. `tests/verify/req-nfr-005-007-a11y-browsers.spec.ts` drives a browser at `localhost:5131`; REQ-FN-035 deleted `Program.cs`, so there is no Kestrel, no `/_framework` and no CDP endpoint, and Appium mac2 (verified live) exposes only the native XCUIElement tree — neither can host axe-core. The sweep therefore moved **IN-PROCESS**: `apps/TechieDesk/A11yScanRunner.cs` (Debug-only, `TECHIEDESK_A11Y_SCAN=1`) evals the same axe-core 4.12.1 bundle into the live WKWebView and walks **all 30 shipped routes** (was 20; `/settings` and `/tool-demo` gone, `/rag-config` `/admin/settings` `/admin/events` `/automations` `/billing` `/support` `/workspace/*/agents` + connector routes new) — measuring the **shipping renderer, not Chromium**, so it is stronger evidence than the run it replaces. **Result: 135 violation nodes → 28 (79%); WCAG 2.0/2.1 A+AA specifically 78 → 3 (96%).** Triage: **51 of 135 were APP-side (the 'all library' claim is false)** and 49 are fixed — 🔴 `#blazor-error-ui` had NO css rule at all in `index.html`, so Blazor's red 'unhandled error' bar rendered **permanently visible on every screen** and put content outside every landmark (`region` ×30); 9 unnamed SelectTrigger/Switch; 3 `Header=""` DataTable action columns; 2 unnamed Progress; 1 culture-formatted `aria-valuenow`. 84 were library-internal, **58 with an app-side workaround**: `TabsContent ForceMount="true"` repairs 13 dangling `aria-controls` with zero visual change (screenshot-confirmed), and `wwwroot/js/a11y.js` marks 33 nameless LucideIcons `aria-hidden` and repairs Pagination's malformed `<ul>` + unnamed rows-per-page combobox. **TR-008 CORRECTED** by reflection over the 1.0.7 assemblies + a purpose-built axe probe app: `SelectTrigger` DOES splat `aria-label` onto its `<button role=combobox>` (the 6 'unnameable' triggers were app-fixable all along — `AriaLabel` is not a parameter, so it was emitted as `arialabel=`), and `FieldLabel` DOES have a `For` parameter. Confirmed genuinely unfixable: `Slider` and `FileUpload` have neither splat nor AriaLabel (wrapping in `role=group` / `<label>` tested, does not work). Two harness bugs found and fixed while building the runner: `BlazorWebView.TryDispatchAsync` takes an `Action` not a `Func<Task>`, so an async lambda bound as `async void` and every result and exception was lost; and `OnHandlerChanged` fires more than once, so two sweeps ran concurrently and cross-contaminated route results. Old Playwright suite kept but `test.skip`'d with the reason rather than deleted. **Remaining 28 = 25 best-practice `heading-order`** (CardTitle hardcodes `<h3>` ×14, AlertTitle `<h5>` ×11; AlertTitle has no splat so a correct outline is genuinely unreachable — NOT a WCAG 2.1 AA success criterion) **+ 3 now resolved by the orchestrator**: the 2 `TabsContent` in AdminSettings got `ForceMount="true"`; the third (`aria-allowed-attr` on `#defaults-upload`) is 🔴 **re-triaged as library-internal, NOT the app one-liner it was handed over as** — `TrBlazeUI.Components.NumericInput<T>` has **no `AdditionalAttributes`** (reflection-verified: `Value, ValueChanged, Min, Max, Step, DecimalPlaces, AllowNegative, ShowButtons, Placeholder, Disabled, Required, Class, Id, AriaLabel, AriaDescribedBy, AriaInvalid, Format`), so splatting `role="spinbutton"` would throw `ThrowForUnknownIncomingParameterName` and dead-circuit the screen. Logged as TR-034. · 2026-07-29 verifier: ⚠ CLUSTER DISAGREEMENT, recorded rather than silently resolved. The FN/NFR verifier graded this NOT-OBSERVABLE, reasoning that axe cannot be injected through Appium mac2 (true — it returns an XCUIElement tree, not a DOM) and there is no HTTP surface for the old Playwright harness (also true). That verdict rests on INCOMPLETE information: the build phase had already BUILT an in-process runner, apps/TechieDesk/A11yScanRunner.cs, which evals axe-core 4.12.1 straight into the live WKWebView and swept all 30 routes — 135 violation nodes to 28, and WCAG 2.0/2.1 A+AA specifically 78 to 3. The capability therefore exists and the measurement is real. Held at `Implemented` and NOT promoted, because that sweep ran during build-phase self-smoke, not during this verify pass, and a builder's own observation cannot produce `Verified`. To close: re-run A11yScanRunner inside a verify pass. Remaining 28 nodes are 25 best-practice `heading-order` (library-blocked, TR-008: CardTitle hardcodes h3, AlertTitle h5 with no attribute splat) plus the 3 in AdminSettings — of which 2 were fixed by the orchestrator (TabsContent ForceMount, live-confirmed no visual regression this run) and 1 is re-triaged as library-internal (TR-034). | [view](#d-req-nfr-005) |

| REQ-FN-035 | MAUI Blazor Hybrid desktop head, macOS + Windows (BRD-128, P1) | Blocked | 70% | [REQ-FN-035] 2026-07-26 — **Mac Catalyst head BUILDS AND RUNS; Windows head is code-complete but UNBUILT on this host.** PARTIAL is deliberate: BRD-128 requires both platforms and only one was proven. `apps/TechieDesk` is now `Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Razor` + `UseMaui`, TFM `net10.0-maccatalyst` (+ `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` added conditionally when building **on** Windows — MAUI cannot build a Windows head from macOS). `Program.cs` → `MauiProgram.cs`; `Components/App.razor` → `wwwroot/index.html` + `MainPage.xaml` mounting `Routes` as the BlazorWebView root component. Screenshot: `test-results/screens-req-fn-035/`. Build 0-err, **387/387** tests. ⚠ **13 tests retired, none lost:** 12 in `LoginEndpointTests` (the HTTP `/auth/login` endpoint) + 1 cookie-principal test — excluded via csproj alongside their subject so REQ-FN-041 deletes both together. ⚠ **ONNX/Mac Catalyst gap found and fixed:** `Microsoft.ML.OnnxRuntime` 1.27.0 ships an EMPTY `_._` placeholder for maccatalyst, so it links no native library while its managed assembly still P/Invokes `RegisterCustomOps` — a symbol defined in NO shipped ORT binary (verified with `nm`). Fixed by declaring the `NativeReference` ORT omits (its xcframework *does* carry a 91 MB Catalyst slice) + a build-time-compiled C stub for Debug only, where the disabled linker forces every P/Invoke. Release trims it and needs neither. Without this the bundled BGE-M3 offline embedder would not ship on macOS. ⚠ Data currently resolves **inside the .app bundle** (`Contents/MonoBundle/data`) — wrong for a signed install; REQ-FN-037 moves it to the per-user OS directory and is the immediate next REQ. **2026-07-26 CLOSED by REQ-FN-037** — data and logs both resolve under `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk` and the in-bundle `data/` was relocated once, byte-identical, then removed. Run 2 emitted **no** relocation line (moves once) and opened the same directory. Window renders the TrBlazeUI sidebar, all 5 home cards, `Admin` badge and the **`Default` workspace as data**; **0 ERR/FTL/exceptions** in the log. **14 `DataDirectoryTests`** (7 carried forward + 7 new: both platform shapes, XDG, per-user-not-app-folder, legacy-root naming, relocation-loses-nothing incl. nested key ring, live-file protection, inert re-run). RED-checked: reverting the root to app-relative fails `DefaultResolutionIsThePerUserDirectoryAndNotTheApplicationFolder`; stubbing out the relocation fails `LegacyDataDirectoryRelocatesLosingNothing` + `LegacyDataDirectoryRelocationNeverOverwritesLiveFiles`. | REQ-FN-038 | Signed desktop packaging + update check (BRD-131, P1) | Blocked | 70% | Added 2026-07-26. **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-07-27 (owner challenge):** the previous remark — *"an external dependency, not a code task"* — was wrong and let this row be parked. Only the signing **identities** need the owner. Split into **038a** (CI/CD build+package workflow), **038b** (in-app update check), **038c** (sign + notarize — genuinely owner-blocked). **[REQ-FN-038a] 2026-07-27 — IMPLEMENTED + self-smoked end-to-end; and it found a crash that every previous smoke missed.** New `.github/workflows/publish-desktop.yml` (5 jobs) + `.github/packaging/macos-INSTALL.txt` + `windows-INSTALL.txt`. **The artefact was proven the whole way: publish → DMG → mount → install from the DMG → launch that installed copy → workspace shell renders** (`Default`, five home cards, `Admin` badge, native menu bar incl. the REQ-UI-041 `Go` menu; screenshot read). 417 MB `.app` → **73 MB DMG**; universal **x86_64 + arm64**, so one artefact covers Intel and Apple Silicon; ad-hoc signature survives the DMG round-trip (`codesign -v` → *valid on disk*, *satisfies its Designated Requirement*). **⚠ DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED — the first Release build was a well-formed bundle that could not start.** It aborted with SIGABRT before writing one log line: `Failed to load AOT module 'Docker.DotNet' while running in aot-only mode because a dependency cannot be found or it is out of date` (message reaches os_log only — captured with `log stream`). Cause: Release trims + AOT-compiles, Debug does neither, so **every prior smoke of this app was a Debug smoke and this entire failure class was unobserved**. Both Docker.DotNet.dll and Newtonsoft.Json.dll were present, so it was the *out of date* half — full trimming rewrites Newtonsoft.Json and Docker.DotNet's AOT image no longer matches. Fixed with `<MtouchInterpreter>Docker.DotNet,Newtonsoft.Json</MtouchInterpreter>` (Release + Catalyst only). **`TrimMode=partial` was rejected, not overlooked** — it would stop trimming Microsoft.ML.OnnxRuntime, and this project *depends* on full trimming there to drop the unused `RegisterCustomOps` P/Invoke, so it would have traded a startup crash for a native link failure. **No signing identity used or needed.** macOS ships ad-hoc-signed (the toolchain default); Windows ships **unpackaged self-contained + zip, deliberately not MSIX** — an unsigned MSIX cannot be installed without the user first trusting a certificate, an unpackaged build just runs. Both signing paths are gated on a secret and **fail loudly if the secret is set**, so the workflow can never emit an ad-hoc artefact labelled as signed. TrBlazeUI restores in CI via `GITHUB_TOKEN` against `github.repository_owner` — the gitignored PAT never enters CI. Test gate runs on cheap `ubuntu-latest` because `TechieDesk.Tests` → `TechieDesk.Core` only (no TrBlazeUI/MAUI), a direct dividend of the REQ-FN-035 Core extraction. Build 0-err; **404/404** Release tests (320 app + 84 lib). ⚠ **The Windows job has never executed** — see 038b/c and the honesty note in detail. **[REQ-FN-038b] 2026-07-27 — IMPLEMENTED + self-smoked; and it caught a bug that would have silently disabled the whole feature.** New `TechieDesk.Core/Services/Updates/` (12 files: `ReleaseVersion`, `AvailableRelease`, `ReleaseAsset`, `IAppVersionProvider` + `AssemblyAppVersionProvider`, `IUpdateFeed` + `GitHubReleaseFeed`, `UpdateCheckResult`, `UpdateOptions`, `UpdatePreferences` + store, `IUpdateService` + `UpdateService`, `UpdateLaunchState`, DI extensions), the platform half `apps/TechieDesk/Services/Updates/MauiAppVersionProvider.cs`, and the surface `Components/Pages/AppUpdates.razor` at `/settings/updates` + a sidebar entry + a **Help › Check for Updates…** native menu item. **⚠ DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED — the feed would never have returned a single release.** The DTOs relied on `JsonSerializerDefaults.Web`'s camelCase policy, but the API sends **snake_case** (`tag_name`, `browser_download_url`, `published_at`, `html_url`). Every property would have bound to null → every release failed its tag check → empty list → **"you are up to date", forever, with no exception and no log line**. The first test fixtures were written in camelCase too, so *the tests passed against the bug* — it was caught by re-reading the contract and confirmed with a live `curl` before being fixed with explicit `[JsonPropertyName]`. Now pinned by `BindsTheSnakeCaseFieldNamesTheApiActuallySends`; re-mutating one attribute reddens **8** tests where previously **0** would have. **LIVE-PROVEN against the real API** with a scratch harness driving the real `GitHubReleaseFeed`: 5 live releases parsed, `tag_name`/`html_url`/`published_at`/asset name/`browser_download_url`/size all bound, and the `desktop-v` prefix filter correctly returned 0 for a repo that publishes `v*` tags. **⚠ DELIBERATE POLICY CALL — the automatic launch check DEFAULTS TO OFF.** The pre-existing `OutboundEgressTests` guard failed the build on the two new `HttpClient` consumers, which is exactly its purpose; REQ-NFR-008/BRD-99 say nothing leaves the instance except operator-configured providers and AppManager, and an update check contacts a host the operator never configured. Rather than allowlist and move on, auto-check is opt-in, the manual button is always available, no credential is ever sent, and a new test `TheUpdateCheckDoesNotRunAtLaunchByDefault` pins it. Flipping that default needs REQ-NFR-008 revisited. **Installing is deliberately NOT implemented** — unsigned packages cannot be cryptographically attributed, so silently replacing the running binary would be an unauthenticated code-execution path; the UI design's third switch renders **disabled with that reason** rather than inert or omitted, and becomes defensible once 038c signs. Downloads land in `{dataDirectory}/downloads` via a new `DataDirectory.DownloadDirectoryName` constant — the single-authority rule from REQ-FN-034/037, and the test `DownloadsIntoTheDataDirectoryNotTheApplication` pins that nothing is written beside the executable (which on macOS is the read-only bundle). Remote asset names are `Path.GetFileName`-collapsed so a feed-supplied `../../` cannot choose where the file lands. 56 new tests, **7 behaviour-removal mutations each proven RED** (prerelease-ordering inverted, tag-prefix filter removed, auto-check default flipped, feed failure downgraded to UpToDate, download directory moved to the app, traversal guard removed, truncation check removed). Live smoke: page renders with real values — **Version 1.0** read from the platform (proving `MauiAppVersionProvider`, not the assembly fallback), "Not checked yet", all three switches, and the data-safety Alert showing the real path; 0 `[ERR]`/`[FTL]`. ⚠ **NOT DRIVEN — no UI automation:** "Check for updates" was never clicked (`osascript` assistive access denied, -1719), so the page was rendered via a **temporary** `@page "/"` swap, screenshotted, and reverted — both files MD5-verified identical to their pre-swap state. Build 0-err; **460/460** tests (84 lib + 376 app). Pending verifier · 038c Not Started · 2026-07-29 verifier: 038b is WELL COVERED — 52 update tests green (UpdateServiceTests 14/14, ReleaseFeedTests 14/14, ReleaseVersionTests 20 cases, UpdatePreferencesTests 5/5) plus OutboundEgressTests.TheUpdateCheckDoesNotRunAtLaunchByDefault proving the opt-in egress default; the Updates tab was additionally observed rendering live this run. 038a remains NEVER EXECUTED (running publish-desktop.yml needs a push, which is manual here) and its `windows` job is additionally doomed by the missing Platforms/Windows sources — see REQ-FN-035. 038c is owner-blocked on the signing identity, the SAME decision that gates REQ-FN-043 and therefore REQ-FN-039 and REQ-FN-032 clause 4. | [view](#d-req-fn-038) |
| REQ-FN-039 | Tokens + provider secrets in the OS credential store (BRD-132, P1) | Blocked | 75% | **[REQ-FN-039] 2026-07-26 — IMPLEMENTED + self-smoked; and sign-in, which was INERT, now actually works.** Two halves of one vertical slice. (1) **OS credential store.** New `ISecretStore` in `TechieDesk.Core` (`Read`/`Write`/`Delete` + an honest `IsDurable`) with the platform half, `OsCredentialStore`, in the MAUI head over `Microsoft.Maui.Storage.SecureStorage` — Keychain on macOS/Catalyst, DPAPI-backed Credential Manager on Windows — registered in `MauiProgram.cs` **before** `AddTechieDeskDesktopAuth`, whose `TryAdd` fallback (`EphemeralSecretStore`, in-memory, never a file) serves only hosts with no platform, i.e. the test project. Core takes **no** MAUI dependency, so `tests/TechieDesk.Tests` still references it under a plain `dotnet test`. `SessionStore` now mirrors the ONE live session into that store and reads it back through the new `ISessionStore.RestorePersistedSession()`; `DesktopSessionContext` calls it lazily on first touch, so restore happens whoever asks first and nothing has to remember a startup hook. A new `SessionTokenStore.Changed` event keeps the stored copy in step with a **silent token refresh** — without it "a restart restores the session" would have held only until the first token rotation. (2) **Provider API keys (supersedes REQ-NFR-004b for the desktop head).** `TechieRagConfigProtector` gained an `enc:v2:<field>` scheme: with a durable store the key goes to Keychain and `techierag-config.json` receives an opaque reference — **no ciphertext on disk at all**, so no key ring is needed to read it back. `enc:v1:` Data Protection is kept as the LEGACY read path and as the fallback when no durable store exists (an in-memory store would make a reference unresolvable after a restart and lose an operator's key — so the fallback is a deliberate check of `IsDurable`, not best-effort). Existing `enc:v1:` and cleartext files keep working and are flagged for rewrite, so an install migrates up on its next save. **`ISessionStore` was NOT deleted** — it still owns sliding + hard expiry, handle rotation on sign-in, and "log out — all devices" (REQ-UI-008); only its persistence changed. All-devices logout now also drops a copy persisted by an EARLIER run, which has no in-memory entry to match on. ⚠ **Deliberate policy call:** a restored session is bounded by the **hard** deadline only (carried across verbatim, so a restart can never renew it); the sliding idle window is not applied to a closed app, because enforcing it would mean an app shut overnight always demanded a fresh sign-in — the exact clause this REQ asks for. Idle timeout still bites inside a running app (test). **Sign-in de-inerted:** `Login.razor`/`Register.razor` posted HTML forms to `/auth/login` and `/auth/register`, endpoints that died with the web host in REQ-FN-035 — submitting did literally nothing. Both now call the new in-process `IDesktopSignInService`, which carries over everything from the retired `SessionEndpoints` that was not about HTTP (field validation, `PasswordPolicy`, the AppManager call, **handle rotation before the new session is minted**, and the same error-code vocabulary the banners switch on) and drops what was (antiforgery — no cross-site request exists on a desktop; the `?error=` redirect round-trip; `NavigateTo(forceLoad: true)`, which on a WebView restarts the whole app). **LIVE-PROVEN (Mac Catalyst `.app` launched twice, both screenshots read):** the window opens straight into the workspace shell — `Default` workspace, five home cards, `Admin` badge, `Sign in` under Account — and `/login` renders the **new interactive form** completely (logo, "Welcome back", the no-licence-server info banner, both bound fields, an enabled `Sign in` button, "Continue without an account →"), with **0 `[ERR]`/`[FTL]` lines** in `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk/logs/`. ⚠ **HONEST GAP — Keychain is REFUSED on this unsigned build, and that is now visible instead of silent.** The probe logs `The OS credential store rejected this process (Exception): Error adding record: MissingEntitlement` (errSecMissingEntitlement / -34018) and degrades to **memory-only for the run — never to a file**, so confidentiality holds and only persistence is lost. I tried the fix: adding `Platforms/MacCatalyst/Entitlements.plist` with `keychain-access-groups` + `<CodesignEntitlements>` builds and the entitlement IS signed in (`codesign -d --entitlements -` confirms it), but the app then **fails to launch** (`RBSRequestErrorDomain Code=5 / Launchd job spawn failed`, build warning MT7139) because an ad-hoc signature cannot carry that entitlement without a provisioning profile. **Reverted in full** — it is blocked on REQ-FN-038's Apple Developer identity, not on code. ⚠ **NOT DRIVEN — no AppManager on this host:** a real end-to-end sign-in and the "a restart restores the session" clause could NOT be exercised live. Both are covered by tests only (round-trip through the store, restart-restores, fresh handle on restore, hard cap not renewable, silent-refresh re-persist, all-devices revoke, unreadable payload discarded). No fake login was staged. ⚠ **NOT DRIVEN — no UI automation:** the Sign in button was not clicked. `osascript` assistive access is denied here (-25211) and no Appium harness exists, so `/login` was rendered by a **temporary** `@page "/"` swap (Home.razor + Login.razor), screenshotted, and reverted byte-for-byte; the working tree carries neither edit. ⚠ Keychain is also unexercised for the `enc:v2:` provider-key path for the same entitlement reason — asserted against a durable fake. Build 0-err (`TechieRag.slnx` + `-f net10.0-maccatalyst`); **443/443** tests (84 lib + 359 app; 359 includes the concurrent REQ-FN-042 storage additions). 22 new tests, proven RED by six separate behaviour-removal mutations (persistence removed → 8 red; restart renews the hard cap → 1; rotation dropped from sign-in → 1; all-devices leaves the stored copy → 1; OS store never used for provider keys → 4; a file-backed session cache added → 1). 2 existing `SessionContinuityTests` were rewritten, not weakened: their premise "a fresh scope holds nothing" is exactly what this REQ reverses, so adoption is now asserted the stronger way — two live sessions, only one named by the principal. Pending verifier · Added 2026-07-26. · 2026-07-29 verifier: the store abstraction is thoroughly tested — SecretStoreSessionTests 18/18 (TokensRoundTripThroughTheSecretStore, ARestartRestoresTheSessionWithoutReEntry, TokensAreAbsentFromEveryPlainFileOnDisk, ARestartCannotRenewTheHardLifetime, AllDevicesLogoutRevokesTheStoredSessionToo) and ProviderSecretStoreTests 6/6 (incl. ProviderKeysGoToTheOsStoreAndLeaveNoCiphertextBehind). 🔴 But the HEADLINE acceptance — 'stored via Keychain, a restart restores the session without re-entry' — is UNMET ON EVERY REAL BUILD: all 7 launches today log OsCredentialStore MissingEntitlement and fall back to EphemeralSecretStore, so the Keychain is never actually used. Blocked on REQ-FN-043 / REQ-FN-038c (owner signing-identity decision); OsCredentialStore itself needs no change once signed. Security property NOT regressed — the fallback holds secrets in memory only, never a file. | [view](#d-req-fn-039) |
| REQ-FN-040 | Docker daemon client drives a configurable endpoint (BRD-134, P1) | Implemented | 75% | **2026-07-26 [REQ-FN-040] IMPLEMENTED + self-smoked.** `DockerContainerService` now targets a configured endpoint instead of a hard-coded local socket: new `DockerDaemonEndpoint` (kind parsing for `unix://`/`npipe://` = local socket, `tcp://`/`http://` = LAN plain TCP, `tcps://`/`https://` = remote TLS, bare `host:port` = plain TCP) + `DockerDaemonSettingsStore` (persisted in `InstanceSetting` under `docker.daemon.endpoint`/`docker.daemon.verifyTls`, defaulting to the `Docker:` config section). Lifecycle extended to status/start/stop/**restart**/pull/**logs** against whichever daemon is connected, and every failure is reported through `DockerDaemonTestResult` naming the endpoint actually tried. **Security:** `VerifyTls` defaults to true everywhere; TLS is real (the client URI becomes `https://`, which is the only scheme Docker.DotNet keeps encrypted); disabling verification and any plain-TCP endpoint both raise a non-null `SecurityWarning`; the verification opt-out fails CLOSED if it cannot be applied. Client certs / endpoint credentials sit behind the same `IDockerDaemonSettingsStore` abstraction as a clearly-marked REQ-FN-039 seam — no second secret store invented. 31 unit tests (kind parsing, TLS-on-by-default, plain-TCP warning, honest failure) proven RED by four separate behaviour-removal mutations. ⚠ **Exercised against a real daemon: NONE.** There is no Docker daemon on this host, so start/stop/restart/pull/logs/create/remove are compile-and-unit-verified only. What WAS driven live on `/qdrant-admin`: endpoint selection, apply+persist, connection test, and the honest-failure path (`Connection refused by tcp://127.0.0.1:2377 — nothing is listening there.` and `The Docker socket /var/run/docker.sock is not present — Docker is not running on this machine.`). ⚠ `IDockerDaemonSettingsStore` is composed inside the service rather than DI-registered (Program.cs is owned by another agent this wave) · Added 2026-07-26 per owner decision — F-QADMIN is explicitly **retained**, not retired. `DockerContainerService` must target a configured endpoint (local socket, LAN, or remote TCP/TLS with cert verification / client certs) instead of assuming a local daemon, and expose the full Qdrant lifecycle (status/start/stop/restart/pull/logs) against whichever daemon is connected. ⚠ Remote daemon access is a privileged capability — endpoint credentials belong in the OS credential store (REQ-FN-039), and a plain unauthenticated TCP endpoint should warn · 2026-07-30 [*verify all] ✅ **runtime render+visual-confirmed** on the live Catalyst head via the project harness `tests/appium/` — screen swept at **1600x1240 AND the enforced 1024x720 floor**, arrival gated on the app's own breadcrumb (21/21 screens arrived, 0 mis-navigated), content tree **settled** before reading, screenshot window-cropped and inspected. §4a: controls render their data. §4b: no zero-size, no true overlap, no off-canvas content. MAUI gaps worked around: `Page.MenuBarItems` is not surfaced on Mac Catalyst, `Window.MinimumWidth/Height` does not reach a Catalyst window, and `Microsoft.Maui.Storage` ships no `FolderPicker`. · Added 2026-07-26. ~~Needs mockups — no screen exists for the data/storage settings surface~~ — stale, `docs/mockups/data-storage.html` exists and was the build contract ⚠ **DEMOTED 2026-07-28 (`*verify all`, live) — the 1024 × 720 minimum window is NOT enforced on the running Catalyst head.** Measured by resizing the bound window through the accessibility API and reading the size back: requested 300 → **actual 515**; 600 → 600; 800 → 800; 1000 → 1000; height requested 200 → **actual 319**. So the real floor is **515 × 319**, roughly half the BRD-133 contract, and a user can drag the window far below the size the shell is designed for. `App.xaml.cs` declares `MinimumWindowWidth = 1024` / `MinimumWindowHeight = 720` and `MainPage.ApplyMinimumWindowSize()` multiplies them by `CatalystPointsPerMacPoint = 1.3` into `UIWindowScene.SizeRestrictions.MinimumSize` — the code is present, but the restriction is not holding at runtime (either the scene has no `SizeRestrictions` when it runs, or it is applied before the scene connects and is then overwritten). The in-code comment “Measured on this host: a 1331 × 936 UIKit-point floor holds the macOS window at 1026 × 768” **no longer reproduces**. The menu bar and file-picker halves of this REQ were not re-exercised this run. Was `Implemented 75%`. · ✅ **FIXED + runtime-proven 2026-07-28.** The floor is now exactly **1024 × 720**: asking the live window for 300×250 or 900×600 clamps to 1024×720, while 1400×950 is honoured. Measured on the **Release** build after the fix (before: 515×319). **Two distinct bugs, one real cause.** **(1)** `MainPage.ApplyMinimumWindowSize()` ran from `OnHandlerChanged`, which on Catalyst fires BEFORE the `UIWindowScene` connects — the loop found no scene, set nothing and returned silently, so the window carried no floor at all. **(2)** More fundamentally, that whole approach was dead code: MAUI applies its own value from `Window.MinimumWidth/MinimumHeight` afterwards and wins. Proved by A/B — deleting the scene-restriction code entirely leaves the floor at 1024 × 720. So the fix is one constant, not a lifecycle dance: `App.CreateWindow` now pre-scales the BRD-133 floor by `CatalystMinimumSizeCorrection = 1/0.77`, because Catalyst draws the iPad idiom at 77% and MAUI passes the value through without undoing that (uncorrected, 1024×720 landed as 789×555 — exactly 0.77 on both axes, 789/1024 = 0.7705, 555/720 = 0.7708). `MainPage`'s `ApplyMinimumWindowSize`, its retry constants and the `CatalystPointsPerMacPoint` factor are **deleted**; the Windows head is unaffected (correction is 1.0 off Catalyst). The in-code claim that a 1331×936 restriction 'holds the macOS window at 1026×768' never reproduced and is gone. ⚠ Held at `Implemented`, not `Verified`: only the **window** half of this REQ was re-driven — the native menu bar and the OS file pickers were not re-exercised this run. | REQ-NFR-011 | Runtime UI verification harness for the MAUI head | Verified | 100% | ⚠ **RE-TESTED END-TO-END 2026-07-28 by `*refresh-status` — the recorded blocker overstated the problem and is corrected here.** Probed the whole chain rather than trusting the note: `appium` + `mac2@4.0.4` installed ✅; server boots on 4723 and returns `{"ready":true}` ✅; **WebDriverAgentMac now BUILDS clean** (`xcodebuild build-for-testing` → `** TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, exit 0) ✅. The previously recorded "cannot start a session / exit 65" was **an unbuilt WDA helper**, not an authorization denial — Appium suppresses WDA's xcodebuild output (`showServerLogs` off), so the first-run build failure surfaced only as a proxy crash. That build is now done and that failure is gone. **The one remaining wall is the run step:** `xcodebuild test-without-building` reaches `Running tests...` then fails after 60s with `Failed to initialize for UI testing: Error Domain=com.apple.dt.XCTest.XCTFuture Code=1000 "Timed out while enabling automation mode."` ⚠ **Do not record this as "permission not granted"** — the owner reports Accessibility was granted 2026-07-27, and that is consistent with what was observed: macOS TCC grants are **per-binary**, and the process driving this attempt is `claude`, not Terminal or Xcode, so a real grant need not cover it; a non-interactive context also cannot answer an authorization dialog. **Owner action = one interactive run** from `~/.appium/node_modules/appium-mac2-driver/WebDriverAgentMac`: `xcodebuild test-without-building -project WebDriverAgentMac.xcodeproj -scheme WebDriverAgentRunner` — reaching `ServerURLHere` proves the driver works. ⚠ **Second, independent gap:** `runtimeVerification` is **entirely commented out** in `.tfcore/core-config.yaml`, so even with a working driver there is **no `maccatalyst` endpoint registered** and any UI verify degrades to `⚠ STATIC-ONLY` for a pure config reason — uncomment `appium.maccatalyst.url` before the first UI verify. **This gates UI verification only; `*verify functional` is not blocked.** Prior history follows — [REQ-NFR-011] 2026-07-26 — **driver side CLEARED, one owner action outstanding.** `appium-mac2-driver` was pinned at 2.2.2 (peer `appium ^2.4.1`) against installed Appium 3.5.2 and would not load; upgraded to **4.0.4** (peer `^3.0.0-rc.2`). Appium 3.5.2 + Mac2Driver 4.0.4 now boot clean and report `ready` — "Mac2Driver has been successfully loaded". Xcode 26.6 present, so WebDriverAgentMac can build. ⚠ **BLOCKED ON AN OWNER ACTION I CANNOT PERFORM:** creating a Mac2 session fails because macOS raises `XCTest is trying to Enable UI Automation` and asks for the user's login password — captured in the REQ-FN-035 smoke screenshot. Until that one-time authorization is granted, the driver process dies on session start and the verifier cannot drive the desktop head. Remaining after that: register the `runtimeVerification.appium.maccatalyst` endpoint in `.tfcore/core-config.yaml` and wire screenshots + element tree into the §4a data-render and §4b visual-truth gates. ⚠ The Windows half (FlaUI / Appium-Windows) is untouched and not buildable from this host. Original note follows — Added 2026-07-26 — **infrastructure dependency of every future `*verify`**, not a product feature. The Playwright gates that produced most of this checklist's runtime evidence drive a browser over `localhost` and cannot drive a MAUI Hybrid app. Stand up the `runtimeVerification` endpoints already stubbed in `.tfcore/core-config.yaml`: Appium `mac2` for Mac Catalyst, FlaUI or Appium-Windows for Windows, feeding the same screenshots + element tree into the data-render and visual-truth gates. ⚠ Until this exists the verifier degrades every UI requirement to `⚠ STATIC-ONLY`; a static pass must never be recorded as a runtime pass. ⚠ The currently-`Verified` UI rows were proven against the Blazor head and are re-verification debt once the head changes — they are NOT carried over as passes ✅ **Verified 2026-07-28 (`*verify all`) — the harness is proven end-to-end, by being used for a full run rather than probed.** Appium 3.5.2 + `mac2` with WebDriverAgentMac on `localhost:10100`, session bound by bundleId, driving the live Catalyst window: **18/18 nav destinations** swept at **three widths (1800 / 900 / 515 px)**, each yielding an XCUIElement tree (96–313 content nodes, 21–85 page-specific strings) and a full-window PNG — i.e. both the §4a data-render gate and the §4b visual-truth gate now run against this head with real evidence. Element-scoped clicks only; no global input injection. **Two operational gotchas, both now reproducible:** WDA must be (re)started immediately before each session — a stale WDA returns `Failed to launch '<bundleId>'` even when the app is already running; and duplicate `.app` bundles sharing the bundleId make LaunchServices pick an arbitrary one (it silently chose `bin/Debug/…` over the registered Release path), so strays must be `lsregister -u`'d first. **Harness correctness was itself gated:** four systematic false positives were found and eliminated before any verdict was recorded — duplicated AXWebArea nodes read as control OVERLAP; the always-present hidden `#blazor-error-ui` div read as off-viewport (the 16/16 false positive flagged on the previous run); the WCAG skip-nav link, off-canvas by design; and below-the-fold content on long pages read as off-viewport, now that check is horizontal-axis only. Harness: `.verify/drive.py`, `.verify/sweep.py`, `.verify/navsweep.py`; evidence `.verify/navsweep*.json`, `.verify/shots/`. Was `In Progress 40%`. · ⚠ **FRAGILITY OBSERVED 2026-07-28 (same day as the `Verified` above, recorded rather than left clean).** The harness worked repeatedly this session — that evidence stands — but later the same day WebDriverAgentMac stopped initialising and would not recover: `Failed to initialize for UI testing … "Timed out while enabling automation mode."` on four consecutive attempts, including after killing Appium, `xcodebuild` and WDA and restarting cleanly. A wedged `/usr/libexec/testmanagerd` (from an earlier session) survives a non-privileged `kill` and is the likely cause; clearing it needs a privileged kill or a logout. **Practical consequence: the UI harness is not reliably available on demand**, so a build phase cannot assume it can self-smoke the UI. Not demoting the row — the capability is real and was exercised end-to-end — but it should be read as "works, and needs a host reset when automation mode wedges", not "always available". · ⚠ **HARNESS DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-28 (DevGuide OBSERVE pass) — it had produced FALSE PASSES in this session's earlier sweeps.** The nav helper clicked `els[0]` of a label match, but each sidebar link is duplicated in the XCUIElement tree and the first match is not always the live anchor — so two clicks silently did nothing and the sweep then captured the PREVIOUS screen and scored it OK. Caught because **Automations and Updates returned byte-identical node and text counts to App Settings and Data & Storage**; their captured text was those screens' content. Fixed by clicking `els[-1]` (the innermost anchor) **and asserting the expected heading appears before trusting the capture**. Re-run: Automations 145 nodes/16 strings and Updates 158/21 — distinct, and genuinely rendering. ⚠ **Consequence for the audit trail: the earlier "18/18 screens clean" results in today's `*verify all` and post-fix re-verify rows overstated coverage by 2 screens** — 16 were genuinely observed, 2 were the previous screen re-captured. Both are confirmed good now, so no verdict changes, but the counts were wrong and a nav-click that silently no-ops is exactly the failure a render gate must not absorb. · 2026-07-29 verifier: the harness is graded by whether it can actually drive the head, and THIS RUN IS THAT EVIDENCE: a full 28-route sweep at two widths, live control interaction (tab activation, dropdown menus, native menu-bar navigation, form save-and-revert), screenshots and element trees — all through Appium mac2 + WebDriverAgentMac. Three harness defects were found and fixed rather than worked around: a STALE Debug per-RID bundle would have hijacked the session via the shared bundle id (fix: always pin appium:appPath, and check ps for foreign instances); a Terminal window stole a pointer click (fix: 'macos: activateApp' plus assert frontmost == TechieDesk before EVERY pointer action); and WDA died after ~10.3h and was restarted mid-run. ⚠ OPERATIONAL NOTE, not a blocker: WDA is time-limited and is torn down whenever an Appium session is deleted, so it needs restarting roughly daily — the standing 'harness not reliably available' blocker is narrower than recorded. · 2026-07-30 [harness] 📍 **THE HARNESS LIVES AT `tests/appium/` — see its `README.md` before any native-head sweep.** Hardened today after a hand-rolled sweep bypassed it and produced **21 false `VISUAL-FAIL`s** (every screen graded against a full-desktop screenshot occluded by the agent's own Terminal). Nothing pointed at it, so it was invisible — that was the root cause, not the driver. Added: (1) `sweep.wait_settled()` polls the content-node count until stable and stamps `settled:false` + a warning rather than grading a half-rendered tree (the '21 screens, constant 63 content nodes' false reading); (2) `run_sweep.arrival_ok()` gates every screen on the app's own breadcrumb and records `MIS-NAVIGATED` instead of filing the PREVIOUS screen's controls under the new slug — a missed click used to produce confidently wrong data rather than an error; (3) `tests/appium/README.md` documenting the four traps (full-desktop screenshots, system-wide element tree, focus theft, pre-settle reads) with the owner-run prerequisites; (4) `verify-phase.md §3b` now tells every future verifier to look for a project-local harness BEFORE writing a driver — propagated to both the `.claude/commands` and `.opencode/command` copies, since those are what the slash command actually loads. ⚠ The pre-existing guards were already correct and were NOT changed: window-scoped `parse()`, `crop_window()` via sips, `activate()` + `frontmost()` assertion before every click, containment-aware overlap with an area threshold. · 2026-07-31 [*build-phase Cluster C] ✅ **HARNESS MADE LOCALE-INDEPENDENT — it could no longer sweep the app at all in Hindi.** REQ-UI-050 took localization from 6.4% to 46.3%, and `run_sweep.SIDEBAR` keyed every screen off a hardcoded **English** caption, so with `AppearanceLanguage='hi'` every screen failed `nav link '<X>' NOT FOUND`. The breadcrumb is localized too, so even a click that landed would then have failed `arrival_ok()`'s English route-segment match. **Fix**: the third column is now a **resource key** (`NavQdrantAdmin`), and new `tests/appium/strings.py` resolves it against the product's own `AppStrings*.resx`, reading the active language from `InstanceSetting.AppearanceLanguage` read-only — so the harness FOLLOWS the app rather than being told. The decisive find is that `MainLayout` renders the **same key** for the sidebar link and the breadcrumb's page rung, so **one key drives both the click and the arrival proof** in any language; `arrival_ok()`/`MIS-NAVIGATED` is preserved. Only a key RENAME can break it, and pre-flight refuses the sweep naming the dead key — a re-translation never can. **Proven by running it: 21/21 with `en` (no regression) and 21/21 with `hi`**, the Hindi run navigating चैट/दस्तावेज़/Qdrant एडमिन/RAG कॉन्फ़िगरेशन etc., with **identical content-node counts on all 21 screens** — independent evidence the same screens were graded. ⚠ **Route-based navigation was measured and REJECTED** (it would have been better on principle): the native `Go` menu is the only route-driving surface and hardcodes 8 routes, none of the five `/workspace/{slug}/…` ones; the Release bundle registers **no `CFBundleURLTypes`** (checked with PlistBuddy) so there is no deep-link scheme; and mac2 cannot execute JS inside a BlazorWebView. ⚠ **AutomationId gap found**: every sidebar `XCUIElementTypeLink` has `identifier=""` and the href is not surfaced in the AX tree at all — per `verify-phase §3b` that is a coding-standard defect, logged for a follow-up REQ rather than fixed here (this cluster is black-box). **Robustness fixed in the same pass**: a dead session now raises `drv.SessionError` with the driver's own message and ABORTS, instead of surfacing as `TypeError("a bytes-like object is required, not 'dict'")` on all 21 screens — the exact failure that cost three wasted sweeps earlier today; and `run_sweep.preflight()` refuses up front (exit 2) on unreachable Appium/WDA, a dead session, a stale REQ-FN-051 lock, multiple heads, or a renamed resource key. ⚠ Notable self-catch: the builder first wrote the naive `isinstance(dict)` check and its own pre-flight caught that `/timeouts` and `/window/rect` legitimately answer with dicts — a shape-based check would have been a NEW false-verdict class; it keys off the `error` member instead. **Trap #5 added** after the REQ-UI-044 finding: the AX tree reports elements inside an overflow-scrolled container at their UNCLIPPED positions, so overlaps are now tagged `visible`/`clipped` with separate counts — suppression was deliberately NOT implemented, because the tree exposes no scroll offset or clip rect so "scrolled out of view" cannot be proven, and a real defect can sit underneath a phantom (exactly what happened on workspace-chat). No product code touched; language restored to `en` and the session torn down. ⚠ Also recorded as PRODUCT defects, not harness ones: the native macOS menu bar (`MainPage.BuildMenuBar`) and the auth screens (`Login.razor`/`Register.razor`) are still hardcoded English at 46.3% coverage. | [view](#d-req-nfr-011) |
| REQ-RAG-049 | Email / IMAP connector (BRD-135, P2) | Implemented | 90% | ⚠ **RE-CHECKED 2026-08-04 (verifier): unchanged.** 24/24 tests pass including the TR-RAG-037 rename reproduction, but the REQ's acceptance is a MAILBOX and `runtimeVerification.services.imap` is still undeclared — the transport half has never spoken to a real server. Prior: | Implemented | 90% | ⚠ **NOT PROMOTED 2026-08-04 (verifier `*verify`) — the blocking DEFECT is closed, the REQ is not.** **24/24 tests pass, 0 skipped**, including the TR-RAG-037 reproduction from the defect report itself (the same bytes under two names, asserted through `EmailConnector` so it checks the id `ConnectorRunner` actually keys `ItemVersions` on) and the position-fallback case. So *"a second run ingests only what is new"* now holds for a local archive across a rename or move. **But the REQ's acceptance is a MAILBOX**, and no IMAP account is configured on this host — the transport half of the connector has still never spoken to a real server. Only the mbox path has been exercised against real bytes. Declaring an IMAP endpoint in `runtimeVerification.services.imap` is what closes this. Prior: | Implemented | 90% | ✅ **TR-RAG-037 FIXED 2026-08-03 (`*build-phase`, RAG cluster) — the "second run ingests only what is new" acceptance now holds across a rename/move.** Root cause exactly as logged: `EmailConnector.ToItem` built every id as `{folder}/{uidValidity}/{uid}`, and for `MboxMailTransport` `folder` is `Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension(path)` — the archive's FILE NAME — so renaming it changed the identity of every message in it and `ConnectorRunner`, which keys `ItemVersions` on that id, re-ingested the lot (5 duplicate documents observed 2026-07-29). The transport already preferred the stable `Message-ID` as its UID; the file-derived segment threw that stability away. Fix follows the logged suggestion: `MailHeader` gains an additive nullable `StableId` that **the transport supplies when its own coordinates are not stable**, and the connector prefers it (`EmailConnector.cs:271-281`). IMAP passes null and is **completely unchanged** — `folder/uidvalidity/uid` is correct there because the server owns all three, so existing IMAP incremental sync has no migration. `MboxMailTransport` supplies `mbox/{Message-ID}`, falling back to `mbox/sha256:{hash of the raw message}` when a message carries no Message-ID — which also closes the OTHER instance of this bug the old code had, since the previous fallback was the message's POSITION and an archive that gained one message at the front renumbered everything after it. **2 new tests, both pass**: `MessageIdentitySurvivesRenamingTheArchive` is the defect report's own reproduction (same bytes under two names, asserted through `EmailConnector` so it checks the id `ConnectorRunner` actually keys on), and `IdentityOfAMessageWithoutAMessageIdSurvivesReordering` covers the position fallback. Library suite **681 pass / 0 fail**. ⚠ Held at `Implemented`, not `Verified`: no verify-phase has run, and the connector remains **unobservable end-to-end** — no live IMAP account is reachable, so only the mbox transport was exercised against real bytes. A host can now declare one via `runtimeVerification.services.imap` (see `docs/VERIFICATION-ENDPOINTS.md`). Prior: | Blocked | 75% | Added 2026-07-26 on owner review of the mockups — email was missing from the connector set. IMAP (generic / Gmail / Microsoft 365 / local `.mbox`), folder + date + sender + subject filters, optional attachment ingestion (PDF/DOCX/XLSX/PPTX), quoted-reply and signature stripping, incremental sync. Mocked in `docs/mockups/connectors.html`. ⚠ Credentials belong in the OS credential store (REQ-FN-039), TLS required, plaintext IMAP refused. ⚠ Highest-sensitivity source in the product — the scope filters are part of the requirement, not polish. **STILL `Not Started` — the feature is NOT delivered: no app service, no DI registration, no UI, no connector type offered.** ⚠ **BUT ~1,700 lines of pre-existing, unrecorded Email/IMAP library code were found in `src/TechieRag/Connectors/Email/` (hand-rolled `SocketImapConnection` + `MimeParser`, written 2026-07-27, never reviewed, zero tests) and AUDITED on owner request 2026-07-28.** 10 findings (4 major), 9 fixed, 52 hermetic tests added (lib suite 507 → 559), **12/12 behaviour-removal mutations proven RED**. **TR-RAG-027 (major) — IMAP command injection on FIVE paths:** folder name, sender filter, subject filter, account name and message UID all reached the wire unchecked, the UID path not even quoted; wire capture proved the client emitting `SELECT "INBOX"` + `STORE 1:* +FLAGS (\Deleted)` + `EXPUNGE` as three protocol lines where one was intended — executing with the mailbox's credentials and defeating the documented read-only `BODY.PEEK` guarantee. A second payload class needs **no control character at all** (`UID="1:*"` = read the whole folder past every scope filter), and `U+0001` in the username forged a field in the XOAUTH2 SASL payload. Fixed with a control-char refusal at 5 call sites **plus a choke-point re-check on the composed command line**, so a future call site that forgets cannot reintroduce it. **TR-RAG-028 (major)** — server-declared literal length honoured unbounded (a hostile `{2000000000}` allocated 2 GB before reading); now a per-response `MaxMessageBytes` budget. **TR-RAG-029 (major)** — `ImapMailboxOptions.Timeout` had **no effect on any async read** (`TcpClient.ReceiveTimeout` does not apply to `SslStream.ReadAsync`): a real server that accepts and never speaks blocked 12s against a 2s setting; explicit `CancelAfter` deadlines now on connect, TLS handshake and every read/write. **TR-RAG-030 (major)** — unbounded response accumulation (300,001 untagged lines retained in 121 ms with no tagged completion); line-length + line-count caps added. Minor, fixed: attachment-count amplification (39 MB message → 400,000 retained attachments, +400 MB heap), `SINCE` formatted in machine culture (`SINCE 02-janv.-2026` under fr-FR — breaks incremental sync), mbox `>From ` unescaping only one quote level (silent content corruption), NTFS ADS colon kept in attachment names. **Open: TR-RAG-035 (product decision)** — `MimeParser` silently discards content past its depth cap with nowhere to report it; a notes channel would be a breaking SDK change, so it was logged rather than guessed. ✅ **Genuinely clean verdicts, asserted not assumed: TLS** (implicit TLS only, **no STARTTLS path to strip**, no cert-validation callback anywhere — pinned by a reflection test; a real plaintext server is refused at the handshake with no `LOGIN` byte sent) and **credential hygiene** (password absent from every log message, exception `Message` and `ToString()` across 5 paths, including a server that echoes the password back in its own text). ✅ **Deliberately NOT copied: the HTTP SSRF guard** — a mail host is operator-named and legitimately private (`mail.corp.internal`, `10.x`, `localhost:993`), so blocking RFC 1918 would break the ordinary enterprise case while protecting nothing; recorded as TR-RAG-036 so a later audit does not "fix" it. ⚠ **Audit gap:** no real IMAP server was ever contacted — the injection proof is client-side (three protocol lines emitted where one belongs, which is unambiguous), but downstream execution is inferred from IMAP framing, not observed · ✅ **BUILT 2026-07-28 (`*build-phase`) — the app surface now exists; this is no longer `Not Started`.** The library code was already there and hardened; what was missing was every app-side seam, and all of it is now in place: **(1)** `ConnectorTypes.Email` (`"email"`, matching `EmailConnector.SourceType`) plus its hub descriptor, so a mailbox is offered as a source you can add; **(2)** the mailbox half of `ConnectorSettings` — server/port/account/OAuth flag, `.mbox` path, folders, since-date, sender and subject filters, own-address, sent/spam/attachment switches, reply- and signature-stripping — with `ToEmailOptions()` / `ToImapOptions()`, `ValidateEmail()` and `DescribeEmail()`; **(3)** `DatabaseConnectorResolver.BuildEmail()`, which builds `ImapMailTransport` over `SocketImapConnection` (implicit TLS) or `MboxMailTransport` for an archive, taking the credential from the OS store like every other connector (REQ-FN-039); **(4)** the mailbox form in `ConnectorEdit.razor`, plus a `mail` icon on both hubs. The page's two-type `IsRepository ? … : …` ternaries were replaced with type-aware members (`TypeHeading`, `CredentialLabel`, `SubjectNoun`, `SelfHostedNoun`) — with a third type they were no longer expressible as a binary. **Security clauses are enforced, not documented:** ports 143/110 and an `imap://` host are REFUSED AT SAVE TIME with the reason (the library speaks implicit TLS only, so a cleartext port cannot work — failing at save names it, failing at run time is a socket error at 07:00 on a schedule); naming both a server and an archive is refused rather than silently merged. **Scope defaults stay narrow:** a blank folder list means INBOX alone and never every folder; sent, spam and attachments are opt-in; reply/signature stripping default on. **19 tests** (`tests/TechieDesk.Tests/Connectors/EmailConnectorSettingsTests.cs`) cover validation, the narrow defaults, option mapping and the JSON round trip. **Live-smoked on the running Catalyst head:** the Mailbox card renders on the connectors hub, its form opens with every control and 0 visual defects at 1800 px, and the switch defaults are correct on screen (`.verify/shots/smoke-email-form2.png`). ⚠ **`Implemented`, not `Verified`, and the gaps are real:** no live IMAP server has ever been contacted from this app (a save-and-run against a real mailbox is owner UAT); **attachment ingestion is wired as a setting but not yet fed processors** — `BuildEmail` passes `attachmentProcessors: null`, so an attachment-enabled connector ingests bodies and silently skips attachments, which is the one part of BRD-135 still outstanding. Was `Not Started 0%`. · 2026-07-29 verifier: hermetic coverage is broad and ALL PASSES — scoping filters, TLS-mandatory refusal (RefusesAPlaintextImapServer, ExposesNoWayToDisableCertificateValidation), credential hygiene, reply trimming, attachment bounds, MIME hardening, IMAP command-injection guards and the sent/spam-off defaults, across 50+ tests. But the 'second run ingests only what is new' acceptance is BROKEN by TR-RAG-037 logged today: EmailConnector item identity embeds the mbox archive's FILE NAME, so relocating an archive re-ingests everything (5 duplicate documents observed). Also unobservable end-to-end — no live IMAP account is reachable, so only the mbox transport was exercised against real bytes. | [view](#d-req-rag-049) |

Multi-line composer + per-turn mode/model/scope controls render on workspace chat (111 content nodes). · 2026-07-31 [*verify all, chained inline from `*build-phase` §6b] ⚠ **visual: `Button:'Read this answer aloud'` overlaps `Button:'Send message'` by 980 px² on workspace-chat @1024×720** — the enforced REQ-UI-041 floor. Clean at 1600×1240 (0 overlaps), so this is a narrow-width composer defect only. Evidence: `test-results/ui-verify/workspace-chat-n1024.png` + `workspace-chat-n1024.json`. **This is PRE-EXISTING, not a regression from this phase** — no cluster touched `WorkspaceChat.razor` (Cluster A: LlmSettings + config services; Cluster B: `Services/Install/` + MauiProgram + App.xaml.cs + Info.plist; Cluster C: the six localization pages, which do not include WorkspaceChat; Cluster D: `src/TechieRag.Telemetry`). It surfaced now because this is the first sweep to reach the screen with the current message history. §4a data-render PASSES (131 content nodes, settled, arrival-gated on the app's own breadcrumb); it is §4b that flags. Two other flags on this run were triaged as the documented false-positive classes and are NOT defects: the a11y skip-link parked at x=-7660, and 42 below-fold sidebar entries at the 1024 floor on a scrollable sidebar (scrollbar visible in the capture). Routes to `/trblazeui` as a layout fix. Stays `Needs re-verify`. · 2026-07-31 [*build-phase Cluster A] ✅ **FIXED, and the diagnosis corrected the orchestrator's own report.** Root cause was **height-driven with a width-driven trigger**, derived arithmetically rather than guessed: the transcript was sized inline `min-height:24vh; max-height:56vh`, and **a viewport percentage scales WITH the chrome it must leave room for**. At the 1024×720 floor the web viewport is 690px, so 56vh = 386px while the chat column's fixed chrome needs **453px** — and the chrome is TALLER at 1024 than at 1600 because `.td-composer-mode` wraps the control bar onto 3 rows. 386+453 = 839 down a 690px viewport, so the column ran **149px past the window bottom**; at 1600 the same sum fits with room to spare, which is why d1600 was always clean. ⚠ **The 980 px² overlap the orchestrator filed was PARTLY A HARNESS ARTIFACT**: the macOS AX tree reports elements inside an overflow-scrolled container at their UNCLIPPED layout positions, and the transcript had no scroll-to-newest anywhere in the file, so it sat parked on the oldest message and laid its overflowing history out over the composer. Proven twice from the orchestrator's own capture — the sidebar's scrollback reports the same way (`Link:Data & Storage @86,788` … `RAG Configuration @86,1115`, all below the 762 floor). **The defect underneath it was real and worse than the overlap**: genuine composer controls were off-window. Fix is three parts — a documented budget in `base.css` (`.td-chat-transcript { min-height:8rem; max-height:calc(100vh - 470px) }`, the measured chrome plus margin), a new `composer.js` `scrollToEnd` parking the transcript on the newest turn, and the assistant action row aligned to the bubble start so the control's x-position no longer moves with the message text. No control removed or hidden; `docs/mockups/workspace-chat.html` unchanged (and the budget expresses what the mockup's `.chatgrid{flex:1;min-height:0}` always meant, which 56vh never did). 5 tests in `WorkspaceChatLayoutTests`, **all proven RED against the pre-fix tree first**. ⚠ **Honest test limit, stated by the builder**: these are NOT bounding-box assertions — the test project is `net10.0` and cannot reference the `net10.0-maccatalyst` head, and there is no bUnit/AngleSharp/Playwright in the repo; two tests assert real geometry by parsing the shipped stylesheet's height against pixel constants from the capture, three are markup guards against the exact revert. · 2026-07-31 [*verify all, chained inline] ✅ **CONFIRMED LIVE after rebuilding Release**: `workspace-chat` is now **ov=0/0 at BOTH widths** (was 1 visible overlap), `Attach`/`Prompts`/mic/Send are back in-window, and older turns now report at NEGATIVE y — independent evidence scroll-to-newest is working. ⚠ **RESIDUAL, and it is not what the builder predicted**: 6 STATIC TEXT nodes still sit below the window floor of y=760 — `Return`/`Shift + Return`/`for a new line…` at y=768 and `Mode:`/`Auto-RAG`/`retrieval scoped to whole workspace` at y=794. They improved by ~59px but did not fully clear. Informational hint rows, not interactive controls, so the screen is usable — but §4b's "lies within the page bounds" is not fully met, which is why this is `Implemented 90%` and **NOT `Verified`**. Was `Needs re-verify 75%`. | [view](#d-req-ui-044) |

| REQ-FN-042 | Background scheduler service (BRD-139, P5) | Implemented | 85% | ⚠ **recovered by `*refresh-status` 2026-07-28** — the row said `Not Started`, but the helper exists: `apps/TechieDeskScheduler/` is a real project carried in `TechieRag.slnx`, with `Services/Scheduling/LaunchAgentSchedulerHelper.cs` (macOS launchd), `WindowsSchedulerHelper.cs`, `SchedulerHelperLocator.cs`, `SchedulerPreferencesStore.cs` and `DesktopRunEnvironmentProbe.cs` (run conditions), install/remove surfaced in `Automations.razor`, covered by `Scheduling/SchedulerHelperTests.cs`. **Held at `In Progress`, not `Implemented`** — the helper **cannot ingest on macOS** (every ingest dies on TR-RAG-025, ONNX native load), which is the helper's whole purpose, and the Windows per-user service has never been run anywhere. — Added 2026-07-26 (2nd owner review) — owner challenged the claim that a desktop app cannot run schedules while closed. It can: a per-user helper (macOS **launchd** agent, Windows **per-user service**) installed/removed from the app UI, hosting the **same in-process scheduler** — no separate server, no job database, no listening port, no third-party job framework. Run conditions: mains-power only, named networks only, wake-for-run, optional menu-bar indicator. With it off, behaviour falls back to run-while-open + catch-up. Mocked in `docs/mockups/automations.html` · ✅ **2026-07-28 (`*build-phase`) — the ONNX blocker is FIXED; this REQ is no longer blocked on the library.** TR-RAG-025 is resolved: `TechieRag.Embedded` now loads ONNX Runtime in a plain `net10.0` host on macOS (`CoreML, WebGpu, CPU` providers), so the helper's own reason for existing — ingesting with the window closed — is unblocked. **The cause was not what the entry said:** the native library was present and correctly laid out all along; `Microsoft.ML.OnnxRuntime` declares its imports as the literal `onnxruntime.dll`, and .NET never substitutes `.dylib` for a name that already has an extension. Fixed by a `DllImportResolver` installed from a module initializer in `src/TechieRag.Embedded/OnnxNativeLibraryResolver.cs`; 4 regression tests in `tests/TechieRag.Tests/Embedding/OnnxNativeLoadTests.cs`. The helper itself was run this session (`--once`) and completed a clean poll cycle. ⚠ **Still `In Progress`, and deliberately so:** (a) **no end-to-end scheduled ingest has been proven on this host** — `src/TechieRag.Embedded/Models/` is empty (models are downloaded, not committed), so nothing can actually embed here; (b) the **Windows per-user service has still never been run anywhere**. Both are what stands between this and `Implemented`. Was 55%. · 2026-07-29 [REQ-FN-042] ✅ **The macOS half is PROVEN END-TO-END — the helper ingests with no app process alive, which had never been demonstrated.** (1) **ONNX in the helper host, deliberately rather than by luck:** the TR-RAG-025 resolver reached the helper only via a `[ModuleInitializer]`, firing incidentally inside the first embedding call — the fix held by ordering luck, and any future path reaching `Microsoft.ML.OnnxRuntime` first would have silently re-broken it. New public `OnnxRuntimeProbe.Check()` installs the resolver explicitly; every run now logs `ONNX Runtime native library loaded (TR-RAG-025); execution providers: CoreMLExecutionProvider, WebGpuExecutionProvider, CPUExecutionProvider`. Failure is a WARNING, not an exit (DB maintenance needs no model). (2) 🔴 **PROJECT-STATUS's blocker was factually WRONG:** it said `src/TechieRag.Embedded/Models/` is empty so nothing can embed here — models do not live there; `GetModelDirectory()` resolves `<assembly dir>/models/bge-m3`, and the full **2.27 GB BGE-M3 download was already cached**. Nothing downloaded, nothing stubbed: **real 1024-dim BGE-M3 vectors** (4096-byte blobs, all distinct and non-zero, checked per chunk). (3) **Four scheduled runs, all `Succeeded`, all `TriggerKind=Scheduled`** against a local `.mbox` connector (no network, no credential), seeded as `Connector` + `Schedule` rows exactly as the Automations screen writes them: 3-of-3 → 0-of-3 (unchanged correctly skipped **across two separate helper processes**, so sync state survives process death) → 1-of-4 (discovered by the helper's own **30-second poll loop**, not `--once`) → 5-of-5. Vector store 3 docs/4 chunks → 12/13. (4) **The launchd agent was installed and run for the first time ever**, through the product's own `LaunchAgentSchedulerHelper.InstallAsync`: `launchctl print gui/501/com.techiedesk.scheduler` → `state = running, pid = 81321, last exit code = (never exited)`, and **that launchd-started process performed a real scheduled ingest**; `UninstallAsync` then booted it out and deleted the plist. Install→fire→remove is live-proven, not merely unit-tested. Also fixed the two CS8602s at the root: `CronExpression.TryParse` now carries `[NotNullWhen(true)]`, deleting the warnings AND four `!` suppressions across `ScheduleInterpreter`/`ScheduleCalculator`/`CronDescriber`. +4 tests. ⚠ **One genuine gap remains: the Windows per-user service has still never been run anywhere** — `WindowsSchedulerHelper.cs` is unit-tested only, and a Windows head cannot be built or run from this macOS host (platform limit, not a skipped step). ⚠ Left for a verifier: fixture `req-fn-042-smoke.mbox` + a DISABLED schedule, plus 9 smoke documents — 5 of them duplicates caused by newly found TR-RAG-037. | REQ-FN-043 | Mac Catalyst keychain entitlement — durable secret store (BRD-132, P1) | Blocked | 0% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*build-phase` (Cluster E).** 🔴 **The shipped Catalyst bundle has NO keychain entitlement, so no signed-in session can survive a relaunch on any build produced today, and the app only WARNs about it.** Evidence: every launch in `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk/logs` (8+ occurrences across 2026-07-27/28/29, including two relaunches driven live this phase) logs `TechieDesk.Services.Auth.OsCredentialStore: The OS credential store rejected this process (Exception): Error adding record: MissingEntitlement`, then `…secrets are held in memory for this run only`. `OsCredentialStore.Probe()` therefore returns false and every write goes to `EphemeralSecretStore` (`IsDurable=false`). **Root cause:** `apps/TechieDesk/Platforms/MacCatalyst/` contains only `AppDelegate.cs`, `Info.plist`, `Program.cs` and `onnx-customops-stub.c` — there is **no `Entitlements.plist`**, and `TechieDesk.csproj` sets no `<CodesignEntitlements>`. This is **NOT a security regression** (nothing sensitive reaches disk; the degradation is the deliberate, correct one) but it **silently voids the acceptance clause of TWO requirements** — REQ-FN-032 clause 4 (session survives relaunch) and REQ-FN-039's headline clause (tokens + provider secrets in the OS credential store). *Acceptance:* (1) `apps/TechieDesk/Platforms/MacCatalyst/Entitlements.plist` declares `keychain-access-groups` = `$(AppIdentifierPrefix)com.techierathore.techiedesk`; (2) `TechieDesk.csproj` references it via `<CodesignEntitlements>` on the maccatalyst TFM; (3) the bundle is signed with a real Apple Development identity — **ad-hoc (`-`) signing cannot carry a keychain-access-group**; (4) a launch logs a DURABLE store (`IsDurable=true`, no MissingEntitlement); (5) a signed-in session survives a real process relaunch, live-driven. ⚠ **OWNER DECISION REQUIRED — needs a signing-identity choice, not just a file.** Relates to REQ-FN-038c (sign + notarize), which is blocked on the same decision · 2026-07-29 verifier: confirmed Not Started and OWNER-BLOCKED. All five acceptance clauses unmet: no apps/TechieDesk/Platforms/MacCatalyst/Entitlements.plist, no <CodesignEntitlements> on any TFM in TechieDesk.csproj, no signing identity configured, and all SEVEN launches logged today print OsCredentialStore MissingEntitlement with zero durable-store lines. Gates REQ-FN-032 clause 4 and REQ-FN-039's headline clause, and shares the signing-identity decision with REQ-FN-038c. Needs an Apple Development identity — ad-hoc signing cannot carry a keychain-access-group. | [view](#d-req-fn-043) |

Key coverage is now **scraped from the components** (`ResolvesEveryKeyTheRazorComponentsAskFor`) rather than hand-listed, and every `{0}` is checked across the whole resource set (`EveryPlaceholderSurvivesTranslation`) — the two failure modes invisible to a key-set comparison.
