That is precisely how इंजेशन/इनजेशन survived tranche 2. Five terms are now settled and enforced: **ingestion→इनजेशन, workspace→वर्कस्पेस, Status→स्टेटस, Actions→क्रियाएँ, Provider→प्रोवाइडर**. | REQ-NFR-012 | Encrypt VectorStore/Persistence connection strings at rest (BRD-95) | Implemented | 95% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*build-phase` (Cluster B), carving the surviving residual out of REQ-NFR-004.** REQ-NFR-004's 004b closed on 2026-07-25 when provider **apiKeys** gained encryption-at-rest (`enc:v1:<DataProtection payload>` via `TechieRagConfigProtector`). **The same file still stores `VectorStore` and `Persistence` `ConnectionStrings` in CLEARTEXT, and a pgvector one embeds `Password=…`.** This is NOT retired by the 2026-07-29 app-side Postgres removal (REQ-FN-029): pgvector belongs to the `src/TechieRag` **library**, which legitimately keeps it, so the cleartext exposure survives in `techierag-config.json`. *Acceptance:* (1) `VectorStore.ConnectionString` and `Persistence.ConnectionString` persist through `TechieRagConfigProtector` exactly as apiKeys do; (2) a legacy cleartext file is transparently upgraded on first save, never silently re-written cleartext; (3) a round-trip test proves a `Password=`-bearing connection string never appears in plaintext on disk; (4) the mechanism degrades the same way apiKeys do when DataProtection is unavailable. ⚠ Standing threat-model limit (unchanged, applies to all of the above): the DataProtection key ring sits unencrypted beside the data it protects — no DPAPI/KMS on macOS/Linux — so this is key separation, not a KMS boundary · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] Implemented by extending the ONE existing mechanism rather than adding a second: `TechieRagConfigProtector.EnumerateSecretFields` — the single list driving protect, reveal, the upgrade decision and the OS-store key — now yields `VectorStore:ConnectionString` and `Persistence:ConnectionString` alongside the five apiKeys, so both inherit the identical `enc:v1:` DataProtection envelope, the identical `enc:v2:` credential-store path when a durable store exists, and the identical fail-safe (field cleared, **field name only** in the log, RevealSecrets returns false so the operator's file is left INTACT). **No call site needed changing.** 8 new tests, 29 green across ConfigEncryption/ProviderSecretStore/ConfigSectionMerge. `ConnectionStringPasswordNeverReachesDiskInPlaintext` asserts the password, the full DSN **and** the substring `Password=` are all absent from the persisted bytes, then reloads byte-identical — so absence is not achieved by losing the value. Caught a non-obvious interaction: the section-scoped merge re-reads from **disk**, so an unowned DSN makes a full decrypt-then-re-encrypt round trip on every unrelated save; pinned by a test that saves three times through a screen owning NEITHER section, catching both a double-encrypted unreadable DSN and the worse case — the password reappearing in cleartext in a file some other screen wrote. **Smoke (service-level, WDA down)**: a scratch host wired exactly as `MauiProgram.cs` issued the same `SaveConfigAsync` call `/rag-config` makes; `techierag-config.json` (which did not previously exist) was created holding `enc:v1:` twice with the password absent from the raw bytes, a fresh service instance reloaded both DSNs intact, and overwriting with a legacy cleartext file then loading once removed the password from disk — the transparent upgrade, end to end. ⚠ The `/rag-config` SCREEN was not rendered. **Standing limit restated, not addressed**: on macOS the DataProtection key ring sits unencrypted beside the data, so this is **key separation — defeating a casual read, a backup copy or a support bundle — not a KMS boundary** against an attacker who already holds the data directory. · 2026-07-29 [build-phase smoke] `/rag-config` re-checked after the encryption change: renders every documented field with values matching the saved `techierag-config.json` exactly (Embedding Source=Ollama, Model=bge-m3, Vector Store=SqliteVec, Chunk Size=500, Overlap=50, Telemetry on), **no error banner**, nothing saved. ⚠ **Caveat worth recording:** every `apiKey` on this install is null and the vector-store connection string is a plain local SQLite path, so **the decrypt path was never fed real ciphertext** — this confirms the screen still renders and does not error after the change, it does NOT re-prove round-trip decryption of an actual encrypted secret (that remains covered by the 29 unit tests and the service-level smoke). | [view](#d-req-nfr-012) |
| REQ-NFR-013 | 🔴 `ConfirmEgress` is a LYING CONTROL — the UI promises to ask before a skill leaves the machine, and nothing ever asks (BRD-99/BRD-84, P1) | Implemented | 95% | **Added 2026-07-30 by `*build-phase` (orchestrator UI observation).** Found while confirming REQ-RAG-022 on the running app. `AgentDefinition.ConfirmEgress` (`AgentDefinition.cs:85`) is declared **`= true` by default**, persisted (`AgentRepository.cs:167/172/198/230`, `0003-AgentRegistry.sql:45`) and bound to a `Switch` in **two** places — `WorkspaceAgents.razor:266` (built-in agent) and `:527` (agent editor), both labelled *"Ask before any skill that leaves this machine"*. 🔴 **It is read by NO execution path.** Confirmed absent from `WorkspaceChat.razor`, `AgentToolPlanner.cs` and `AgentSkillResolver.cs`; the only references in the whole repo are the declaration, the Dapper persistence, and the two switches. The user is shown a privacy guarantee that is **on by default** and enforced nowhere. ⚠ **Newly reachable as of today:** REQ-RAG-022 wired `web-scrape` into the live chat loop, so enabling that skill now performs outbound requests with no prompt while this switch reads ON. Before today the toggle was inert because no egress-capable skill was wired, which is why it was never noticed. ⚠ This ALSO supersedes the open question raised by REQ-RAG-022 ("should a first outbound request need a second confirmation?") — the product already **claims** it does. *Acceptance:* (1) with `ConfirmEgress` true, the first skill invocation in a turn that leaves the machine (`web-search`/`web-scrape`, i.e. anything the catalogue badges *Leaves the machine*) **blocks on an explicit user confirmation** before the request is made; (2) declining cancels that tool call and the agent reports it as unavailable, without killing the turn; (3) with `ConfirmEgress` false the agent proceeds silently, which is the setting's whole purpose; (4) the per-agent editor value is honoured, not just the built-in agent's; (5) a test asserts NO outbound request is issued while a confirmation is pending — the defect is that nothing reads the flag, so a test that only checks the flag's value would pass today; (6) if a confirmation UI is genuinely out of scope, the switch must be **removed or disabled with an explanation** — shipping a privacy control that does nothing is worse than shipping none. ⚠ Relates to REQ-NFR-008 (privacy/data locality, `Verified`) — that row's zero-egress guarantee rests on skills being OFF by default, which remains true; this defect is about what happens once a user turns one ON. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-NFR-013] ✅ **FIXED — the switch now has a reader.** `EgressGate` (`apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/Agents/EgressGate.cs`) wraps the turn's `SkillImplementation`s and blocks the invoker of any skill the catalogue marks `SkillExposure.LeavesMachine` — **keyed off exposure, never a name list**, so a future egress skill inherits the gate; `EveryCatalogueSkillThatLeavesTheMachineIsGated` fails if one doesn't. Enforcement is at **EXECUTION, not registration**: the skill stays registered and visible to the model, and a refusal returns `SkillUnavailable.Because(...)` so the loop records it and finishes the turn. Confirmation asked through the new `IEgressConfirmation`, implemented by `WorkspaceChat.razor` as a modal; **deny-by-default when no confirmer is registered**; dismissal/cancellation are declines. Asked **once per turn**, cached both ways. Read from `AgentDefinition.ConfirmEgress`, so the per-agent editor and the built-in agent share one path. `AgentToolPlanner.BuildRegistry` two-arg semantics UNCHANGED. *Acceptance:* (1)(2)(3)(4) by `EgressConfirmationTests` (15) + `AgentRegistryPersistenceTests.PerAgentEgressConfirmationSurvivesARestartAndReachesTheGate`; 🔴 (5) proven HARD by counting at an `HttpMessageHandler` under the **shipped** `HttpWebContentFetcher` — `NoRequestIsIssuedWhileConfirmationIsPending` asserts `RequestCount == 0` with the prompt open AND the tool task incomplete, then exactly 1 after release; a flag-value-only test could not pass it, which matters because the defect WAS that nothing read the flag. (6) N/A — the UI was built, not the switch removed. Suite **1,822 passed / 0 failed** (was 1,806); `OutboundEgressTests` 4/4, `AgentToolPlannerTests` 7/7, `AgentSkillResolverTests` 10/10 all unmodified and green. **OBSERVED LIVE on the Catalyst head** (`test-results/ui-verify/egress-*.png`): modal raised mid-turn ("Send a request off this machine? … Nothing has been sent yet — this turn is waiting for your answer."); **decline → `unavailable:` message + a 3-step execution trace with the turn intact** (clause 2 visually confirmed by the orchestrator); allow → real fetch returned page content; switch OFF → no dialog at t+2/4/6s and the fetch completed silently (clause 3). ⚠ **HONEST LIMITS:** the model's tool-call decision was scripted by a throwaway stub LLM (this host has no provider; stub killed and config/DB restored + verified afterwards) — the app, gate, dialog and fetcher were all the shipped ones. The dialog's X/Escape gesture is code-covered but not screen-observed. Clause 4's live turn used the BUILT-IN agent; the per-agent editor path is test-proven, not screen-proven. Caps at `Implemented` — a builder's smoke never writes `Verified`. | [view](#d-req-nfr-013) |
| REQ-FN-052 | 🔴 LLM Settings "Save & apply" does not reach `techierag-config.json` — the page and the RAG instance read different stores (BRD-9, P1) | Implemented | 90% | **Added 2026-07-30 by the REQ-NFR-013 fix (incidental finding, not touched).** Selecting Ollama + endpoint `http://localhost:11434` + a model and clicking **Save & apply** leaves the page reading back as Ollama — **and it survives an app restart** — while `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk/techierag-config.json` still holds `llm.source: 0`, and chat keeps answering *"No LLM provider is configured. Configure one in LLM Settings to chat."* Only hand-editing that file made the provider resolve. 🔴 So the settings page's persisted state and the file `TechieRagManager` builds the RAG instance from appear to be **two different stores** — the UI reports success and survives restart, which is exactly why this reads as working. ⚠ This is the same shape as REQ-FN-010's silent false success and REQ-NFR-013's unenforced switch: **the surface says it worked and the effect never lands.** *Acceptance:* (1) Save & apply writes the provider selection to the store `TechieRagManager.CreateInstanceFromConfigAsync` actually reads; (2) after save, a chat turn resolves the configured provider with no restart and no hand-editing; (3) a test asserts the page's write and the manager's read hit the SAME path — not two stores that happen to agree; (4) if two stores are intentional, one must be derived from the other, never independently written. ⚠ Blocks any end-to-end LLM verification on this host, and therefore a large share of the 45 `Needs re-verify` rows. · 2026-07-31 [REQ-FN-052] **FIXED — root cause proven from the app's OWN logs, and all five starting hypotheses REFUTED.** The logs show one absolute path written and re-read within 1ms of every save, which kills the divergent-path, section-merge, enum-round-trip and `ReconfigureAsync`-no-op theories outright. **Two stacked defects.** (a) The "second store" is not a file, a DB table or a second appsettings layer — it is `TechieRagConfigService.cachedConfig`, handed out **BY REFERENCE** by `LoadConfigAsync()`. Every settings screen `@bind`s its form fields onto that object, so merely SELECTING a provider wrote it into the service's cache **before and independently of any save**, and every later read (the page re-entered, MainLayout's LLM hint, App Settings) was served that mutated cache while `TechieRagManager` built from the untouched file. Fixed by handing out and storing a **deep copy**, so the in-memory config is strictly DERIVED from the file and is replaced only by a disk read or a write that reached disk (acceptance clause 4). (b) Nothing ever reached the file because the save was being **REFUSED** — log 2026-07-30 23:06:11 `Save refused: the "Ollama" provider is not fully configured: Base URL is required... Model is required` — because `http://localhost:11434` was **placeholder text only**. `LlmConfigValidator.DefaultEndpoint/HasUsableDefaultEndpoint` now materialise a local server's base URL into the bound field. Writer and reader both derive the path from the new `DataDirectory.ConfigFilePath`, and `TechieRagManager` exposes `ResolveConfigFilePath`/`ReadSavedConfigAsync`/`ResolveConfiguredLlmAsync` which `CreateInstanceFromConfigAsync` itself calls, so tests assert the PRODUCTION read path. 6 new tests in `tests/TechieDesk.Tests/Settings/LlmSettingsPersistenceTests.cs`, **RED-proven in two independent reverts** (shared-cache revert → 2 fail; path revert → 3 fail). ⚠ One test is a spec test for a helper that did not previously exist and so cannot be red-proven by reverting; its RED evidence is the production log line. · 2026-07-31 [*build-phase §6 smoke, LIVE on the Catalyst head] ✅ **Clauses 1, 3 and 4 PROVEN ON THE RUNNING APP.** Selecting Ollama then **Save & apply** wrote `source: 1 / endpoint http://localhost:11434 / model` into `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk/techierag-config.json` (before: `source 0`, `endpoint null`, `model ""`, mtime 30 Jul 23:44 — the exact defect state). **Clause 4 negative check passed**: switching the provider to Anthropic WITHOUT saving, navigating away and back, read back as **Ollama** (the persisted value) — pre-fix this read back as Anthropic forever. **Clause 2 closed on RESOLUTION, not on a completed answer**: `Test connection` now returns `Connection failed: Could not connect to the server`, i.e. the app resolved Ollama and attempted a REAL connection, where pre-fix the same control returned `No LLM provider configured. Save settings first.` ⚠ **Ollama is not installed on this host**, so a chat turn producing an actual answer remains undriven — hence 90%, not 100%. ⚠ **Correction to an earlier reading**: the `No LLM provider is configured` text still visible on /chat is NOT a live banner and NOT a lying control — `WorkspaceChat.razor:1355` PERSISTS that string into the conversation store as an assistant message, and the store holds exactly **3 such rows** in `TrMessage.Content`, matching the owner's failed 2026-07-30 turns visible in the screenshot. ⚠ Also observed: the accessibility tree reports an empty field's PLACEHOLDER as its `value`, so `Model` reads as `llama3.2` while empty — a smoke that trusted the tree would have recorded a false pass here. The owner's config was backed up and **restored** after the smoke. | [view](#d-req-fn-052) |
| REQ-FN-044 | Instance mode from AppManager licence tier — Individual vs Team/Enterprise (BRD-142, P2) | Implemented | 90% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*amend-docs` (owner decision).** New `InstanceMode` service resolves the tier from AppManager and caches it. *Acceptance:* (1) tier resolves to exactly one of Individual / Team / Enterprise and is observable in the UI; (2) mode affects **entitlements and the visibility of team features only** — it never introduces roles, never partitions data, and never makes the install multi-user; (3) an **unassigned, expired or unreachable** seat degrades to **full Individual capability, never to a locked or read-only state** (BRD-129 is absolute — a team member who loses network or whose org stops paying keeps working over their own documents); (4) the cached tier honours the BRD-51 grace terms; (5) a test asserts no code path can make local data unreachable on tier loss. ⚠ Deliberately NOT in scope: roles, capability matrix, workspace assignment — REQ-FN-041's deletions stand and `0002-DropWorkspaceAssignment.sql` stays dropped · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] New `InstanceMode`/`SeatState` enums, `InstanceModeStatus`, a pure `InstanceModeResolver` and a scoped fail-open `InstanceModeService` under `Services/Licensing/`, registered in `AddTechieDeskLicensing`; tier maps configurable via `LicensingOptions`. **No second cache** — mode resolves from the `LicenseStatus` that `LicenseService` already persists, so BRD-51 grace and restart-survival are inherited and no migration was needed. Surfaced on existing licence surfaces only (`LicenseStatusCard.razor`, `Billing.razor`) — no new screen. 🔴 **BRD-129 proven THREE ways**: `NoStateCanMakeLocalDataUnreachable` resolves all **432** availability x tier x status combinations asserting Mode >= Individual and LocalDataAccessible in every one; `ModeVocabularyCannotExpressALockedInstall` pins Individual as the enum ZERO value and bans any future member named like a denial (lock/readonly/denied/suspend); `LocalDataPathsNeverConsultTheInstanceMode` scans `Services/{Data,Workspaces,Storage,Threads,Agents,Connectors}` and FAILS if any data-access file even mentions InstanceMode — so no data path is in a position to ask about licensing. No roles reinstated (grep + a reflection test over the shipped assembly; `0002-DropWorkspaceAssignment.sql` untouched). 57/57 licensing tests green. Service-level smoke against the REAL installed `techiedesk.db`: mode=Individual/None, TEAM_SEATS gated, RAG_CHAT allowed, all 14 tables readable. ⚠ **PARTIAL for one reason only**: acceptance (1) requires the mode be 'observable in the UI' and **the rendered surface was NOT observed** — WebDriverAgentMac would not start (developer mode disabled, needs sudo). Razor compiles into the signed Release bundle; no render seen, none claimed, no screenshot exists. | REQ-FN-045 | Seat-based team licensing via AppManager (BRD-143, P2) | PARTIAL | 60% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*amend-docs` (owner decision).** An organisation buys seats in AppManager and assigns them to named users; each seat entitles one user to one install signed in as themselves. *Acceptance:* (1) a signed-in user on an assigned seat sees Team/Enterprise entitlements; (2) seat state is cached and survives an AppManager outage per BRD-51; (3) revoking a seat degrades that install to Individual at the next successful check — it does **not** lock it; (4) a seat is one user on one install — the product never asks two people to share a running instance. ⚠ Depends on a reachable AppManager, which this host does not have; the wire contract is testable now (`AppManagerClient` is `Verified`) but end-to-end seat assignment will stay unexercised until a licence server is reachable — do not mark `Verified` on wire-contract evidence alone · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] Seat awareness layered on the existing `LicenseService`/`AppManagerClient` with **no new wire endpoints**: seat state derives from validated tier + status (Active→Assigned; Expired→Expired; Revoked/Cancelled/Suspended→Revoked; unrecognised→Unassigned). TEAM_SEATS added to `OfflinePremiumFeatures` following the existing WHITE_LABEL pattern rather than duplicating it. Grace proven end-to-end: a Team seat survives a 24h outage as Team/Assigned/IsFromCache and degrades to Individual/Unverified only past the 72h window. **Degradation proven against the REAL installed database** — a Team seat was written to the real `LicenseCache` table, revoked, then rewound past grace, after which every local table was unchanged and the user's own content was still fully readable (techierag.db Documents=12/Chunks=13, rag-store TrWorkspace=1/TrWorkspaceDocument=3), and a rebuilt service graph still resolved Individual with RAG_CHAT enabled. Seeded row removed, cleanup independently verified. ⚠ **Cannot honestly exceed PARTIAL**: end-to-end seat assignment needs a reachable licence server, and AppManager `https://192.168.1.14:5101` was probed LIVE during the smoke and is unreachable (TaskCanceledException after 6s), with no `AppManager:BaseUrl` in the shipped bundle. No UI observation either. **Do not mark Verified on wire-contract evidence alone.** · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-045] **AUDIT — NO CODE GAP in the built pipeline, but acceptance clause (4) is HALF-enforced, which is a real finding.** Verified present: `SeatState` (`InstanceMode.cs:38-69`), the full status→state mapping (`InstanceModeResolver.cs:139-160`), `TEAM_SEATS` following `WHITE_LABEL` (`LicensingOptions.cs:45`), grace (`LicenseService.cs:153-197`). Clause (4) splits in two. | REQ-FN-046 | Backup and restore to a self-contained archive (BRD-144, P2) | Implemented | 90% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*amend-docs` (owner decision).** New `BackupService` + a Backup & Restore settings surface. This is how a team hands work to a colleague: export to a shared OneDrive/Drive/Dropbox folder, colleague restores. *Acceptance:* (1) export at **whole-instance OR single-workspace** granularity to any OS-picker-reachable destination; (2) the archive carries documents, chunks, **embeddings**, workspace settings and threads, and restores on a **different machine and a different OS**; (3) 🔴 **the archive NEVER contains credentials** — AppManager tokens, provider API keys and connector secrets stay in the OS credential store and are excluded by construction, because the archive is expected to land in a third-party sync folder (a test must assert no secret material appears in a produced archive); (4) pack and unpack **stream** to and from disk so a large instance cannot exhaust memory — the benchmark removed its own export partly because large instances *crash during zipping*; (5) a manifest records schema version, app version and **embedding-model identity**. ⚠ Per-workspace granularity is a requirement, not a nicety — instance-only export is what made the benchmark's version unusable · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] `BackupService` + the `.tdbak` archive built in `Services/Backup/` (9 files) with a Backup and Restore page at `/settings/backup`, wired into the Operator nav group and the breadcrumb map. **Instance AND per-workspace granularity, both round-tripped.** 🔴 **Key design discovery: the data model spans THREE SQLite files**, not one — workspaces/threads in `techiedesk-rag-store.db`, documents/chunks/**vectors** in `techierag.db`, and the credential-bearing tables in `techiedesk.db`. That made credential exclusion clean and provable. Archive is a **streamed** ZIP of 6 JSONL streams + a versioned manifest (archiveFormatVersion, appVersion, schemaVersion, createdAtUtc, scope, workspace ids/names, **embedding model + dimension + source**, counts, per-entry sha256+length); rows enumerate unbuffered from SQLite and hash as they stream, so peak memory is ONE ROW — the benchmark removed its own export partly because large instances crash during zipping. 🔴 **Credential exclusion BY CONSTRUCTION**: the packer reads a fixed allow-list read-only with explicit column lists and **never enumerates the data directory**, so `keys/`, `connector-secrets.json`, the `enc:v1:` apiKeys and `LicenseCache` are structurally unreachable. Proven by scanning for **3 REAL secret fragments taken from the owner's live install** — absent from the raw archive bytes AND every decompressed entry. **Live round trip**: exported 1 workspace / 12 documents / 13 chunks (60,775 bytes) from the owner's real install and restored into a scratch data directory with **all 13 embedding vectors byte-identical** (sqlite3 hex(Vector) diff); re-export matched the original counts; the live install was verified UNMODIFIED (export opens SQLite ReadOnly, mtimes unchanged). 🔴 **Caught a silent-failure mode**: with a non-SqliteVec vector store (Qdrant/pgvector) the embeddings are not in the data directory at all, so an archive would restore documents and chunk text with **every vector missing while looking like a successful backup** — Export now refuses with an actionable message. 65 Backup tests green. ⚠ PARTIAL: the **UI was never rendered** (WDA down, developer mode disabled), so the OS-picker destination path and the screen itself are unverified at runtime. · 2026-07-29 [build-phase smoke] `/settings/backup` **reached by CLICKING the sidebar OPERATOR entry** (not by URL), breadcrumb resolving to `Operator > Backup & Restore`. Renders the full surface: export scope RadioGroup (Everything — every workspace / One workspace), the `Choose a folder and export…` destination button, the cloud-folder guidance alert, the restore card, and the red `Restore this backup` action. 🔴 **The red sync-folder danger Alert is correctly ABSENT** — the data directory is `~/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk`, not inside a cloud-sync folder — which exercises `SyncFolderDetector`'s negative path against a real path. Verified at 1600 and the 1024x720 floor, 0 overlaps / 0 zero-size / 0 off-viewport. ⚠ **Render-only by instruction — no export was executed through the UI**, since the service layer is already proven by a live round trip and a UI-driven run could touch real data. The native OS folder picker is outside the Appium session and was deliberately not opened. | [view](#d-req-fn-046) |
| REQ-FN-047 | Restore safety — model check, conflict choice, zip-slip, integrity (BRD-145, P2) | Implemented | 85% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*amend-docs` (owner decision).** The safety half of REQ-FN-046, split out because each clause is separately testable and each maps to a known real-world failure. *Acceptance:* (1) 🔴 **refuse an archive whose embedding-model identity differs from the target install** and offer re-embed instead — same-dimension vectors from a different model **silently corrupt retrieval** rather than failing loudly, which is the worst failure mode in this feature; (2) **never write outside the data directory** whatever paths the archive claims (zip-slip) — mutation-prove it with a hostile archive containing `../` entries; (3) present an explicit **skip / duplicate / replace** choice when a restored workspace already exists and **never silently merge or overwrite** — the benchmark's restore was an all-or-nothing instance rollback with no merge path; (4) **verify integrity before applying anything** — a partial restore must leave the install as it was; (5) a pre-flight report states exactly what will change before the user commits. ⚠ **Live-directory sync is PROHIBITED and this REQ owns saying so in the UI**: the data directory holds live SQLite + a live embedded vector store, and consumer sync clients corrupt both (ADR-013). If a user points their data directory at a synced folder we should detect and warn, not silently proceed · 2026-07-29 [build-phase] All five safety clauses implemented and **mutation-proven**. (a) Restore refuses an archive whose embedding-model **identity** differs — comparison is model name AND dimension, so a **same-dimension foreign model is caught**; re-embed imports chunk text with a NULL vector so the result is visibly un-embedded rather than invisibly mis-embedded. (b) Zip-slip: rejects blank/NUL/rooted/UNC/drive-qualified names and `..` under BOTH separators, then requires the resolved path to sit under the staging root **plus a separator**, re-checked immediately before the FileStream that writes — not only at pre-flight. (c) Conflict choice is explicit **skip / duplicate / replace with no Merge member**; Skip is the default and Apply stays disabled until pre-flight clears. (d) Integrity: every entry's sha256+length verified, and the manifest must cover every content entry, BEFORE anything is applied; apply then runs in transactions on both databases committed together. (e) Pre-flight report names each workspace, its counts and whether it already exists here, and is strictly read-only. ADR-013's live-sync prohibition is surfaced by `SyncFolderDetector` (OneDrive/Dropbox/Drive/iCloud/Box/pCloud/MEGA/Syncthing, matched on whole path segments so `/Users/megan` is not a false positive) as a red Alert — applied to the LIVE data directory only, never to an archive written INTO a sync folder, which is the intended workflow. 🔴 **Mutation proof worth reading**: removing EITHER zip-slip guard alone stayed GREEN (they are genuinely redundant); removing BOTH turned **10 tests red**. Also: dimension-only model comparison → 4 red; skipping VerifyIntegrity → tamper test red; Skip made to overwrite → conflict test red; packer made to ship `techierag-config.json` → 2 credential tests red. Live smoke: a hostile archive built FROM the real archive (valid manifest, passes integrity, so the traversing entry is the only thing left to stop it) was blocked reason=UnsafeEntry, Restore threw, planted file absent; a foreign-model install refused a same-dimension archive with offersReEmbed=true. ⚠ PARTIAL: the on-screen presentation of the pre-flight report and the conflict choice was **never rendered** (WDA down). · 2026-07-29 [build-phase smoke] The restore surface renders on `/settings/backup` — `Choose a backup file…` plus the red `Restore this backup` action **correctly gated inactive pending pre-flight** — at 1600 and the 1024x720 floor. ⚠ **The pre-flight → apply flow itself was NOT driven through the UI** (deliberate: a UI restore could touch the owner's real data), so the on-screen presentation of the pre-flight report and the skip/duplicate/replace choice remains proven at the SERVICE layer only, where all five safety clauses are mutation-proven. Held at 85% for that reason. | [view](#d-req-fn-047) |
| REQ-FN-048 | Default vector-store path must not resolve against the CWD (BRD-130, P1) | Implemented | 95% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*build-phase`.** 🔴 **The app writes a live database INTO its own signed `.app` bundle.** The default `VectorStore.ConnectionString` is the relative `Data Source=techierag.db`, resolved against the process CWD; on the Mac Catalyst head that CWD is the **bundle root**, so a run produced a live 24 KB `techierag.db` at `apps/TechieDesk/bin/Release/net10.0-maccatalyst/TechieDesk.app/`. Consequences observed by THREE independent clusters this phase: **codesign fails outright** ('unsealed contents present in the bundle root', `Xamarin.Shared.targets:2596`), the Release build breaks until the stray file is deleted by hand, and **user data ends up inside a signed application bundle**. Path-dependent — it reproduces when the binary is exec'd directly with CWD inside the bundle, not via `open` — which is why it went unnoticed. *Acceptance:* (1) the default vector-store connection string resolves against `TechieDeskDb.DataDirectory`, never the CWD, exactly as `AppDb:ConnectionString` already does (REQ-FN-034 pinned that one in `Program.cs`; this is the same fix for the other database); (2) an existing config carrying the relative default is migrated to the absolute path on first load, and a stray bundle-root artefact is relocated rather than orphaned; (3) 🔴 **a test over REAL production call sites** asserts no persistent artefact resolves against the CWD — note `DataDirectoryTests.EveryArtefactSharesOneDirectory` is tautological (it builds its own paths then asserts they match) and stayed green through this AND the `TechieDeskDb.Cli` CWD-log defect, so a new guard is required, not an extension of that one; (4) launching from the Release bundle leaves `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passing. ⚠ Relates to REQ-FN-034 (demoted today because its 'no path is resolved against the CWD' claim is false) and REQ-FN-037 · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-048] ✅ **FIXED — all four acceptance clauses proven on the shipping Release bundle.** (1) The SqliteVec connection string now resolves through the new `DataDirectory.ResolveSqliteConnectionString`, the exact counterpart of `AppDbConnectionString`, applied at all FOUR seams in `TechieRagConfigService` (saved-config load, `WriteConfigAsync`, appsettings fallback, pure-defaults fallback) plus the builder call in `TechieRagManager`. 🔴 **The old `?? DefaultVectorConnectionString()` was DEAD CODE** — `VectorStoreConfig.ConnectionString` is non-nullable and pre-populated with the relative literal, so the defensive guard could never fire; that is how this survived. (2) Migration proven on the REAL install: the live `techierag-config.json` carried `Data Source=techierag.db` and was rewritten to the absolute path at 12:25:43, and a 196,608-byte vector DB seeded in the bundle root was relocated **byte-identically** (sha `3bc8913c…` unchanged) into the data directory rather than orphaned. (3) New guard `tests/TechieDesk.Tests/Data/WorkingDirectoryArtefactTests.cs` (6 tests) drives REAL production call sites from a sandbox CWD then **enumerates that directory**, so an artefact fails the test by existing. Deliberately NOT an extension of the tautological `EveryArtefactSharesOneDirectory`. **Verified non-vacuous**: reverting the three production fixes turned 4 of 6 RED. (4) `codesign --verify --deep --strict` reproduced `unsealed contents present in the bundle root` (exit 1) with the stray present and returned exit 0 after relocation. 🔴 **CORRECTION TO THE DEFECT DESCRIPTION — it is NOT path-dependent.** `lsof -d cwd` on the running Catalyst process shows **UIKit resets the process CWD to the `.app` bundle root at launch**, discarding the inherited one, so this reproduced on EVERY launch however started, not only via direct exec. ORCHESTRATOR SMOKE 2026-07-30: after a real `open` of the Release bundle the bundle root contains `Contents` ONLY and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` exits 0. Library root cause logged as **TR-RAG-040** (every SQLite default in the library is a bare relative path; `ConnectionString` non-nullable + pre-populated makes consumer `??` fallbacks dead code). Suite 1,806 pass / 0 fail. | [view](#d-req-fn-048) |
| REQ-FN-049 | 🔴 P0 — app deadlocks at launch whenever a saved config exists (BRD-128, P0) | Implemented | 95% | **Added 2026-07-29 by `*build-phase` smoke. THE APP CANNOT LAUNCH.** Any install that has ever saved LLM/RAG settings presents **zero windows, forever**. *Root cause — sync-over-async on the UIKit main thread:* `MauiProgram.cs:401` does `ragManager.InitializeAsync().GetAwaiter().GetResult()` on the launch delegate, and `TechieRagManager.CreateInstanceFromConfigAsync` then does `await File.ReadAllTextAsync(configFilePath)` (`TechieRagManager.cs:168`) **without `ConfigureAwait(false)`**, so the continuation is posted back to the very main-thread SynchronizationContext that `GetResult()` is blocking. Classic deadlock. When the config file does NOT exist, `File.Exists` is false, that await never runs, and startup completes — which is exactly why it stayed hidden. *Proven by `sample`:* the main thread parks in `MauiUIApplicationDelegate application:willFinishLaunchingWithOptions:` → managed code → `Monitor.Wait` and never returns, so `CreateWindow` is never reached (process frontmost, 0 windows, 0.6s CPU over 6 min). *Proven empirically BOTH ways:* config present → 0 windows every time; config moved aside → window appears and the log reaches 'TechieRag persistence store initialized'. *Log evidence bounds it precisely:* last good boot **09:05:15** (before the config file was first written at **09:39:11**), then **SEVEN consecutive dead boots** — 17:03:22, 17:04:40, 17:06:11, 17:10:53, 21:04:49, 21:10:46, 21:13:06 — each stopping at the identical line. Same failure via Appium, via `open`, and via executing the binary directly, so it is a code defect, not a harness or binding problem. 🔴 **NOT a same-day regression** — `TechieRagManager.cs` is dated **26 Jul 22:39** and was untouched by today's encryption work (which changed only `TechieRagConfigProtector.cs` / `TechieRagConfigService.cs`); this is a **latent P0 of at least three days**, invisible only because no config file existed on this host until today. ⚠ `MauiProgram.cs:374` and `:407` use the same `GetAwaiter().GetResult()` pattern and are **latent duplicates of the same bug**. *Acceptance:* (1) the app launches and presents a window with a saved `techierag-config.json` present; (2) no `GetAwaiter().GetResult()` remains on the launch path — initialization moves off the UI thread, or the whole chain carries `ConfigureAwait(false)`; (3) a **regression test that launches with a config file present** — its absence is precisely why 1,622 green tests never caught this; (4) the two sibling call sites are fixed or proven safe. ⚠ Explains why six rows were capped 'no screen was ever rendered' all afternoon — the harness outage MASKED this, it did not cause it · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-049] ✅ **FIXED — THE APP OPENS.** Owner's chosen shape implemented: window first, load after. All three `GetAwaiter().GetResult()` sites are gone from the launch path — `:401` and `:407` deleted outright into a new `AppStartup.InitializeAsync` (`Services/Hosting/AppStartup.cs`) dispatched by `Task.Run` and never awaited (**`Task.Run` is load-bearing — it strips the SynchronizationContext so no continuation can be posted back to the main thread at all**), and `:374` replaced by a genuinely synchronous `ILanguageStore.Load()` over a new `IInstanceSettingRepository.Get` (Dapper `ExecuteScalar`). 🔴 **That third site was safe only BY ACCIDENT** — SQLite has no async file I/O, so its await completed inline and never posted a continuation; that is a property of the current driver, not a guarantee, and it was the wrong pattern to leave standing next to the one that killed the app. Only DbUp migrations and the UI-culture read now precede first paint, both synchronous end to end and both justified: the first render reads the app DB, and `IStringLocalizer` reads `CurrentUICulture` **per lookup**, so a culture applied after first paint would leave the launch screen in English. All 39 awaits in `TechieRagManager` now carry `ConfigureAwait(false)`. **Failure is no longer fatal OR silent** — a new observable `AppStartupState` singleton (Initializing/Ready/Failed) plus a native 'started with limited functionality' alert naming the log folder, on top of the existing Serilog error. 🔴 **REGRESSION TEST PROVEN RED FIRST**: `StartupDeadlockTests.RagInitializeCompletesWhenBlockedOnFromAUiThreadWithSavedConfig` seeds a real config file and drives `InitializeAsync` from a genuine single-threaded SynchronizationContext whose pump stops while a delegate blocks — the UIKit launch thread. Against unmodified code it **HUNG and failed on the 45s budget while its no-config sibling passed in the same run**, reproducing the reported asymmetry exactly; after the fix it passed in **151 ms**. `AppStartup` was extracted into Core specifically to create a testable composition-root seam — **the absence of exactly that seam is why 1,622 green tests never touched this: every one ran against an empty data directory, so none ever entered the branch that only executes when the config file exists.** SMOKE (Appium + WDA, Release bundle, `appium:appPath`): with the 1838-byte `techierag-config.json` **PRESENT** — the exact condition behind seven consecutive dead boots — a 1800x1250 window appeared in **10.5s** and the shell painted the first-run wizard with its 6-step rail and native menu bar; the log reached `TechieRag persistence store initialized`, the line those seven boots never got to. Config absent: window in 2.1s, working case not regressed. Config restored byte-identically (sha256 eca7bc4d…0bd0ea, 1838 bytes) — the app itself re-encrypted `vectorStore.connectionString` during the run, which is REQ-NFR-004b behaving correctly, not test damage. Build 0 errors / 4 warnings (baseline-identical); **1,648 tests pass**. ⚠ OPEN follow-ups: no in-shell banner binds to `AppStartupState` yet (needs `MainLayout.razor`, concurrently owned); and `src/TechieRag/TechieRagClient.cs:101` still awaits `vectorStore.InitializeAsync` without `ConfigureAwait(false)` — harmless now that nothing blocks, but the same latent shape and worth its own row. · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-049] ✅ **BOTH OPEN FOLLOW-UPS CLOSED.** (a) The shell now binds to the `AppStartupState` singleton: `MainLayout.razor` renders a non-blocking strip under the topbar — spinner + "Finishing startup…" while Initializing, and a dismissible destructive strip carrying `FailureMessage` verbatim when Failed — placed ABOVE the licence and provider strips because a store that did not come up explains everything else on screen. Subscribed to `Changed` (not polled), marshalled through `InvokeAsync` because `Changed` is raised on a thread-pool thread, and unsubscribed in `Dispose` (a process-lifetime singleton holding a handler on a per-circuit layout is a leak). Failure dismissal is per-circuit and deliberately NOT persisted. Strings localized en+hi. (b) `src/TechieRag/TechieRagClient.cs:101` now carries `ConfigureAwait(false)` — it was the only await on the initialization chain without it, the same shape that caused the P0. **`Task.Run` in `AppStartup.BeginAsync` is UNTOUCHED and still load-bearing.** +3 tests asserting the observable contract the strip depends on (every phase change published; the phase already readable inside the handler; the failure message arriving with its phase; an unsubscribed handler really stopping). ORCHESTRATOR SMOKE 2026-07-30: the **Release bundle** was launched via `open` with the real saved config present — **a window appeared (count=1, 1800×1250)** and the log reached `TechieRag persistence store initialized`, the line the seven dead boots never got to. ⚠ Neither strip has been SEEN rendered (both need a forced failure / a cold-launch race); handed to the verifier. | [view](#d-req-fn-049) |

Result: `GuardFirstRunAsync` skips `/setup` **permanently**, the user is never offered LLM setup, and the app sits with no provider configured while the wizard that would fix it is never shown. This is why the owner never saw the Ollama/LM-Studio/offline choice. ✅ **The once-only machinery already exists and is correct** — `ISetupStateService.IsFlagCompleteAsync()` plus `Setup.razor:456 MarkCompleteAsync(mode, baseUrl)`; it is simply short-circuited by the workspace check above it. *Acceptance:* (1) the setup-complete FLAG, not the existence of a workspace, decides whether first-run setup is offered — a default workspace must not suppress it; (2) 🔴 **once completed OR explicitly skipped, the wizard NEVER appears again** — the owner was explicit that re-prompting 'will irritate the user that okay, why the hell I have to set it up every time when I have done it once'; (3) the wizard checks whether an **LLM provider** is configured (it already detects Ollama, offers LM Studio / OpenAI-compatible, and allows staying fully offline/embedded-only) and, per the owner, whether **agents** are configured; (4) choosing offline/embedded-only is a **legitimate completed outcome**, not an unfinished state to nag about — a user who deliberately stays offline is never prompted again; (5) if a provider is missing AFTER setup is complete, surface at most a **dismissible, non-blocking** hint — never a forced redirect; (6) a test asserts the guard on a fresh install WITH a default workspace present still routes to `/setup`, which is exactly the case the current code gets wrong. ⚠ Account-free launch (BRD-129) is UNCHANGED and was re-affirmed by the owner 2026-07-30 — no sign-in is introduced by this row · 2026-07-30 [REQ-FN-050] ✅ **FIXED AND VISUALLY PROVEN — the owner's reported bug.** ⚠ First, a status correction: this row read `Not Started 0%` but the work had ALREADY been built earlier the same day (`FirstRunGate.cs` 00:38, `FirstRunGateTests.cs` 00:44, `SetupStateService.cs` 00:31) by a session that died before the status gate ran — the row was STALE, not unstarted. The guard is now flag-first: `MainLayout.GuardFirstRunAsync` reads `ISetupStateService.ReadAsync` and hands the facts to `FirstRunGate.Decide` (a pure static in Core — testable, which a razor file is not, and that is precisely why a mis-ordered early return survived). The old `if (workspaces.Count > 0) return;` above the flag check is GONE; the count is carried into `FirstRunContext` for reporting and asserted irrelevant in BOTH directions. REAL-STATE SMOKE 7/7 (real `SetupStateService` → real Dapper `InstanceSettingRepository` → real SQLite copies of the installed databases, real workspace count 1 from `TrWorkspace`): live install as-is → None; 🔴 **setup rows deleted + default workspace present → ShowWizard** (the case the old code got wrong on every real install); completed-with-provider → None; explicitly skipped → None; offline embedded-only finish → None; completed-but-provider-gone → ShowProviderHint (never a redirect); hint dismissed then re-read → None. 🔴 **ORCHESTRATOR VISUAL PROOF 2026-07-30**: the Release bundle was launched against an ISOLATED COPY of the data directory (`AppDb__DataDirectory`, confirmed in-log at 12:38:48 — the owner's real install was never mutated) with the setup rows deleted and 1 default workspace present. **The first-run wizard RENDERS**: AuthLayout with no sidebar, 6-node step rail, `Step 1 of 6 · Defaults`, the zero-config offline card (Embedded BGE-M3 + SqliteVec), Back/Continue, and the `Set up later` ghost exit.
