| REQ-FN-051 | Seat is bound to one install — local identity + single-instance guard (BRD-143, P2) | PARTIAL | 60% | **Added 2026-07-30 by `*build-phase` (cluster F audit).** REQ-FN-045 acceptance clause (4) says "a seat is one user on one install". The *second* half of that clause — one user on one install — is **enforced nowhere**. Confirmed independently: no mutex, no `LSMultipleInstancesProhibited` in `Platforms/MacCatalyst/Info.plist`, no locally-computed install/device/machine identity anywhere in the repo. `MaxDevices`/`ActivatedDevices` (`UserLicenseData.cs:46-50`) are display-only counters echoed back from AppManager, and `ValidateAsync` posts only a bearer token, so **nothing ties a validated seat to THIS installation** — the same credentials activate unlimited installs and the product cannot tell. *Acceptance:* (1) the app computes a stable local install identity that survives restart and is not trivially clonable; (2) that identity is presented at licence validation so AppManager can bind a seat to it; (3) a single-instance guard prevents two copies of the same install racing on one data directory; (4) revoking or reassigning a seat degrades the un-bound install at its next successful check, consistent with REQ-FN-045 clause (3) — degrade, never lock; (5) tests cover identity stability across restart and the second-instance refusal. ⚠ **Blocked on an owner/server decision:** (2) needs a server-side install-registration contract in AppManager that does not exist today. (1) and (3) are buildable locally now. ⚠ Do NOT let this regress the account-free launch guarantee (BRD-129) — an unlicensed local user must never be gated by install binding. · 2026-07-31 [REQ-FN-051] Clauses **(1), (3), (5) BUILT**; **(2) client-half only, dark by default**; **(4) documented, NOT built** — the scope split this row itself predicted. Install identity (`apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/Install/`) is a GUID minted once into the data directory combined with a salted SHA-256 of the macOS `IOPlatformUUID` re-derived every launch, exposed as a composite; a copied data folder keeps its install id but changes its composite, which is what lets a server tell a MOVE from a SECOND install. No raw hardware UUID is ever persisted or transmitted (REQ-NFR-008). **HONEST LIMITS, in the XML docs and not softened:** this is seat-accounting hygiene, **not copy protection** — it does not resist a patched binary, an edited identity file, or a cloned VM image, and if the Catalyst head is ever App-Sandboxed the `ioreg` read is denied and the fingerprint **degrades to the user-editable host name** (logged, surfaced on `IsMachineBound`). ⚠ Note this dev host is itself a VM (`machineName: Ss-Virtual-Machine`), which is precisely the case the design does not resist. It needs no keychain, so **REQ-FN-043's missing entitlement cannot break it**. Guard is a data-directory-scoped exclusive file lock (`FileShare.None`, released by the OS on crash) plus a readable PID+start-time owner record, with `LSMultipleInstancesProhibited` in Info.plist for the Finder case. ⚠ **MAUI's incremental build does NOT regenerate `obj/**/AppManifest.plist` after an `Info.plist` edit** — the key silently failed to reach the bundle across three builds until the intermediate was deleted; the orchestrator re-confirmed `LSMultipleInstancesProhibited => true` in the REBUILT **Release** bundle via `plutil`. BRD-129 guarded twice and behaviourally: an account-free offline install never so much as computes an identity (counter-asserted, not flag-asserted). `.tdbak` **cannot** carry the identity by construction (fixed six-entry allow-list), so a restored backup mints a new one and consumes its own seat. All five implementation reverts RED-proven, including deliberately injecting the classic naive PID-file bug to confirm the stale-lock test catches it. Clause (2) travels as the `X-Install-Id` **header** behind `AppManager:SendInstallIdentity` defaulted **OFF** — `docs/AppManager-api-usage-guide.md` specifies `POST /LicenseSvc/validate` as taking no body, so a body was rejected as the contract-breaking option; with the flag off the request is byte-for-byte pre-REQ-FN-051 (pinned by test). · 2026-07-31 [*build-phase §6 smoke, LIVE on the Catalyst head] ✅ App **launches** with `App`'s new REQUIRED `SingleInstanceState` ctor parameter (the highest risk in this change — a DI resolution failure would have prevented launch entirely). `techiedesk.lock` + `techiedesk.instance.json` (PID 66532 + start time) present after launch; **`install-identity.json` ABSENT** on the account-free launch — the BRD-129 evidence. **Second-instance refusal PROVEN CROSS-PROCESS**: launching the bundle binary directly produced a real window titled *"TechieDesk is already running"* reading *"Another copy of TechieDesk (process 66532) is already using this data folder: /Users/sravikumar/Library/Application Support/TechieDesk"* with a **Close this copy** button, and the lock owner did not change — a visible refusal, not a silent exit. ⚠ **Cannot exceed PARTIAL**: clause (4) is unbuildable until AppManager returns an install-binding verdict — it needs registration, an `installBinding` response field, and a `GET /LicenseSvc/{id}/devices` (the existing DELETE-device endpoint is **unreachable today** because nothing in the documented API ever issues a `deviceId`). ⚠ **OWNER DECISION owed:** a restored `.tdbak` mints a new identity and therefore consumes a fresh seat — so restoring your own backup onto a replacement Mac burns a second seat, with no server contract to release the old one. ⚠ **Harness consequence:** this guard now affects the verify harness — a sweep that launches the app while a prior instance holds the lock gets the refusal window instead of the app. | [view](#d-req-fn-051) |
| REQ-UI-051 | User-visible English left in `@code` blocks on localized pages (BRD-91, P4) | Implemented | 95% | **Added 2026-08-01 by `*build-phase` — disclosed honestly by the REQ-UI-050 builder and confirmed live in the Hindi smoke.** The coverage counter measures MARKUP by documented design (one markup position, many C# branches), so user-visible strings built in `@code` are outside its measured population and **no current test catches them**. Observed rendering as English inside an otherwise-Hindi screen on 2026-07-31: `Local socket` and `Disconnected` on `/qdrant-admin`. Known sites: `ConnectorsHub` `OutcomeLabel`/`ReportTitle`/`ItemRows`/`FormatDuration` + the WebSources tile names; `Support` `HostDescription`/`PasteShortcut`/`Describe(...)` (whose output reaches the user via `loadError`, `newIssueError`, `detailError` and a toast); `QdrantAdmin` `qdrantStatus`/`TlsBadgeText`/`LastCheckedText` + the three endpoint-kind Select option strings (which double as bound VALUES — see TR-024, so localizing them naively would put translated strings on the wire). ⚠ `WorkspaceAgents` and `Automations` already de-staticked theirs (3 and 6 members) and localized them, so the pattern is proven. Also outside file scope: `SkillCatalog.ExposureLabel` and skill `DisplayName`/`Description` in `TechieDesk.Services.Agents`. *Acceptance:* (1) these members are de-staticked and localized; (2) the coverage counter is extended to measure `@code` string literals that reach the user, or a separate test is added that does — an unmeasured gap re-grows; (3) bound Select VALUES stay culture-invariant while their display text localizes. · 🔴 **2026-08-01 [*build-phase §6 smoke] — SEVERITY RAISED. This is not a tail of leftover strings; it is the single most visible string in the product.** Driving the app in Hindi, the PRIMARY screen `/workspace/{slug}` renders English in five places. Root causes confirmed by reading the source, not inferred: **(1) `WorkspaceChat.razor:1248` — `ComposerPlaceholder()` is a hardcoded English `@code` interpolated string** returning "Message {workspace name}… / Shift + Return for a new line, Return to send." It is the chat composer's placeholder and hint — what a user looks at every time they open the app — and it is **invisible to the coverage counter by design**, so REQ-UI-050 correctly reported `WorkspaceChat` at 101→0 hardcoded MARKUP sites while this stayed English. **(2)** the mode / model / scope controls paint English. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-01 — the orchestrator's original diagnosis of this half was WRONG.** It was recorded as TR-024 (a `Select` trigger painting its bound value). In fact `WorkspaceChat` has exactly **ONE** `Select` — the mode picker — and it **already had `DisplayTextSelector`**; the model and scope 'triggers' are **`DropdownMenu` chips, not Selects**, and they rendered English because `ModelChipLabel()` / `ScopeChipLabel()` built the text in `@code`. Same symptom, different mechanism: this is entirely REQ-UI-051, not TR-024. (`ChatScopeWholeWorkspace` was indeed already localized in markup at `WorkspaceChat.razor:371` — which is exactly what made the wrong cause look plausible.) ✅ **Both are now fixable**: TR-024 has a proven workaround as of 2026-08-01 (`Select.DisplayTextSelector`, demonstrated on 9 Selects in `WorkspaceFlows.razor`), and `WorkspaceChat` currently uses it on only 2 of its Selects. Scope note: applying `DisplayTextSelector` app-wide is arguably TR-024 follow-up rather than this row, but the two are indistinguishable to a user looking at a Hindi screen, so they should be fixed together. ⚠ Also folded in here from REQ-UI-050 tranche 3: **text-bearing attributes the counter does not score** and which are still English — `errorMessage` (6, Setup), `progressMessage` (5, TextIngestion), `PickerTitle` (3, Ingestion), `testResult`, `catalogueError`, `promoError`, `deactivateError`, `currentPasswordError`. Because they are unscored, **the reported 80.1% coverage overstates true coverage** — widening the counter's `TextAttributes` will lower the percentage without any localization being lost, which is the honest direction. · 2026-08-01 [*build-phase Cluster A] **DELIVERED — and the largest find was NOT on the brief.** 🔴 `LlmSettings`' ENTIRE LLM provider form (every label, placeholder, hint and the direct-chat-disabled warning) lives in a `RenderFragment` **inside `@code`**, so the markup counter never saw a character of it while the page measured **zero** hardcoded sites — the same mis-measurement that produced REQ-UI-052, in a shape nobody had considered. 261 new keys across the 20 registry components (MainLayout excluded, owned elsewhere). The headline defect — `ComposerPlaceholder()` — is fixed. **Bound values made culture-invariant, which caught a latent bug**: `QdrantAdmin`'s daemon endpoint kind was a STRING that WAS the English label and was parsed back via `ParseKind` to build the endpoint, so localizing it naively would have broken the Docker connection; it now binds the enum and `ParseKind` is deleted. Same for `ConnectorEdit` (`imap`/`mbox`, not `Mail server (IMAP)`) and `WorkspaceChat`'s persisted thread-title sentinel. **New guard**: `CodeBlockStringCoverage` — a SIBLING counter that leaves the ratchet's definition untouched and gates every registry file at zero, RED-proven by reverting the real defect into the real 2,000-line file rather than a fixture. ⚠ It states its blind spots AS blind spots: one-word labels are invisible, and English reaching the user from outside the razor tree cannot be seen. ⚠ Its v1 and v2 came back noisy/green and were REJECTED — "the current shape is what survived being wrong twice." ⚠ It also fixed a counter defect a concurrent cluster introduced the same day: `IsTextBearing` gained suffix matching plus `error`, which made Blazor's own `Context="row"` directive count as user-visible text (it ENDS IN "text"), turning 10 already-localized files red for 53 sites of text that does not exist. 🔴 **CORRECTION to the orchestrator's own earlier diagnosis on this row** — see the amended clause (2) above: the model/scope controls are `DropdownMenu` chips, not `Select`s, so that half was never TR-024. · 2026-08-01 [*verify all §6 smoke] ⚠ **NEW FINDING — service-layer English still reaches the screen.** `/settings/data` renders 9 artefact names and 9 descriptions in ENGLISH on a Hindi install (`Embedding model` / `BGE-M3 weights, downloaded once for offline RAG`, `Vector store` / `SqliteVec embeddings`, …), sourced from a static table at `apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/Storage/DataStorageInspector.cs:44-51`. This is EXACTLY the blind spot Cluster A declared, and it fell between two clusters' scopes — A owned registry files, D owned markup, and this is neither. **It is why "100% coverage" must always be quoted as "100% of MARKUP".** Held at `Implemented 90%`: the declared scope is done, this class is not. · 2026-08-02 [*build-phase Cluster B] ✅ **SERVICE-LAYER HALF CLOSED — by making the class structurally impossible rather than by patching instances.** The six known services now return **invariant resource KEYS** and the presentation layer resolves them: `DataStorageInspector`, `SkillCatalog`, `AgentTrace` (all 12 step-kind titles + 5 detail lines; the REQ-UI-040 kind→label mapping unchanged and still asserted by `FlowTraceTests`), `ChatComposerState`, `SupportIssueCatalog`, `LlmConfigValidator`. ⚠ **The page-level mapping the previous pass chose was ALREADY LEAKING, which is what decided this**: `WorkspaceAgents.razor` had a six-arm `SkillDisplayName` switch, but lines 1003 and 1483 called `SkillCatalog.Find(name)?.DisplayName ?? name` **directly** and rendered English on every install; `AgentTracePanel` and `DataStorage` had no mapping at all; and every page switch ended in a `_ =>` arm falling back to the service's own English, so a seventh skill would have shipped English silently. As the builder put it: *"(a) is not a fix, it is a per-page re-implementation that has to be remembered at every call site."* Where a service owns real fallback logic it takes a `LocalizeText` delegate instead, so the localizer stays at the one call that actually renders. **Wire vocabulary provably culture-invariant**: a test collects every service-owned persisted/sent/matched value — skill `Name`s, all 14 AppManager `Code`s, artefact `RelativePath`s, validator field constants, `DefaultEndpoint` URLs, brand names — builds the array under `en` then `hi` and asserts byte equality, **and** asserts it still literally contains `rag-search`/`InProgress`/`techiedesk.db` so a stable-but-WRONG vocabulary also fails. Three deliberate invariant calls, each documented: the validator's exception/log text is the key-plus-provider form (it lands in the log and in `TechieRagManager`, never on a screen); `DescribeSource` stays English because every value but `None` is a brand rendered as itself; and **the support thread's priority note is built from wire CODES, because it is a comment POSTED TO APPMANAGER — a Hindi install must not file a ticket its responder cannot read.** 45 keys (2,577 → 2,622). 🔴 **THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST: the class is ~95× larger than the six known sites — 569 English prose literals across 88 files under `apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/`**, a large share with a razor binding traced. Raised as **REQ-UI-055**. **New guard `ServiceStringCoverage`** (8 tests): zero tolerance on the 8 converted files, and a **ratchet at exactly 569** so any new English label anywhere in the service layer fails — RED-proven by adding one label to a file on no registry, which failed exactly one test. ⚠ **One mutation came back GREEN first time and it exposed a real hole in how this project tests localization**: dropping a Hindi key resolved to the **English** value with `ResourceNotFound == false`, so a missing translation was silently passing. The harness was changed (`OwnKeys` now excludes parent cultures) and the mutation re-proved red. That flaw would have affected any future localization test written the obvious way. ⚠ Blind spots stated by the builder and worth repeating: it cannot tell user-visible English from machine-facing English, cannot see one-word labels or runtime-composed sentences, only looks under `Services/`, and **the ratchet FREEZES the backlog rather than fixing it**. ⚠ `AgentTrace.ToPlainText` has **no caller anywhere in the razor tree** — the copy-trace affordance REQ-UI-034 describes appears never to have been wired up; someone should decide whether to wire it or delete it. | [view](#d-req-ui-051) |
| REQ-UI-052 | Native macOS menu bar and auth screens are hardcoded English (BRD-91, P4) | Implemented | 90% | **Added 2026-08-01 by `*build-phase` — found by the REQ-NFR-011 harness work while driving the app in Hindi.** Two user-visible surfaces never entered the localization programme: (a) the **native macOS menu bar** built in `MainPage.BuildMenuBar` — `File` / `Go` / `View` / `Help` and every item under them, including the REQ-UI-041 accelerators; (b) the **auth screens' copy**, `Login.razor` and `Register.razor`. Neither is on the `LocalizedFiles` registry, so `LocalizedFilesNeverRegainAHardcodedString` does not guard them and the 46.3% coverage figure never counted them. ⚠ Convenient accident worth preserving deliberately: `recover_to_app()` drives the `Go` menu and keeps working in Hindi *because* the menu is English — if this is localized, `tests/appium/nav.py`'s `go_menu()` must move to resource keys in the same change, exactly as `sidebar_key()` did, or the chromeless-recovery path breaks. *Acceptance:* both surfaces localized in `en` + `hi`, added to the `LocalizedFiles` registry so they are ratcheted, and the harness's `Go`-menu navigation updated in the same pass. · 2026-08-01 [*build-phase Cluster B] **DELIVERED.** All four menus and 18 items — plus the native dialogs those items raise, because a Hindi menu that opens an English alert is the same defect one click later — 36 caption keys, resolved through `IStringLocalizer` obtained from `IPlatformApplication.Current` (MAUI builds `MainPage` with `new`, so there is no constructor injection). **All FIVE auth screens** localized, not the two the row named — `AuthLayout`, `Login`, `Register`, `ForgotPassword`, `ResetPassword` are the same surface — and all five joined the registry. The raw `<input>` elements on /login and /register were left exactly as TR-011 requires (a real form POST is needed for the session cookie); only their text moved. **The coupling was honoured**: `nav.go_menu()` now takes a resource key and resolves the Go menu's OWN title too. ⚠ Sharp call worth recording: it kept `go_menu()` returning a **bool** rather than mirroring `sidebar_key()`'s tuple, because `recover_to_app()` writes `if nav.go_menu(...)` and a `(clicked, detail)` tuple is **TRUTHY ON A MISS** — which would have silently converted a failed recovery into a reported success. Proven offline against per-language fake trees: the OLD implementation returns False on a Hindi menu bar (the 11-screens-stranded break), the new one returns True in both. **Menu-bar guard**: a source scan (the net10.0 test project cannot reference the MAUI head), requiring every caption to match `^Menu[A-Za-z0-9]+$` AND exist in BOTH resx — "the resource namespace and the English language do not overlap". Accelerators pinned as an exact ordered 18-row table so a caption change cannot move ⌘, off App Settings (REQ-UI-049). Six mutations, ALL RED. 104 keys. · 2026-08-01 [*verify all §6 smoke, LIVE] ✅ **Menu bar CONFIRMED in Devanagari with zero tofu** — a different text renderer from the WebView, so this proves font fallback separately from page content: `फ़ाइल` (दस्तावेज़ इम्पोर्ट करें… · फ़ोल्डर इम्पोर्ट करें… · डेटा फ़ोल्डर दिखाएँ · लॉग फ़ोल्डर दिखाएँ), `जाएँ` (होम · RAG चैट · दस्तावेज़ इनजेशन · टोकन उपयोग · सेटिंग्स · RAG कॉन्फ़िगरेशन · LLM सेटिंग्स · डेटा और स्टोरेज), `मदद` (अपडेट जाँचें… · वर्शन और डेटा फ़ोल्डर · मेरा डेटा कहाँ है?). 🔴 **BUT THE SMOKE FOUND A REAL PRE-EXISTING DEFECT THE SOURCE SCAN CANNOT SEE — logged as REQ-UI-054**: the `View`/`दृश्य` menu is DECLARED at `MainPage.xaml.cs:149-165` and added to `MenuBarItems`, yet the runtime AX tree contains **zero** occurrences of `दृश्य`, `Zoom In`, `Zoom Out` or `Actual Size` in EITHER language. Its three accelerators (⌘+, ⌘−, ⌘0) do not exist at runtime. Cluster B's guard passes correctly — the code does declare them — and it said plainly that a source scan "does NOT prove that UIKit drew the strings". This is exactly that gap. Held at `Implemented 90%` for it. ⚠ Owner calls: `View → दृश्य` follows Apple's macOS Hindi menu register rather than this project's loanword register (`व्यू`); and `Register.razor`'s password hint moved from the `PasswordPolicy.Requirement` constant to a resource, so **the copy and the enforced rule can now drift**. | [view](#d-req-ui-052) |
| REQ-UI-053 | Sidebar nav links carry no AutomationId — UI-testability coding-standard defect (P2) | Blocked | 40% | 🔴 **MEASURED 2026-08-02 by `*build-phase` (Cluster D) — the open question is now answered with a number, not an inference: `nav.py ids` reports 0 of 23 sidebar link identifiers reaching the macOS accessibility tree on a freshly built Release head; every one reads `identifier=<empty>`.** This is what the README's desk evidence predicted and it had still been carried as an OPEN QUESTION. **Consequence: the whole sweep runs ENTIRELY on the resource-key label fallback** — confirmed in `sweep-results.json`, which records `navLabel: label=बैकअप और रीस्टोर` rather than an `id=nav-…` selector. That makes `REQ-NFR-014`'s no-English-selector guard **load-bearing today, not theoretical**: the label path is the only path, so any future localization that changes a label breaks navigation outright. README updated with the measured answer. · **Added 2026-08-01 by `*build-phase` — measured on the live app, not inferred.** Dumping `/source` from the running Catalyst head shows every sidebar `XCUIElementTypeLink` has `identifier=""`, and the anchor's `href` is not surfaced anywhere in the accessibility tree (the full attribute set is elementType, identifier, value, label, title, placeholderValue, enabled, selected, x/y/width/height — label/value/title all carry the display text and nothing else). Per `verify-phase.md §3b` and the MAUI UI-testability rule (day1 §4) **a missing `AutomationId` is a defect, not a licence**. Consequence today: `tests/appium/` can only locate nav links by DISPLAYED TEXT, which is localized — REQ-NFR-011 works around it by resolving labels from `AppStrings*.resx`, a workaround that survives a re-translation but **breaks on any resource-key rename** (loudly, via pre-flight, but it still breaks). *Acceptance:* every one of the 22 sidebar entries (`MainLayout.razor` `SidebarMenuButton`) carries a stable route-derived `AutomationId` (e.g. `nav-qdrant-admin`) surfaced as `identifier` in the macOS accessibility tree; `tests/appium/nav.py` can select every entry by identifier alone with no resource lookup. ⚠ Fixing this also removes the harness's dependency on the product's resource keys, which is the cleaner long-term contract. · 2026-08-01 [*build-phase Cluster C + orchestrator probe] 🔴 **NOT ACHIEVABLE FROM BLAZOR MARKUP — measured, not inferred. Status `Blocked` (platform/upstream), not `Implemented`.** The builder split the question and refused to claim the half it could not prove. **(a) Markup → DOM: PROVEN**, and without the app — `SidebarMenuButton` declares `AdditionalAttributes` (reflected over TrBlazeUI 1.0.7), and rendering it through `HtmlRenderer` in a scratchpad console app emitted `<a href="/qdrant-admin" … id="nav-qdrant-admin">`. **(b) DOM → macOS `identifier`: DISPROVEN by the orchestrator's live probe.** On a freshly built Release bundle carrying all 23 ids, with the shell settled (134 nodes, 50 sidebar links visible, breadcrumb correct), **`non-empty identifiers: 0`**, and the string `nav-` appears **ZERO times in the entire 125,915-character AX tree**. A real node reads `<XCUIElementTypeLink identifier="" value="" label="Qdrant Admin" …>` — the only carrier of meaning is the LOCALIZED display text. This matches the builder's desk evidence exactly: WebKit's Catalyst AX wrapper implements `accessibilityDOMIdentifier` (the DOM id) but NOT `accessibilityIdentifier`, which is what `XCUIElement.identifier` reads, and WebDriverAgentMac serves only the latter — so there is no second channel to ask down. **The 23 ids ship anyway** (harmless, correct in the DOM, and immediately useful if that ever changes) with `nav.sidebar_key()` trying identifier FIRST and falling back to the REQ-NFR-011 label lookup, so a partial or dead rollout cannot strand the harness; `sweep-results.json` now records WHICH selector carried each screen, making a regression visible rather than silent. ✅ Fallback proven: **23/23 screens swept clean in English AND Hindi** after the change. ⚠ The builder explicitly REJECTED `aria-label` as a handle because it would overwrite the accessible name and break REQ-NFR-005 "to serve a test" — the right call. **The next step is NATIVE, not another HTML attribute**: a WKWebView accessibility shim in the MAUI head, or extending `MainPage.BuildMenuBar`'s Go menu to all 22 routes (it hardcodes 8 and reaches none of the five `/workspace/{slug}/…`). The Go-menu route is cheap and locale-invariant but changes a user-visible menu — an owner decision. Was `Planned 0%`. | [view](#d-req-ui-053) |
| REQ-UI-054 | 🔴 The `View` menu is declared but never reaches the macOS menu bar (BRD-133, P2) | Implemented | 95% | **Added 2026-08-01 by the `*build-phase` §6 live smoke — found by looking at the running app, and invisible to every test we have.** `MainPage.xaml.cs:149-165` declares a fourth `MenuBarItem` (`Text("MenuView")`) with three items — Zoom In (`+`), Zoom Out (`-`), Actual Size (`0`) — and adds it to `MenuBarItems` alongside File, Go and Help. At runtime **it is not there**: the accessibility tree of the running Catalyst head contains **zero** occurrences of `दृश्य`, `Zoom In`, `Zoom Out` or `Actual Size`, in **either** language, on a freshly built Release bundle. The `View` menu visible in the tree is macOS's own (Show Toolbar / Customize Toolbar… / Show Sidebar / Enter Full Screen) and the app's three items are **not merged into it**. So three declared accelerators — **⌘+, ⌘− and ⌘0 — do not exist for the user**, and the zoom feature they expose is unreachable from the menu bar. ⚠ **This is PRE-EXISTING, not caused by REQ-UI-052's localization** — the English strings are equally absent, so the menu never shipped. ⚠ **It is also a precise illustration of a stated test limit rather than a test failure**: REQ-UI-052's `MenuBarLocalizationTests` is a source scan (the `net10.0` test project cannot reference the MAUI head, so `BuildMenuBar` cannot be CALLED), it correctly asserts that four menus are declared with proper resource keys, and its author wrote plainly that it "does NOT prove that UIKit drew the strings." This is exactly that gap, and only the live run could close it. *Likely cause to investigate first:* UIKit's menu builder de-duplicating or rejecting an app menu whose identifier/title collides with a system menu — macOS already owns a `View` menu — in which case the fix is a distinct `MenuBarItem` identifier or inserting into the existing system menu rather than adding a sibling. *Acceptance:* the app's zoom items appear in the running menu bar in both `en` and `hi` with ⌘+/⌘−/⌘0 operable, **verified on the live head** (a source scan cannot close this row), and a note recorded in `tests/appium/README.md` that menu-bar presence is only provable at runtime. · 2026-08-02 [*build-phase Cluster A] ✅ **FIXED AND VERIFIED LIVE IN BOTH LANGUAGES — and the root cause was NOT the title collision this row hypothesised.** 🔴 **UIKit hands every Mac Catalyst app a stock `Format ▸ Font ▸ Text Size` group owning `⌘+` (Bigger) and `⌘−` (Smaller), and `UIMenuBuilder` DISCARDS THE ENTIRE MENU that re-declares an owned key equivalent — silently, no exception, no log.** Proven by a throwaway probe build of three menus: one carrying only `⌘0` **was drawn**; one carrying only `⌘+` **was not**; one carrying a `⌘+` item *alongside four valid items including a shortcut-less one* **was not drawn either**. In Hindi the identifier sequence was the smoking gun — `td.menu.0`, `td.menu.1`, `td.menu.3` present, **`td.menu.2` missing**. Title collision is **disproven**: Hindi `दृश्य` collides with no `UIMenuIdentifier` and vanished all the same. Accidental confirmation during the work: moving App Settings from `⌘,` to `⌘;` (owned by *Check Document Now*) made the **whole Go menu** disappear. ⚠ Note the operative code is the repo's OWN `AppDelegate.BuildMenu`, not MAUI's `MenuBarHandler` — `MainPage.xaml.cs` is deliberately **unchanged**, because the declaration was always correct. **Fix:** `AppDelegate.SupersededStandardMenus = [UIMenuIdentifier.TextSize]` with a `RemoveMenu` loop at the top of every `BuildMenu` pass. The stock pair it removes was permanently **inert** in a `BlazorWebView` anyway — nothing implements `increaseSize:`/`decreaseSize:`, which is precisely why `zoom.js` could still see `⌘+`. 🔴 **THE ROW'S ORIGINAL IMPACT CLAIM WAS TOO STRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** The orchestrator recorded "three declared accelerators do not exist for the user". Measured on the running app by summing rendered element heights from the accessibility tree, **`⌘+`/`⌘−`/`⌘0` DID zoom whenever the web view had keyboard focus** — 3033 → **3716** on `⌘+`×3, → **2231** on `⌘−`×3, → 2863 on `⌘0`. What was genuinely wrong: (a) **zero discoverability** — all three items absent from the menu bar in both languages; and (b) a real but narrower functional gap — **on a fresh launch, app frontmost, before the user's first click into the content, all three were completely inert** (2860 → 2860 → 2860), which is exactly the fallback the code comment at `MainPage.xaml.cs:146-148` says the menu exists for. Post-fix that same no-click sequence gives 2860 → **3503** → 2860. **LIVE EVIDENCE, both languages, on the Release bundle with the language set BEFORE launch** (UIKit builds the menu bar once): EN shows `Zoom In ⌘+` / `Zoom Out ⌘−` / `Actual Size ⌘0` at the top of the View menu and `Format ▸ Font` no longer lists Bigger/Smaller; HI shows `दृश्य` restored as `td.menu.2` with `ज़ूम इन` / `ज़ूम आउट` / `वास्तविक आकार`, menu bar reading `फ़ाइल जाएँ दृश्य मदद` — screenshot inspected by the orchestrator. File/Go/Help and the full 18-row accelerator table unchanged; `⌘,` still opens App Settings (REQ-UI-049). **GUARD — a RUNTIME assertion, and deliberately not another source scan** (a source scan is what missed this): `tests/appium/menu_check.py` reads the DECLARATION out of `MainPage.xaml.cs` so it cannot drift from the product, resolves every caption through `strings.py` so it is language-independent, then checks the LIVE menu bar — titles and items from the AX tree, and **key equivalents from `AXMenuItemCmdChar`/`AXMenuItemCmdModifiers` via System Events, which WebDriverAgent does not expose at all** and which a screenshot cannot show. Exit 0/1/2. RED-proven three ways: reverting the fix (3 failures naming the missing items), moving an accelerator onto a taken key (`MENU MISSING: MenuGo`), and disabling the runtime accelerator emission while leaving the source declaration intact (all 14 accelerators flagged). ⚠ **`menu_check.py` is not wired into anything automatic** — it is the only check in the repo that can see this defect class, and `verify-phase` should probably call it. ⚠ Only `⌘+` and `⌘−` were true collisions; `Actual Size` (`⌘0`) was **collateral damage from sharing a menu with them** — worth remembering when reading the original report, which implied all three were individually rejected. Build green; 1,412 / 661 tests pass. Was `Planned 0%`. | [view](#d-req-ui-054) |
| REQ-UI-055 | Service-layer English backlog — 569 prose literals across 88 files (BRD-91, P3) | Implemented | 85% | **2026-08-02 [`*build-phase` Clusters B+E → `*verify all` inline] — ratchet lowered 330 → 189, and the arithmetic matters more than the number.** Only **118 of the 141** are conversions: Cluster B took its scope (Services/** minus Connectors/ and Scheduling/) from 240 → 198 (−42) and Cluster E took Connectors+Scheduling from 90 → 14 (−76). 🔴 **The other 23 were never debt at all.** `ServiceStringCoverage.MachineFacingCall` matched `logger\.Log…` but **not** `logger?.Log…`, so 23 pure log templates in `SingleInstanceGuard` (12), `InstallIdentityStore` (7) and `AppStartup` (4) had been counted as user-facing prose the whole time. Cluster B measured this and deliberately did NOT fix it (four clusters shared the file); the orchestrator widened the regex to `\??` and re-measured. **The count and its derivation are written into `ServiceEnglishCeiling`'s remarks so nobody later reads 141 as 141 conversions.** Slices converted: web ingestion −18 (`Services/Web/*` now at ZERO), support attachments −10, thread export −5, agent-editor refusals −4, dictation −3, flow-run refusal −2; plus Cluster E's connectors 65 / DatabaseMaintenanceJobHandler 7 / JobRunner 3 / SchedulerService 1. 68+40 = 108 resx keys added across BOTH languages, parity intact (2972/2972). **Live-proven in Hindi on the Catalyst head**: `/workspace/default/documents/web` renders Devanagari, an empty-URL Start renders `पता जाँचें / पहले एक URL डालें।`, and the **parameterized** refusal renders `'192.168.1.10' एक निजी-नेटवर्क पता है।…` with the host preserved in Latin inside the sentence — the format-with-arguments path proven end to end, not a bare key. Full 23-screen sweep clean at both widths. ⚠ **NOT `Verified`, deliberately.** A substantial part of the converted set was never observed rendering — the web-ingestion summary keys, the support-attachment slice, `ChatExport*`, `Agents*`, `DictationPermissionDenied` and `FlowsRunNeedsLlmProvider` are unit-proven in both languages but were never eyeballed, because two live ingest attempts were navigated away mid-run by a sibling cluster sharing the single mac2 session. 🔴 **And the verify sweep found English still rendering on the PRIMARY screen** — see `REQ-UI-059`; that is a different root cause (persisted transcript rows), but it means this class is not closed. ⚠ Of the 189 remaining, ~170 are argued deliberate with named evidence (77 model-facing tool descriptions/results, 6 persisted into the append-only `EventLog`, 15 SQL/backup, 12 ops exception text, 8 path-matching tokens, 7 posted to AppManager as wire payload) — **but see `REQ-UI-059`: the "model-facing" exclusion was disproven for at least the EgressGate family, which renders verbatim to the user.** · **Added 2026-08-02, split out of REQ-UI-051 because it is a body of work, not a remark.** REQ-UI-051 fixed the six *known* service-layer sites and, in doing so, measured the real size of the class: **569 English prose literals across 88 files under `apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/`**, a large share of them with a razor binding traced to a screen. They are invisible to every counter the project has — `RazorStringCoverage` scans markup, `CodeBlockStringCoverage` scans `@code` in registry components, and neither looks at the service layer. ✅ **The backlog is FROZEN, not fixed**: `ServiceStringCoverage.TheServiceLayerNeverGrowsMoreEnglish` ratchets it at exactly 569, so it cannot grow, and any new label fails with the file and strings named. **Confirmed-rendered clusters, in the builder's recommended order of user-visibility per unit of work:** **(1) Connectors** — `ConnectorSecretStore.CredentialStorageDescription` is interpolated **RAW** into an otherwise-localized page (`ConnectorsHub.razor:180,189`, `ConnectorEdit.razor:495,505`), plus `ConnectorRunReport.Summary`, `RagConnectorDocumentSink` item reasons, `ConnectorTypes` display names, `ConnectorSettings.Describe()/Validate()`, `ConnectorJobHandler`, `DatabaseConnectorResolver`. **(2) Scheduling** — `CronDescriber` is an entire file of prose (day/month names, ordinals, "every weekday") feeding `ScheduleText` on `Automations.razor:159,793`; plus `ScheduleInterpreter` refusals, `CronExpression` parse errors, `RunConditions` skip reasons. **(3) Licensing** — `LicenseStatus`/`LicenseService.Message` renders in the **always-visible MainLayout banner** (`:524`); `InstanceModeResolver` in `LicenseStatusCard:121`; `FeatureGateService` denial reasons in `FeatureGate.razor:28`. **(4)** `BackupService.BlockDetail` (`BackupRestore.razor:212` — the title is localized and the body is not), `DockerContainerService.FailureReason`, `DockerDaemonEndpoint.SecurityWarning`/`KindLabel` (QdrantAdmin), `SingleInstanceState.Describe()`. **(5) Leaf files**: `UploadTypePolicy`, `BrandingSettings.DefaultWelcomeMessage`, `FileManagerReveal`, `PromoCodeValidator`, `DocumentSizeDisplay`, `AppSettingsChangeLog` field labels, `SpeechText` (**spoken aloud**), `ThreadExporter` (headings inside the exported `.md`), `AccentPalette`, `SupportedLanguages` English names. **(6) Agent skill results that land in the chat transcript and trace**: `FileOperationsSkill`/`Sandbox`, `SqlQueryGuard`/`SqlQuerySkill`, `ChartGenerateSkill`, `WebSearchSkill`, `WebScrapeSkill`, `EgressGate` (which also quotes a UI toggle label verbatim), `McpEgressGuard`; plus `FlowGuardrailCatalog`'s runtime `GuardrailDecision.Block(...)` reasons at `:64,73`, which appear in the run trace even though its *descriptions* are localized by id. *Acceptance:* the confirmed-rendered clusters localized in priority order with the ratchet lowered as each lands; the wire vocabulary provably unchanged at each step (tool names, AppManager codes, enum tokens, paths — the `QdrantAdmin` trap where a bound value WAS the English label must not recur); and machine-facing English (LLM-facing prompt text, developer exceptions, SQL) explicitly classified rather than translated — grow `ServiceStringCoverage.MachineText` (capped at 12, in-use-checked) rather than raising the ceiling. ⚠ **Do not treat the 569 as a single number to drive to zero** — it deliberately counts machine-facing prose too, because the guard cannot tell the two apart. The deliverable is the *user-visible* subset. · 2026-08-02 [*build-phase, 6 parallel clusters] ✅ **DELIVERED — service-layer literals 569 → 330, a 239-literal reduction, and the ratchet is lowered to 330 so the gain cannot be given back.** All six confirmed-rendered clusters converted by the REQ-UI-051 pattern (services return invariant KEYS; the surface resolves them): **connectors** −32 (incl. the named worst site, `ConnectorSecretStore.StorageDescription`, interpolated RAW into four otherwise-localized alerts), **scheduling** −62, **licensing** −38, **backup/Docker/single-instance** −62, **leaf services** −18, **agent skills** −3. ~250 keys added to both `.resx` files, parity intact at 2,864 entries. **Highest-visibility fix**: `LicenseStatus.Message` at `MainLayout.razor:524` — the always-visible shell banner, English on *every screen* of a Hindi install the moment AppManager became unreachable. 🔴 **THE HARDEST PROBLEM WAS `CronDescriber`, and it was solved properly rather than mechanically**: Hindi reverses three of the joins (trailing बजे where English leads with "at"; months *before* the day phrase; no ordinal ending on a date), so fragment-gluing would have produced something no Hindi speaker would say. It now composes from **whole localizable patterns with numbered placeholders**, leaving only 26 bare nouns where word order cannot be wrong — `0 7 * * 1-5` reads `हर कार्यदिवस 07:00 बजे`. There is deliberately **no English default overload**, so a caller that forgets a localizer cannot silently render English. ⚠ A known infelicity is **shipped and recorded in the code** (a multi-date list repeats तारीख़ once per date) because fixing it needs a translator's judgement, not more code. **Several traps were avoided by evidence rather than luck**: the agent-skills slice found the *line* that decides the audience split (`AgentLoopRunner.cs:126` — `ToolResult.Content` enters the model's context, `ErrorMessage` never does) and left ~45 model-facing literals English on that basis; it also caught that `FlowGuardrailCatalog`'s output-stage reasons reach BOTH audiences via `AgentToolHandler.cs:200`, so a Hindi reason would have leaked into the model's context. The Docker slice proved `Uri`/`ClientUri`/`Display`/scheme byte-identical across cultures **and** asserted the label DOES differ, so a silent lookup miss cannot pass as success. The leaf slice found the **Turkish-i trap** — `PromoCodeValidator` used `ToUpper()`, so under `tr-TR` a correctly-typed `india-2026` became `İNDİA-2026` and the payment service rejected it. 🔴 **It also found a live pre-existing defect localization would have worsened**: `MauiReadAloudService` called `SpeakAsync(text)` with **no `SpeechOptions`**, so the synthesiser used the OS language, not the app's — Hindi answers were already being silently skipped by an English voice. Now gated on a new `CanSpeakAsync(culture)` matched on the neutral language, falling back to **English speech even in a Hindi process**, because shipping unspeakable text is worse than English. **Three mutations came back GREEN and were reported rather than hidden**: a sync-provider name (coverage hole, +2 tests), an entitlement comparer (ICU collation still matched `BUSINESS`→`Business` under `tr-TR`, so a structural assertion on the comparer was added), and — most instructive — **a `.resx` mutation applied via a shell heredoc that silently did not modify the file at all** (UTF-8 mangling), which would fake a RED-proof invisibly. ⚠ **65 connector literals were deliberately DEFERRED**, correctly: they feed `ConnectorException` → `ScheduleRun.FailureReason`, and `Automations.razor` renders `Reason`/`FailureReason`/`Detail` **RAW** for every job kind, so emitting keys there would print `ConnectorRunTailFailed` on screen — worse than English. The unblock is one line (bind the Automations grid to a live `CronDescriber.Describe(...)` instead of the stored `ScheduleText`) and is recorded under REQ-UI-056. ⚠ **`ScheduleRunItem.Reason` / `ScheduleRun.FailureReason` / `ScheduleRun.Detail` are PERSISTED English rows**; any future conversion needs a permanent key-or-legacy-text fallback for rows already on disk. Found independently by two clusters. · 2026-08-02 [*verify all §6 smoke] ✅ **23/23 screens swept clean in Hindi at both widths**, node counts matching the English run. ⚠ `workspace-chat` reported one narrow-width overlap (`यह जवाब पढ़कर सुनाएँ` × `संलग्न करें`, 1023 px², tagged *visible*) — **inspected and judged a trap-#5 PHANTOM**: the screenshot shows Attach fully visible and unobstructed, and the read-aloud button belongs to a transcript message clipped behind the composer, which the AX tree reports at its unclipped position. `outside_window()` cannot catch it because both are inside the window rect. ⚠ **The licence banner could NOT be smoked** — it renders only when a licence message exists, which needs a reachable AppManager; a live probe found **zero** occurrences of any licence text on this install. It is proven by test through a real `IStringLocalizer` in both cultures, but **nobody has seen it on screen**. Held at `Implemented 75%`: 330 literals remain, of which a substantial share is deliberate machine-facing text and the rest is tracked below. | [view](#d-req-ui-055) |
| REQ-UI-056 | Unblock the deferred connector/scheduling strings + persisted-English policy (BRD-91, P3) | Implemented | 90% | ✅ **2026-08-02 — OWNER DECIDED THE POLICY, and the decision was made on measured evidence rather than the framing this row carried.** The orchestrator queried the real installed DB before asking: `ScheduleRun` held **5 rows** (all with `Detail`, **ZERO** with `FailureReason` — the column this row named as the main concern) and `ScheduleRunItem` held **17** reasons, all 22 written in a two-day window and all of them synthetic fixtures (`req-fn-042-*@techiedesk.example`). The "permanent legacy burden" was 22 disposable test rows on one dev machine, so the cost of deciding correctly was ~zero — which is exactly why it was worth deciding now rather than after ship. 🔴 **This row's proposed fix would NOT have worked.** It says "emit a resource key from the connector side"; four of the five distinct stored strings carry live data (`"2 ingested of 2 listed"`, `"Added to workspace {guid}"`, `"Unchanged since the previous run (version {x})"`), and a bare key loses the numbers. **DECISION: store a stable CODE plus its ARGUMENTS and render at display time; a row with no code prints its stored text verbatim, permanently.** Built by Cluster E: new `JobMessage` (ordered segments, each either code+args or verbatim text; arguments captured as InvariantCulture text so a stored row means the same thing to every later reader), three NEW NULLABLE columns (`ScheduleRun.DetailJson`, `ScheduleRun.FailureReasonJson`, `ScheduleRunItem.ReasonJson`) via migration `0009-RunHistoryMessageCodes.sql`, and **both halves always written** — the codes AND the English rendering — because the scheduler helper logs `run.Detail` and support reads these rows in a DB browser. `JobMessage.Render(storedText, storedJson, localize)` IS the policy; its null-code branch is documented as permanent. **RED-PROVEN**: dropping the `?? storedText` fallback fails 6 tests across both culture arms. **Migration applied to the owner's installed DB at launch — all 22 legacy rows kept their exact text with NULL json, zero rows modified.** Also delivered: the one-line unblock both prior clusters identified — `Automations.razor:159` now calls live `CronDescriber.TryDescribe(row.CronExpression, Localize)` instead of the stored `ScheduleText`, and the same treatment was extended to the "What it does" column via `DescribeAction`. Also corrected: `RunConditions`' skip reasons were being localized AT WRITE TIME by REQ-UI-055, so a Hindi user who switched to English read their own history in a language they had stopped using. 76 literals converted; 14 remain in the two directories, all machine-facing (launchd plist fragments, `schtasks`, `pmset`/`airport` tokens). **Live-proven in Hindi**: (a) the schedule column renders `हर 5 मिनट में` while the DB stores English — definitively the live path; (c) legacy run 5's dialog prints `5 ingested of 5 listed` VERBATIM inside Hindi chrome — the fallback doing its job. ⚠ **NOT `Verified`**: smoke (b), a NEW coded row, is proven in the database (`ScheduleRun` #6 carries code+args written by the real `JobRunner`) and by test, but its screenshot was defeated by Appium session contention. ⚠ Interface changes are deliberately source-breaking (`JobRunResult.Failed(string)` is gone) so English cannot keep compiling. 🔴 **The same defect class was then found in the chat transcript** → `REQ-UI-059`. · **Added 2026-08-02, split out of REQ-UI-055 because it is blocked on a decision, not on effort.** The connectors slice converted 32 literals and **deliberately deferred 65 more**, correctly: they feed `ConnectorException.Message` → `JobRunResult.Failed` → `ScheduleRun.FailureReason`, and `Automations.razor` renders `item.Reason` (:560), `FailureReason` (:537) and `Detail` (:533) **RAW for every job kind** — so emitting a resource key from the connector side would print the literal `ConnectorRunTailFailed` on that screen, which is worse than the English it replaced. **The unblock is one line**, and the scheduling cluster identified it independently: bind the Automations schedule column to a live `CronDescriber.Describe(CronExpression.Parse(row.CronExpression), Localize)` rather than the stored `Schedule.ScheduleText`, keeping the stored text purely as the confirmation audit record BRD-140 intends it to be. The API is already shaped for it. 🔴 **BUT A POLICY DECISION IS REQUIRED FIRST, and it is the reason this is its own row**: `ScheduleRunItem.Reason`, `ScheduleRun.FailureReason` and `ScheduleRun.Detail` are **persisted English rows in the database**. Rows already written cannot be re-rendered in another language, so any conversion needs a **permanent key-or-legacy-text fallback** in the renderer — forever, not transitionally. Two clusters found this independently and neither would decide it unilaterally. ⚠ Also blocked on the same seam: `DatabaseMaintenanceJobHandler` (7 literals — its `DisplayName` leaks into the interpreter's refusal sentence, so a Hindi refusal currently contains an English action name) and `JobRunner` (3), because localizing either means changing `IScheduledJobHandler`, which `ConnectorJobHandler` also implements — it crosses a cluster boundary by construction. *Acceptance:* the persisted-English policy decided and written down; the Automations grid bound to a live describe; the 65 connector literals and the two job-handler files converted; the ratchet lowered accordingly. | [view](#d-req-ui-056) |
| REQ-UI-057 | ⚠ `ConnectorEndToEndTests` port-binding flake makes the suite non-deterministic (P3) | Verified | 100% | ✅ **VERIFIED 2026-08-02 (`*build-phase` Cluster A → `*verify all` inline). Acceptance met literally: 20 consecutive full-suite runs, 1,549 passed, ZERO occurrences.** 🔴 **The recorded hypothesis was WRONG and is corrected here.** This row guessed "the fixture binds a fixed port, or disposes without waiting for the listener to release". Neither. The cause is that `Dispose()` called `listener.Stop()` **and then** `listener.Close()`: on .NET's managed `HttpListener` on Unix BOTH unregister the prefix, and the second pass runs through `HttpEndPointManager.RemovePrefixInternal` → `GetEPListener`, which **RE-BINDS the port** when the endpoint has left the static map. A host that had already surrendered its port took it back purely in order to release it again, and threw if anything had been handed that port in the gap. Proven three independent ways: a real stack trace captured from a live suite failure; a forced-interleaving harness that fails 100% with `Stop();Close()` and passes 100% with `Close()` alone; and message forensics showing the rival is always a plain socket (errno 48 "Address already in use"), never another `HttpListener` ("conflicts with an existing registration"). 🔴 **That third strand also disproves the shared-collection fix this row offered** — the rival is another fixture's `TcpListener` probe, never a second `FakeGitHubHost`, which has exactly one caller whose tests already run one at a time. Fix: `Close()` once in teardown (the proven change), plus `StartOnFreeLoopbackPort()` retry hardening for the same defect in the constructor (8/800 losses under a forced 5 ms window → 0/800 with retry; **never observed in a real run** — reported as hardening, not as the cause). New permanent detector `ConcurrentLoopbackHostsTakeDistinctPortsAndReleaseThemOnDisposal` builds 24 hosts concurrently and asserts every port re-binds after disposal; proven non-vacuous by removing `listener.Close()` (red, all 24 ports still held). ⚠ **Honest caveat: 40 baseline full-suite runs BEFORE any change produced 0 occurrences** — the flake could not be reproduced from the pre-existing suite on this machine; it surfaced only once the new concurrency test amplified it (3-in-20). ⚠ **Six other fixtures use the same stop-then-close pattern** and were left untouched (not Cluster A's files) → `REQ-NFR-016`. ⚠ A **second, unrelated flake** was found in passing: `SessionStoreTests.cs:33` asserts a 43-char random base64url handle does not contain `UserId.ToString()`, and `UserId` is `123` — it fails by chance whenever "123" appears, observed live → also `REQ-NFR-016`. · **Added 2026-08-02 — test infrastructure, not a product defect, and logged because it has now cost four separate runs.** `ConnectorEndToEndTests` intermittently throws `HttpListenerException: Address already in use` from `FakeGitHubHost`'s teardown. **Independently reported by three build clusters** (connectors, leaf-services, agent-skills — two naming `AnAnonymousConnectorSendsNoAuthorizationHeader`, one naming `AMissingRepositoryFailsWithAReadableReason`) and reproduced once by the orchestrator, which then saw two clean full runs and 77/77 in isolation. 🔴 **The real cost is not the failure, it is the habit**: a suite that fails once and passes on re-run teaches everyone to re-run rather than investigate, which is precisely how a genuine regression gets waved through. It also forced two clusters to spend time attributing a red suite to someone else's work. *Likely cause:* the fixture binds a fixed port, or disposes without waiting for the listener to release, so two test classes racing in the same collection collide. *Acceptance:* the fixture binds an ephemeral port and releases it deterministically (or the affected classes share one xUnit collection so they cannot run concurrently); proven by 20 consecutive full-suite runs with zero occurrences. | [view](#d-req-ui-057) |

The app-side consumer `REQ-UI-058` stays here.** Prior remark retained: **Added and built 2026-08-02 by `*build-phase` (Cluster C).** Carried in the Deferred list for days with the note *"No cluster owns it"* — it does now. When a flow guardrail BLOCKS a tool call, the sentence the user sees originates in the *library*, which has no localization, so a Hindi user got an English refusal at exactly the moment something went wrong. 22 user-reachable literal sites (20 distinct sentences) across `FlowRunner` (15), `FlowGuardrailChain` (4) and `GuardedToolHandler` (3). **Approach: extended the EXISTING `FlowValidationCodes` precedent rather than inventing a second mechanism** — new `FlowMessage` record (Code, Format, Arguments, Text) + `FlowMessageCodes` with 20 constants, the run-time counterpart of validation's before-it-runs codes. Codes carry ARGUMENTS (a bare key cannot render a message containing a tool name); Text renders from Format with InvariantCulture so the English fallback and the argument list can never drift. **NO BREAKING NuGet CHANGE**: `GuardrailVerdict.Message` was added as an init-only property in the record BODY, deliberately not as a 5th positional parameter, which would have changed primary-constructor and `Deconstruct` arity and broken any consumer constructing or deconstructing positionally; every existing English string is byte-identical, asserted by `TheEnglishEveryExistingConsumerReadsIsUnchanged`. 15 new tests, all reading codes off steps a REAL `FlowRunner` emitted rather than off a factory, because the claim is that the code REACHES the surface a host renders. Library suite 676/676. **The defect was photographed live in Hindi** — an all-Devanagari flow screen with `"Blocked by guardrail 'compliance-check' at Input."` in English. ⚠ **NOT `Verified`, and the honest reason is that this is half a feature: the 20 codes currently have NO CONSUMER.** The app-side mapping lives in `AgentTrace.cs` / `WorkspaceFlows.razor`, which belong to another cluster's scope, so a Hindi user still sees English until `REQ-UI-058` lands. Cluster C stayed inside its boundary rather than reaching across and colliding with a sibling — the right call, but it means the user-visible half is outstanding. ⚠ 14 `AgentToolHandler` literals were excluded as model-facing and documented as such; **that exclusion was later disproven at the render seam** — see `REQ-UI-059`. | [view](#requirements-status) |
| REQ-NFR-014 | Verification-harness guards against three recurring failure classes (P2) | Verified | 100% | ✅ **VERIFIED 2026-08-02 (`*build-phase` Cluster D → `*verify all` inline). All three guards written, 12/12 planted mutations came back RED, 8/8 green, and `menu_check.py` was run live in BOTH shipped languages during this very verify run.** Each guard closes a failure that had ALREADY happened more than once. **(1) `SidebarSweepCoverageTests`** — parses every `<SidebarMenuButton>` out of `MainLayout.razor` and set-compares against `run_sweep.SIDEBAR` and `nav.NAV_IDS` in BOTH directions. Written in C# not Python, deliberately: a Python check only runs when somebody runs the sweep, and *the sweep is the thing that is incomplete*, so a guard inside it is guarded by its own subject. This is the `/settings/backup` failure — a screen that shipped for days and was **never graded**, while the harness reported all-clean over a list that did not contain it. RED-proof included planting a 24th real button in `MainLayout.razor` (restored, SHA-256 verified), not merely deleting a table row. **(2) `HarnessSelectorLiteralTests`** — the pattern that has now bitten THREE times: localizing a surface silently invalidates a harness selector. It never asks "does this look English" (a heuristic fires on `XCUIElementTypeLink` and `mac2`); it asks the product one factual question — *is this exact string something the app displays?* — comparing 745 harness literals against 2,570 displayed values. Run against the REQ-UI-052 change it fails the exact commit that should have been caught. **(3) `menu_check.py` wired into `verify-phase` §4c** with its exit-code contract (0 MENU-OK / 1 MENU-FAIL / **2 REFUSED-is-not-a-pass**) and the run-in-both-languages requirement, because an English menu title matching a `UIMenuIdentifier` merges into the stock menu while a Hindi one becomes its own top-level menu — two different `AppDelegate.BuildMenu` paths. Prior: | Implemented | 100% | ✅✅ **VERSION STAMP ADDED 2026-08-04 — the last acceptance clause is now met, so a stale corpus is DETECTED instead of trusted.** `IEmbeddingProvider.EmbeddingSignature` publishes `{provider}/{model}/r{revision}` (embedded provider = `Embedded-ONNX/bge-m3/r2`); ingestion stamps it on every document through the new `DocumentMetadataKeys.EmbeddingSignature`, written by a single `EmbedAndStampAsync` helper so a future ingest path cannot embed without stamping; `EmbeddingStaleness.Analyze` compares a stored corpus against the current signature; `ITechieRag.DetectStaleEmbeddingsAsync` exposes it. **The revision is the load-bearing part** — this defect changed neither provider nor model, so a provider/model comparison would have missed it entirely. Three judgement calls worth recording: an **UNSTAMPED document is stale, not "probably fine"** (the pre-2026-08-04 corpus is precisely the population this exists for); a provider publishing no signature reports **`IsDeterminable = false`, never a clean result** — an empty stale list from a check that never ran would repeat the lying-surface pattern; and **`IsMixed` is distinguished from `IsEntirelyStale`**, because a half-finished re-ingest leaves two incomparable spaces in one store and is the worse, less visible condition. Added as **default interface members** so external implementations keep compiling (REQ-NFR-007), and implemented explicitly on `TechieRagClient`/`TechieRagManager` since a default member is unreachable from the concrete type. **18 new tests, all passing** — 12 on the analysis and 6 end-to-end against a REAL `SqliteVecStore`, because the round trip is where `FileSize` was silently dropped once (TR-RAG-038) and asserting the chunk dictionary alone would have proved nothing. ✅ **AND IT HAS A CONSUMER:** the document library renders a localized warning (en + hi) naming how many documents are affected, plus an extra line for the mixed case — computed from the catalog the page already fetches, so it costs no extra query. A detection API nobody calls detects nothing, which is the mistake `REQ-RAG-050` made and `REQ-UI-058` is still paying for. Solution suite **2,282 pass / 0 fail**. 🔶 **Owner action unchanged: re-ingest the whole corpus.** The banner will now tell you when it is done — and will say so more loudly if only part of it is. ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-04 (`*build-phase`, RAG cluster) on the owner's explicit decision to re-ingest.** `EmbeddedEmbeddingProvider.GenerateEmbedding` now applies its own `ToModelId` (fairseq `+1`, spm `0` → `<unk>`=3) and wraps the sequence in `<s>` … `</s>` — it previously had **neither**, feeding raw ids with no special tokens at all. Two slots are reserved before truncation so a maximum-length input cannot overflow the sequence limit; `tokenCount` now reports what the model actually processed, wrapper included. **Measured on the same probe that found it: cross-lingual similarity 0.3536 (LOSING to 0.3642) → 0.7182** — noise-with-the-wrong-winner to a genuine multilingual match; same-language retrieval unchanged, as expected. `LiveEmbeddedTokenizerDiagnosticTests` now runs **by default** whenever the BGE-M3 weights are staged (it was opt-in only while the decision was outstanding), and asserts a real threshold rather than mere ordering — "higher" was too weak a claim when the broken values differed in the third decimal. The two multi-GB ONNX test classes were moved into one non-parallel collection (`OnnxModelCollection`), since ~4.5 GB of concurrently-resident weights is its own hazard; solution suite **2,264 pass / 0 fail** across 3 consecutive runs. 🔶 **OWNER ACTION REQUIRED — RE-INGEST THE WHOLE CORPUS, DO NOT TOP UP.** Vectors embedded before this change are in a different space from ones embedded after; cosine similarity between them is meaningless. A partial re-ingest silently mixes both and degrades retrieval with nothing in the logs. ⚠ **Deferred, and the reason this is 90% not 100%:** there is still **no version stamp on stored vectors**, so nothing DETECTS an old-code corpus — the guard against a partial re-ingest is procedure, not code. That was the one acceptance clause not met, and it is the sensible follow-up. ⚠ Also note this retroactively qualifies `REQ-RAG-002`/`REQ-RAG-005`'s "runtime-confirmed" remarks: the embedder ran, but not correctly, until today. Prior: | Planned | 0% | **Added 2026-08-03 by `*build-phase` (RAG cluster), found by fixing `REQ-RAG-025` and then asking whether the sibling had the same bug.** It does. `EmbeddedEmbeddingProvider.GenerateEmbedding` passes `tokenizer.EncodeToIds(...)` straight into the graph — **no fairseq `+1` offset, and no `<s>`/`</s>` wrapper at all**, which BGE-M3's pooling expects. Same root cause as **TR-RAG-044**, in the provider that is the **desktop default** and produced **every vector currently in the store**. **Measured, not inferred (2026-08-03):** Hindi query `फ्रांस की राजधानी क्या है?` → cosine **0.3536** to *"Paris is the capital city of France."* vs **0.3642** to *"Bicycles should have their chains oiled regularly."* — the irrelevant passage WINS, and both sit at noise level (a genuine BGE-M3 cross-lingual match is ~0.7+). English ranks correctly, exactly as the reranker did while broken, because a consistent id shift preserves lexical-overlap signal. ⚠ **NOT FIXED, deliberately — this is an owner decision.** Correcting it changes every vector the provider produces, so **every embedding already ingested becomes incompatible and the whole corpus must be re-ingested**. That trade — materially better retrieval, especially multilingual, against a full re-index — is not a build's call to make. *Acceptance:* `ToModelId` mirrored into the embedder plus the `<s>`/`</s>` wrapper; the cross-lingual diagnostic passes; a stored-vector version stamp so an old-code corpus is DETECTED rather than silently mixed with new vectors; re-ingestion documented in the UsageGuide. **Executable evidence exists and is opt-in:** `LiveEmbeddedTokenizerDiagnosticTests` (skipped by default so a machine with BGE-M3 cached does not go red on a known-open defect; `TechieRagEmbedderDiagnostics=1` runs it, and the skip reason carries the numbers). ⚠ Note this also puts an asterisk on `REQ-RAG-002`/`REQ-RAG-005`'s "runtime-confirmed" remarks: the embedder ran, but not correctly. | [view](#d-req-rag-052) |
| REQ-NFR-016 | Residual test-suite determinism + single-session harness contention (P3) | Planned | 0% | **Added 2026-08-02 by `*build-phase`, collecting three findings that are the same hazard `REQ-UI-057` was raised for — a suite that fails once and passes on re-run teaches people to re-run rather than investigate.** **(1) Six fixtures share the exact stop-then-close defect** `REQ-UI-057` root-caused, untouched because they were not Cluster A's files: `LiveWebIngestionEndToEndTests.cs:340`, `HttpConnectorTransportTests.cs:363`, `HttpWebContentFetcherGuardTests.cs:160`, `LiveSsrfGuardTests.cs:177`, `FlowOrchestrationEgressTests.cs:213`, `TechieRagTelemetryExporterTests.cs`. Any `HttpListener` on Unix that calls `Stop()` then `Close()` re-binds its own port in order to release it. **(2) `SessionStoreTests.cs:33`** — `HandleIsOpaqueAndCarriesNoTokenMaterial` asserts a 43-char random base64url handle does not contain `UserId.ToString()`, and `UserId` is **`123`**; it fails by pure chance whenever "123" appears in the handle, observed live once in 40 runs. Fix is a GUID user id, **not** a loosened assertion — loosening it would delete the check the test exists for. **(3) `mac2` allows exactly ONE Appium session**, so concurrent cluster smokes are mutually destructive: this pass cost Cluster B two live ingest observations and Cluster E one screenshot, and Cluster C measured its session being recreated roughly every 25 seconds. A shared lock file — like the `REQ-FN-051` one `run_sweep` already pre-flights — would turn "session died" into "waiting for the host". ⚠ Orchestrator note: the fan-out that caused (3) was an orchestration error, not a harness defect; the harness behaved correctly by aborting rather than grading a wandered screen. *Acceptance:* all six fixtures close once; `SessionStoreTests` uses a non-colliding id; a harness lock serializes smoke sessions; 20 consecutive full-suite runs clean. | [view](#requirements-status) |
| REQ-FN-054 | Own repository; `TechieRag`, `TechieRag.Embedded`, `TechieRag.Agents` consumed as pinned NuGet packages — no `ProjectReference` to a library project (BRD-146, P1) | Not Started | 0% | Added 2026-09-03 by `*amend-docs` (owner decision). ⚠ Partly OWNER-RUN (new repository, history move — git). Library side is TechieRag `REQ-FN-006`. Plan: `docs/TechieRag.Agents-Proposal.md` §10. | [view](#d-req-fn-054) |
| REQ-RAG-053 | Agent runtime is `TechieRag.Agents` (Microsoft Agent Framework): `@handle` turns, named agents, flow agent nodes and scheduled agent runs; `rag-search` + system prompt on the library's agentic retrieval contract; `list_documents` under the same permission (BRD-147, P4) | Not Started | 0% | Added 2026-09-03 by `*amend-docs`. Depends on `TechieRag.Agents` ≥ 1.0.0-preview carrying TechieRag `REQ-RAG-015/016/017`. Existing agent rows (`REQ-RAG-021/022/023`, `REQ-UI-034/045`, `REQ-NFR-013`) keep their status; this row carries the re-verification of the runtime switch. | [view](#d-req-rag-053) |

**Status values:** `Not Started` · `In Progress` · `Implemented` (code done, not yet verified) · `Verified` (self-smoke or verifier PASS — acceptance AND data-render AND visual gates all pass) · `Done (pre-existing)` (migrated from an earlier dev plan as already complete — build agents must NOT rebuild; terminal like `Verified`) · `Needs re-verify` (a defect or change was logged — must be re-run before it can return to `Verified`) · `PARTIAL` (some acceptance unmet — say what in Remarks) · `FAIL` (verifier ran and failed — bug in Remarks) · `Blocked` (external/library gap — link the TR-/TR-RAG- entry in Remarks) · `N/A`.

**% guide:** `0` not started · `25` scaffolded · `50` in progress · `75` implemented-unverified · `100` verified.

**Remarks:** date + what was done / what is missing / bug or library reference. This is the home for bugs and change notes — do not spawn a separate file. Visual-gate failures are prefixed `⚠ visual:`; security findings `⚠ SECURITY`.

- **REQ-UI-041** — Native desktop shell (BRD-133, P1, added 2026-07-26): window with a 1024 × 720 minimum size, native menu bar with standard shortcuts, OS file/folder pickers for ingestion (replacing browser drag-drop upload), and a data/storage settings surface that shows the data directory, its disk usage, and a reveal-in-Finder/Explorer action. ~~⚠ No mockup exists for the data/storage surface — run `*mockups TechieDesk --update` before building.~~ **Corrected 2026-07-26 [REQ-UI-041]:** the mockup exists — `docs/mockups/data-storage.html` was created in the round-2 mockup pass and was the build contract.
  - **2026-07-26 [REQ-UI-041] — Implemented (75%), self-smoked.** Files: `apps/TechieDesk/App.xaml.cs`, `apps/TechieDesk/MainPage.xaml.cs`, `apps/TechieDesk/Platforms/MacCatalyst/AppDelegate.cs`, `apps/TechieDesk/DesktopFolderPicker.cs` (new), `apps/TechieDesk/DesktopImportQueue.cs` (new), `apps/TechieDesk/Components/Pages/DataStorage.razor` (new), `apps/TechieDesk/Components/Pages/Ingestion.razor`, `apps/TechieDesk/Components/Pages/DocumentLibrary.razor`, `apps/TechieDesk.Core/Services/Storage/*` (new). Tests: `tests/TechieDesk.Tests/Storage/` (24, all green; proven RED by three behaviour-removal mutations — dropping the remainder row, disabling recursive measurement, and dropping `open -R`'s reveal flag turned 6 of them red).
  - **Window floor.** `MinimumWidth`/`MinimumHeight` on the MAUI `Window` are the portable declaration and drive the Windows head, but they never reach a Mac Catalyst window — measured, not assumed: raising them to 1360 × 880 moved the opened window from 1024 × 768 to 1048 × 768, nowhere near the floor requested. `MainPage.OnHandlerChanged` therefore also sets the window scene's `sizeRestrictions.minimumSize`, compensating for Catalyst's 1.3 UIKit-points-per-macOS-point iPad-idiom scaling, without which a 1024-point floor would only hold the window at ~788 macOS points. Measured with `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`: a 1360 × 880 floor held the macOS window at 1026 × 768; the shipped 1024 × 720 floor holds it at exactly 1024 × 768.
  - **Menu bar.** .NET MAUI 10.0.20 does not surface `Page.MenuBarItems` on Mac Catalyst at all — with three populated `MenuBarItem`s and a handler attached, the tree UIKit assembled contained only the stock Application/File/Edit/Format/View/Window/Help set, on both the launch pass and a forced rebuild. `AppDelegate.BuildMenu` now translates the declared model into UIKit menus (merging by title into the standard macOS menus rather than adding a second "File"), and `MainPage` marks the menu system as needing a rebuild once the page has a handler. The resulting real menu tree was dumped at runtime and carries every item with its modifier flags.
  - **OS pickers.** `FilePicker` comes from `Microsoft.Maui.Storage`; there is no `FolderPicker` in MAUI Essentials 10.0.20 (its Mac Catalyst assembly exposes `FilePickerImplementation` and nothing folder-shaped), so `DesktopFolderPicker` wraps `UIDocumentPickerViewController` restricted to `UTTypes.Folder`. The browser drag-drop `<FileUpload>` is gone from the document library; picked files are ingested from the path the user chose instead of being streamed out of the WebView and re-staged in a temp directory under their original name.
  - **Not exercised.** No menu item was clicked and no picker panel was opened: macOS denies `osascript` assistive access on this host (error −25211) and no Appium harness exists. The click-through of every menu command, the Finder/Explorer reveal, the folder panel and the ingestion of a picked file remain UNPROVEN by execution — only their construction and their non-UI logic are covered.
  - **Deviation from the mockup.** The mockup's "Backup & reset" card (Back up now… / Restore from backup… / Delete all local data…) is NOT built. Those are three unbuilt features, and three buttons that do nothing are worse than none; the card is replaced by a Diagnostics card whose two buttons both work. Library gap logged: TR-027 (`BadgeVariant` has no success/warning tone, so the mockup's green "Healthy" badge ships as an outline badge).

Extends the existing `/qdrant-admin` connection card (REQ-UI-003). ~~⚠ Needs a mockup.~~ **Corrected 2026-07-26 [REQ-UI-042]:** the mockup exists — `docs/mockups/qdrant-admin.html` carries the Docker-daemon card and was the build contract.
  - **2026-07-26 [REQ-UI-042] — Implemented (75%), self-smoked.** "Docker daemon" card added to `/qdrant-admin` above the Qdrant connection card: endpoint-kind Select + address Input + Test connection + Use this daemon, TLS-verification Switch, active-daemon tiles (endpoint · daemon version · host OS · last checked), TLS-&-credentials badges, honest-failure Alert naming the endpoint tried, plain-TCP/unverified-TLS warning Alert, and the standing root-on-host warning. Container table gained Restart + View logs; page head gained Pull latest image.

  - *Acceptance:* changing the provider changes the visible field set — no union-of-all-providers form; every field the chosen provider requires is marked required; Save is refused while any required field is empty, with the error named on the offending field.

Tests: `tests/TechieDesk.Tests/Workspaces/ChatComposerTests.cs`, `ChatTurnScopeTests.cs`; runtime spec `tests/verify/req-ui-044-composer.spec.ts`.

<a id="d-req-ui-045"></a>
- **REQ-UI-045** — Named, user-defined agents (BRD-138, added 2026-07-26).
  - *Acceptance:* an agent can be created, edited, duplicated and deleted; it carries handle, description, instructions, model, skill subset, knowledge scope and guardrails; `@handle` invokes it in chat; the built-in `@agent` cannot be deleted.
  - ⚠ *Permission precedence is the hard part:* the workspace skill catalogue (BRD-84) is the outer boundary. An agent must not be able to enable a skill the catalogue forbids, and turning a catalogue skill off must immediately disable it for every agent. The editor shows forbidden skills greyed and marked *Blocked* rather than hiding them, so the reason is legible.

<a id="d-req-ui-046"></a>
- **REQ-UI-046** — Natural-language authoring of schedules and flows (BRD-140, added 2026-07-26).
  - *Acceptance:* a plain-language description produces a structured schedule or flow; every generated element is individually editable; nothing saves without an explicit confirm; a follow-up instruction refines the existing result rather than starting over.
  - ⚠ *Hard constraint:* **cron is never required and never surfaced by default.** Lists, grids, run history and notifications show plain-language schedule text. A cron field may exist only behind an *Advanced* disclosure.
  - *Interpretation runs on the configured local model* — authoring an automation must not require a cloud provider (BRD-99 data locality).
  - ⚠ *Failure mode to design for:* the model will sometimes misread the instruction. The confirm step is the safety net, so it must show what was understood in full — schedule, each step, and the delivery action — not a summary.
